# Anti-Codex Findings

## 2026-06-20 Rounded Button Hit Testing
- Chromium/Electron hit testing can exclude the extreme rounded corners of a button even though the visual control appears to occupy the full bounding rectangle. A center-only test cannot detect this.
- For controls that must accept the full rectangular touch target, keep the interactive element rectangular and draw the rounded appearance on a pointer-events-disabled child or pseudo-element. Verify a grid of interior points, not only the center.

- 2026-07-02 goal/release report refresh race: do not run `test-anticodex-goal-readiness.mjs` and `test-anticodex-release-gates.mjs` in parallel. Release gates can read `latest-goal-readiness.json` while goal-readiness is refreshing it and briefly report `damagedReports:["goalReadiness"]`; rerun the two checks serially before classifying it as a product regression.
- 2026-07-02 PowerShell report writer race: running Windows-user account prepare, account-isolation status, and second-account readiness summary in parallel can make multiple processes refresh the same latest/history JSON reports. The shared report writer used to check `Test-Path` and then call `File.Move`, so a second writer could create the target in between and produce "file exists" before account logic completed. `Write-AntiCodexTextAtomic` now catches that `IOException` and falls back to `File.Replace` when the target appeared concurrently; parallel account report refreshes then pass and still report the real blocker `target_windows_user_missing`.
- 2026-07-02 free-chat submit obstruction: the latest visible `free-chat` failure was not model incapability and not a guard false positive. Root cause was the embedded Anti-Codex Manager overlay still open over the composer; the verifier selected the right send button by DOM but the actual click point hit `.anticodex-panel`, so no model request was produced. The smoke now closes `#anticodex-manager-overlay` before prompt submission and requires `document.elementFromPoint` at the send-button center to resolve back to the selected send button before DOM click. Fresh real run marker `UI_CHAT_SMOKE_1782970352466` passed with `usedRealTraffic:true`, `failures:[]`.
- 2026-07-02 PowerShell wrapper gotcha: `powershell.exe -Command "$d='...'; ..."` inside the current shell stripped `$d`, and `rtk New-Item` failed because `New-Item` is a PowerShell cmdlet, not a PATH executable. For bounded local directory/file setup, use direct current-shell PowerShell cmdlets or escape `$` carefully; do not classify these wrapper failures as Anti-Codex product evidence.
- 2026-07-02 Claude visible desktop coding-project retry: the UI verifier can now select `Claude Sonnet 4.6 (Thinking)` in the visible model picker and confirms it again beside the actual send composer before submit, but the task still did not produce a Codex-like run. The verifier now closes the model menu with Escape, records `preSubmitModelSelectionEvidence`, rejects missing request payloads instead of marking them as real traffic, and the server rejects non-prewarm empty Responses input with `empty_responses_input` before Antigravity traffic. - 2026-07-02 direct MCP worker run lesson: launching an async worker through a short-lived standalone MCP client and then closing stdin cancels the job as `mcp_server_shutdown`; keep the same MCP client alive through wait/get, or use a synchronous call with a request timeout longer than the worker budget. A MiMoCode retry produced a useful direction but wrote a flawed hand-rolled MCP/TOML probe that treated TOML arrays as strings, so it was not merged. The accepted mainline probe uses the official MCP SDK client and only performs `tools/list`.
- 2026-07-02 MCP transport diagnosis: current Codex-channel MCP tool calls to MiMoCode, Kilo, OpenCode, Antigravity, and prior DeepSeek/MIMO starts returned `Transport closed`, but independent MCP client probes could start the same local server scripts and list tools for all seven worker servers: MIMO Claude Coworker, MiMoCode, Kilo, OpenCode, Antigravity, DeepSeek, and DevEco. `latest-worker-mcp-server-probe.json` now records the no-traffic direct SDK `tools/list` result and diagnostics reject missing workers or empty tool lists. Treat current-channel `Transport closed` as a stale/dead Codex MCP session, not as broken worker server code, missing config, or Anti-Codex task capability evidence. The practical recovery is a Codex MCP/session reload or new channel after the current checkpoint, then re-test one read-only `get_job`/tool-list path before dispatching real worker tasks.
- 2026-07-02 worker MCP probe caveat hardening: `latest-worker-mcp-server-probe.json` now explicitly says its direct SDK probe does not test the current Codex session transport (`codexSessionTransportStatus:"not-tested-by-direct-probe"`). - 2026-07-02 second-account no-traffic chain recovery: the second-account readiness/status/proof scripts could fail with Windows `Access is denied` before any account logic because several reports launched nested `powershell.exe -File` subreports. Fresh no-traffic checks now pass for Windows-user prepare, setup guidance, account-isolation status, second-account login entrypoint, readiness summary, and post-login proof dry-run; the current correct state remains `targetUserExists:false`, `safeToAskUserForSecondAccountLogin:false`, `releaseAcceptableAccountProof:false`, and `nextOperatorAction:"create_or_verify_target_windows_user_then_rerun_summary"`. No login, user creation, password capture, system change, or real traffic occurred.
- 2026-07-02 native-feel long-task recovery: the user's complaint was correct. Anti-Codex could run Codex-owned tools, but the desktop UI could sound unlike a real coworker because inserted progress text repeated child-like "I do X" wording instead of planning, judgement, verification, and follow-up. The current fix changes visible progress wording to evidence-oriented Chinese process text, expands it to report-site and repo multi-file workflows, rejects repeated visible progress in the UI smoke, and keeps insertion gated on real tool calls. Fresh real desktop Antigravity/Gemini evidence now passes the five agentic long-task modes: `coding-project`, `multi-turn-project`, `report-site`, `complex-project`, and `repo-multi-file-edit`. Latest strict multi-turn marker `UI_MULTI_TURN_PROJECT_SMOKE_1782929390199` has `usedRealTraffic:true`, `failures:[]`, 280 task tool calls, 18 distinct visible progress entries, exact first/follow-up markers, passing tests/CLI, `modelLedExecution:true`, and `codexLikeProcessProof:true`. The no-traffic `agentic-capability-matrix` and `long-task-landing-matrix` are green for the five-task agentic set; the follow-up all-profile refresh also passed `repo-file-edit` and `web-search`, so current task coverage is green.
- 2026-07-02 web-search parity recovery: the real desktop web-search failure was not model incapability. Root cause was translator routing: a plain web-search prompt fetched internal web evidence, then asked Antigravity to summarize it again, which could loop through internal pseudo-tool state and leave the session lock active. The fix returns plain web evidence directly, keeps model-led local artifact tasks on the Codex-tool path, removes `source URLs?` from the local-artifact classifier, and formats source URLs with ASCII `: ` separators. Fresh real desktop marker `UI_WEB_SEARCH_SMOKE_1782930925371` passed with `usedRealTraffic:true`, `failures:[]`; all-profile roadmap, desktop task suite, long-task landing, agentic capability, and native-Codex parity matrices are green after the refresh.
- 2026-07-01 visible coding-project UI recovery: the user's complaint was correct. Anti-Codex could already create files and run commands, but visible progress could look childish/repetitive because the translator inserted the same generic file/test progress line on repeated tool turns and the verifier counted repeated response-history snapshots as repeated UI. The current fix records visible progress per artifact marker, deduplicates assistant history by visible text, recognizes combined PowerShell artifact commands as multi-step visible work, and rejects excessive repeated progress in the UI smoke. Fresh real Gemini low desktop evidence passed with marker `UI_CODING_PROJECT_SMOKE_1782923837755`, `failures:[]`, 20 task tool calls, exact files/tests/CLI/done proof, 6 distinct visible progress entries, and `repeatedVisibleProgressCount:0`.
- 2026-07-01 stricter long-task matrix truth update, now superseded by 2026-07-02 evidence: this red state was valid when recorded and correctly rejected older artifact-only green claims. At that point `agentic-capability-matrix` was `complete:false` and `long-task-landing-matrix` had `longTaskLandingComplete:false`, `landedLongTaskCount:0`. Keep the lesson: files/tests/CLI artifacts alone are not native-Codex UI proof; valid long-task evidence must include visible distinct progress, real tool calls and outputs in response history, marker-scoped tool output, final assistant marker, model-led execution, and `codexLikeProcessProof`.
- 2026-07-01 native-Codex UI parity truth update: do not claim "Anti-Codex works like Codex" from artifact existence alone. Fresh stricter evidence now separates three states: (1) Chinese visible `agentic-step-flow` passed with real Gemini low traffic, `artifactGuardUsed:false`, `codexLikeProcessProof:true`, 20 task tool calls, visible progress, real files, verification, and final marker `UI_AGENTIC_STEP_FLOW_SMOKE_1782915124978`; (2) complex research+code+dashboard real runs are still not release-grade parity because they can make progress/tool calls but fail to finish with final marker/idle state; latest complex marker `UI_COMPLEX_PROJECT_SMOKE_1782916438111` had 28 task tool calls and 6 visible progress entries but no final marker and left active request state before manual Anti-Codex-only cleanup; (3) old hidden artifact guard/file-exists successes are no longer accepted as Codex-like process proof.
- 2026-07-01 streaming shell parameter bug: complex real desktop tasks exposed that the stream mapper path could still pass a `shell_command` call with `command` while the Codex-side schema required `cmd`, producing `Anti-Codex rejected tool request "shell_command": missing required parameter "cmd"`. Non-streaming formatting already had coverage, but streaming completion and final schema validation did not. The fix buffers shell args when normalization is required, normalizes before final stream item emission, and adds final schema validation fallback for `command` -> `cmd` only. Keep this one-way; do not accept `cmd` -> `command` for schemas that intentionally require `command`.
- 2026-07-01 complex-project current gap after shell-parameter fix: after the runtime sync, a real complex-project rerun no longer stopped only at file-exists evidence, but the outer verifier timed out and the report stayed red because final assistant marker/idle proof did not land. Anti-Codex was restored by stopping only Anti-Codex-owned patched app/runtime/translator/web-search child processes and restarting via runtime sync; native Codex was not touched. Next complex fix should focus on finishing/idle/final-marker behavior, not on claiming success from partial artifacts.
- 2026-07-01 Chinese agentic-step-flow hardening: a fresh post-sync real visible desktop run proved Anti-Codex can do the Codex-like long-task loop the user asked about without the earlier abnormal phase spam: Chinese prompt, visible `阶段1` / `阶段2` exactly once each, 10 task tool calls, marker-scoped tool output, `artifactGuardUsed:false`, exact no-BOM `done.txt`, and marker `UI_AGENTIC_STEP_FLOW_SMOKE_1782901302579`. The reruns exposed three real issues now covered by tests: model-authored `WriteAllText(..., [System.Text.Encoding]::UTF8)` produced a BOM, restored phase text could be repeated in the UI, and verifier history counting could inflate repeated context into fake 35/99-call evidence.
- 2026-07-01 complex-project long-task truth check: do not count complex-project as proven just because files exist or the marker appears. The verifier found three false-green paths: stale `model-routes.latest` could mask a current wrong model, deterministic artifact guards/web-backed artifact fallback could create the project without model-led tool calls, and complex-project command repair could append the local guard to model commands. These are now rejected/disabled for required-model complex-project evidence. Latest honest Gemini result routes through `gemini-3.5-flash-low` but still fails Codex-like long-task proof: the first turn stops at a web/search summary and does not create the project; the repair turn does not produce the requested files. Treat `uiComplexProjectSmoke` as red until the model-led loop itself emits tool calls, observes tool outputs, creates the files, passes tests/CLI, and replies with the marker without fallback.
- 2026-07-01 autonomous long-task UI visibility: the backend was not the blocker for long tasks. A completed autonomous-reasoning run (`UI_AUTONOMOUS_REASONING_SMOKE_1782891681148`) produced real Codex-owned tool calls, files, tests, CLI output, and final artifact proof with `ok:true`; the user-visible gap was that Antigravity often emitted tool calls without intermediate natural-language status, making the UI look like one question/one final answer. The translator now inserts short Chinese visible progress messages before real autonomous tool calls, and the follow-up response history proved those messages appear before actual tool calls. Do not count this as fake execution: the progress text is only emitted when a real function/custom tool call is present.
- 2026-07-01 autonomous long-task verifier gotchas: `codex-update-analyzer.mjs` may correctly export `summarizeSources` as `export async function summarizeSources`; the verifier must accept async exports and export-list forms. Also, desktop marker counting can miss the final marker even when response history contains the assistant marker and artifacts are complete, so autonomous verification should use response-history marker evidence to avoid false negatives while still requiring file/test/CLI/tool evidence.
- 2026-07-01 UI forced-model smoke update: `test-anticodex-ui-chat-smoke.mjs --model gemini-3.5-flash-low --model-label "Gemini 3.5 Flash (Low)"` now fails before submit if the visible desktop UI is still on another model, so wrong-model evidence cannot be counted. - 2026-07-01 CC Switch reference cleanup: the useful comparison remains protocol/client compatibility rather than rewriting tool execution. The temporary local clone `C:\Users\XD\AppData\Local\Temp\cc-switch-ref` was verified as `https://github.com/farion1231/cc-switch.git` and deleted; the separate `CC Switch-3.16.4-updater-*` temp cache was left untouched because it looks app-owned, not a research clone.

Treat those as unfinished tool work and replay with the exposed Codex tool schema; do not count them as real Codex-like task completion.
- 2026-07-01 latest Chinese agentic-step-flow retest: after adding bare-tool-name and prompt-echo guards, the rerun was not valid task evidence because the UI showed `Antigravity 账号额度已到`, `modelRouteEvidence.newRouteCount:0`, and no marker-scoped tool calls/outputs. Follow-up proved quota is model/model-family specific, not whole-account: `gemini-3.5-flash-low` returned `ACCOUNT_PROXY_READY_34956` successfully while another lane had shown an exhaustion toast. Default health/profile selection should prefer the low Flash model so one exhausted model lane does not poison the account traffic state.
- 2026-07-01 model-specific quota UX: health now carries `accountTrafficModel` when a request failure can be tied to a model, Manager status shows `受限模型`, and the desktop quota toast title changes from account-level exhaustion to model-level exhaustion when possible. If the model cannot be determined, it falls back to a generic model quota message.
- 2026-07-01 Antigravity CLI quota exhaustion diagnosis: a direct `antigravity.exe --print` marker can exit 0 with empty stdout while the log contains `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED (code 429): Individual quota reached`. Treat this as model/model-family-specific quota/limit evidence unless a follow-up marker fails on the selected low-cost model too; Gemini can still be usable when Claude or a high-tier Gemini lane is exhausted. Do not ask for an account switch based only on one exhausted model lane.
- 2026-07-01 Claude fallback transcript extraction: Claude CLI conversations can contain the exact readiness marker only inside protobuf/transcript payloads such as `ACCOUNT_PROXY_READY_24882`. The generic no-space token filter previously discarded uppercase underscore markers as random IDs, making real Claude responses look empty. Preserve uppercase underscore marker candidates before the random-token rejection, then rerun account/proxy readiness with `-Model claude-sonnet-4-6`.
- 2026-07-01 release-status doc guard after quota exhaustion: current-status tests must distinguish Gemini quota exhaustion from overall release readiness. After Claude fallback refresh, required release gates can be `releaseReady:true` even while the active goal remains `goalComplete:false` because second-account proof is missing.
- 2026-07-01 Claude compact-summary fallback: `claude-sonnet-4-6` can return only the `COMPACT_SEED_*` marker for `/responses/compact`, omitting `Next action: verify installed compaction resume`. If the compact summary is non-empty but missing marker/next-action facts present in the compact input, append recovered compact input facts before returning the `context_compaction` item. The real Claude compaction gate then passes direct and chained resume.
- 2026-07-01 cancellation verifier parameter note: `test-anticodex-cancellation.ps1` does not accept `-CancelAfterMs`; passing it is a caller error, not product evidence. Use `-TimeoutSec` and `ANTICODEX_TEST_MODEL` / `-Model` for model selection.
- 2026-07-01 second-account proof Windows-user binding: post-login proof is unsafe if the command only names an isolated profile and account IDs. The proof command must also carry `-WindowsUser <target-windows-user>` through status, entrypoint, readiness, approval-plan, approved-run, goal, and release-gate surfaces. The real-smoke verifier must compare current Windows identity with the target user and reject `separate-windows-user` proof unless it is actually running under that target Windows user.
- 2026-07-01 second-account command template propagation: approval and approved-run sequence reports must consume `latest-second-antigravity-account-login-entrypoint.json` command templates instead of hand-written fallback login/proof commands. Release gates should require the source report, `-WindowsUser`, `-StartLoginApproval start-separate-windows-user-login`, `-AllowSeparateAccountProof`, and `SeparateAccountProofApprovalId`; otherwise the operator plan can silently drift away from the verified entrypoint.
- 2026-07-01 second-account entrypoint goal-readiness chain: second-account login-entrypoint evidence is not enough if it only appears as its own latest report. `test-anticodex-goal-readiness.mjs` must consume it, `proven.secondAccountLoginEntrypoint` must require no-traffic safety guards plus login/status/proof command presence and a valid post-login proof plan, release gates must reject goal-readiness reports that omit those fields, and diagnostics summaries must preserve them. This keeps the active goal blocked only by missing real separate-account proof, not by missing operator proof-chain visibility.
- 2026-07-01 diagnostics reports-summary support-field whitelist: when adding a new account-boundary support field, update `summarize-anticodex-report.mjs`, `collect-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1`, `test-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1`, and Manager latest-report renderers together. A passing direct summarizer is not enough; installed diagnostic bundles can still fail if the collector fixed field list omits the evidence. Skipped optional Antigravity CLI real-smoke summaries must also preserve lane owner defaults (`anticodex-model-text`, MCP lane not managed here) so support bundles do not misread a skip as missing account-boundary metadata.
- 2026-07-01 stability soak long-context prompt hardening: a fresh short soak failed only the long-context marker because the instruction appeared before a large filler block and the model answered with generic help. Keep the long-context verifier instruction repeated after the filler and set that request to `temperature = 0`; the rerun then passed Chinese turns, long-context markers, tool loop, streaming, cancellation, health checks, and stable translator PID.
- 2026-07-01 diagnostics reports-summary second-account evidence: source `summarize-anticodex-report.mjs` can expose `secondAccountLoginEntrypointEvidence`, but installed diagnostic bundles also require `collect-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1` to copy that field into `reports-summary.json`. If the collector whitelist omits it, `test-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1 -VerifyReportsSummary` fails even though direct summarizer output looks correct. Always verify the installed collector bundle after adding new report evidence fields.
- 2026-07-01 second-account same-Windows-user negative check: the user-facing second-account login entrypoint verifier now proves that targeting the current Windows user is blocked with `target_windows_user_is_current_user`, `startedLogin:false`, no traffic, no system change, and no password capture/storage. This negative check must use a temporary report root; running it against the main report root poisons release-required latest account-readiness reports with the intentional current-user failure.
- 2026-07-01 Kilo same-user entrypoint dispatch: first Kilo dispatch failed because `forbidden_paths` escaped the worker cwd. The narrowed retry produced a useful same-user block draft but wrote the negative current-user run into the main report root, which would poison release reports, and the wrapper ended with `caller_timeout_no_valid_changes`. Do not merge that worker diff; keep the mainline temp-report-root implementation.
- 2026-07-01 second-account login entrypoint audit hardening: `start-anticodex-second-antigravity-account-login.ps1` must expose direct JSON safety fields, not only rely on the wrapper test report. Required direct fields include `requestedRealTraffic:false`, `behaviorCovered:true`, `coverageKind:"second-account-login-entrypoint-direct"`, `changedSystem:false`, `createdUser:false`, `capturesPassword:false`, `storesPassword:false`, and an account-isolation caveat. The no-traffic entrypoint verifier now checks those fields across status-only, missing-approval, and approved-but-blocked paths.
- 2026-07-01 required real-traffic release gates passed after approval: bounded `accountProxyReadiness` succeeded with a real marker request, and full `postVerification` completed with `usedRealTraffic:true`. Reports that model the required real-traffic sequence must now support the completed-gate state: `requiredReleaseComplete:true`, completed gates `accountProxyReadiness` and `postVerification`, `requiredActionCount:0`, and no explicit-approval blocker for those already completed gates. Keep `goalComplete:false` until separate second Antigravity account proof is real and release-acceptable.
- 2026-07-01 no-traffic report self-cycle correction: after required release gates complete, `realApprovalPlan`, `goalReadiness`, `requiredRealTrafficSequence`, `goalRealRunReadiness`, and `approvedRealRunSequence` may legitimately report release-ready/completed required gates instead of pending required actions. Self-tests should reject weakened evidence but must not require old `releaseBlockedOnlyByRequiredRealTraffic:true` semantics once `releaseReady:true` is real.
- 2026-07-01 real multi-turn desktop smoke now passes again. The failure was not model incapability; it was bridge/tool-name compatibility plus verifier brittleness. The model requested `shell_command`, while the Codex-side tool route exposed `exec_command`, so strict routing rejected a valid shell operation as unavailable. Add shell-command aliases in the Chat tool catalog and normalize `{command}`/`{cmd}` before executing the Codex-owned tool. Keep this as a client/tool adapter compatibility issue, not an Antigravity coworker/tool-executor issue.
- 2026-07-01 desktop UI multi-turn verifier gotchas: stale button labels can include old `Thinking`/`正在思考` text after the backend is idle, so busy detection must key on the exact stop button rather than broad label substring matching. CDP mouse clicks can also fail to submit even when the send button is visible; submit through a DOM click first, then fall back to mouse/Enter evidence fields.
- 2026-07-01 Anti-Codex conversation cleanup: the operator-facing validation chats in the screenshot were generated smoke-task conversations under the isolated `deepcodex` project, not user work. Cleanup succeeded after backing up to `C:\Users\XD\AppData\Roaming\anticodex\conversation-backups\cleanup-20260701-011658`, preserving project-root state, and verifying the visible `deepcodex` no-conversation UI. Real desktop validation will recreate new smoke chats unless cleanup is run again after testing.
- 2026-07-01 nested PowerShell launch failure: Windows can return Access denied when scripts try to start a nested `powershell.exe` through `Start-Process`, including installed runtime restart/sync paths. Treat this as host process-launch friction, not translator failure. Work around by running the installed `start-deepcodex.ps1 -TranslatorOnly` script in the current PowerShell process and then validate `/health` plus UI state.
- 2026-07-01 MIMO shell-alias dispatch failure: MIMO job `dsw_mr0xk05s_sytgnc` was assigned the shell-command alias fix in an isolated worktree but timed out with `caller_timeout_no_valid_changes`, zero diff, and no checks. Treat it as no-evidence worker latency; rely on the mainline patch plus local tests and real desktop evidence.

It correctly blocks assistant claims like "I ran/read/wrote/searched" without actual tool evidence, but it also blocked ordinary evaluation advice that mentioned files/commands. The guard now considers whether the latest user request actually requires tool completion before replacing the answer with an Anti-Codex blocked-tool message.

Their effective release-blocked-only calculation should ignore only these self-cycle gates and still fail on unrelated missing gates such as `installedRuntimeSync`.

For isolated worktree tasks, keep `forbidden_paths` repo-relative/in-cwd only and state external report roots as text constraints instead of tool policy paths.

- 2026-06-30 installed-overall UI absence failure: full installed overall requires the Anti-Codex main window/CDP, not just translator health. - 2026-06-30 Kilo native-parity dispatch notes: Kilo job `klw_mr0pky3z_2gkym0` produced an advisory `findings.md` direction in an isolated worktree, but its diff reordered too much surrounding findings text and was not merged. - 2026-06-30 real-task approval visibility, superseded by 2026-07-02 evidence: when a future desktop refresh is needed, reports and Manager summaries should show concrete pending task scenarios, not just task IDs. Current roadmap has no pending real-task scenarios after the all-profile refresh.
- 2026-06-30 object-array helper gotcha: do not reuse string-normalizing helpers such as `asArray()` for arrays of evidence objects. It stringifies objects to `[object Object]`, which can silently empty later object filters. Use a separate object-array helper for fields such as `pendingRealTaskScenarios`, and verify the generated JSON, not only static source strings.
- 2026-06-30 Kilo doc-only worker coordination failure: a Kilo worker was scoped to `docs/audits/2026-06-30-real-task-result-evidence-review.md`, but the main agent edited scripts/tests while it was running, so the worker finished with `changed_forbidden_paths` even though its useful doc diff was in the allowed docs path. Avoid overlapping worker policy snapshots with main-agent edits in forbidden paths; either wait for the doc-only worker to finish or run it before main edits. A duplicate Kilo job was accidentally started and then cancelled with zero diff.
- 2026-06-30 rtk PowerShell wrapper gotcha: wrapping PowerShell built-ins or parser snippets through `rtk` can fail with `program not found`, `拒绝访问`, or broken `$null` quoting. For bounded PowerShell built-ins/parser checks, use direct `powershell.exe` or native PowerShell in the current shell and keep outputs compact.
- 2026-06-30 diagnostics bundle collection gotcha: `collect-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1` does not accept `-TimeoutSec` and is guarded to run from `C:\Users\XD\AppData\Local\Anti-Codex`, not from the source repo. Use the installed runtime script from the installed Anti-Codex root when generating `reports-summary.json`.
- 2026-07-01 account/proxy readiness vs CLI model-text path coupling: the release gate must not let a stale Antigravity Desktop account probe hide a working CLI model-text path, but it also must not pass when the real marker request fails. Allow `accountProbeStaleButModelsAvailable` only as a non-blocking signal when CLI models exist and the real marker succeeds; if refresh/request returns connection-closed or kept-alive failures, diagnose proxy/network unavailable instead of `ready`.
- 2026-07-01 account/proxy readiness transport-shape gap: visible desktop `free-chat` can pass through the `/responses` WebSocket relay with streaming retry (`fetchAttempts:2`) while the old readiness verifier's single non-stream `/v1/responses` marker request fails. The release gate should accept only real marker success, but it must probe the desktop-equivalent streaming path before declaring the account/model path unavailable.
- 2026-07-01 PowerShell parameter/list collision: parameter names are case-insensitive, so a local list named `$requestAttempts` collides with `[int]$RequestAttempts` and fails with `Cannot convert Generic.List[Object] to Int32`. Use a distinct local name such as `$requestAttemptRecords` for retry evidence lists.
- 2026-07-01 account/proxy readiness retry budget: adding multiple real marker attempts without a total budget can exceed the caller timeout because each non-stream and streaming request may consume its full timeout. Keep a `TotalRequestTimeoutSec` cap and record `budgetExceeded` in the report.
- 2026-07-01 account/proxy readiness streaming hang: `Invoke-WebRequest` can wait on a streaming `/responses` body longer than the intended verifier budget. Use bounded `HttpWebRequest` line reads with a marker stop pattern and abort/dispose the request in `finally`.
- 2026-06-30 real-task result proof gate, superseded by 2026-07-02 evidence: `latest-real-task-coverage-roadmap.json` should preserve `taskResultRequirements`, per-task `taskResultProofs`, and `missingResultProofTaskCount` separately from artifact proofs. This proves marker/chat/search/artifact-backed task result evidence is not dropped. Current evidence has no stale `free-chat`/`web-search` reports on the explicit real-traffic refresh path.
- 2026-06-30 required real-traffic sequence verifier: `test-anticodex-required-real-traffic-sequence.ps1` is a no-real-traffic report over `latest-real-approval-plan.json`. It should keep `requestedRealTraffic:false`/`usedRealTraffic:false`, require non-empty approval-blocked required actions, and verify optional real-traffic actions are not mixed into the required release sequence. It is indexed by runtime sync, diagnostic bundles, installed overall report inventory, report signing, and Manager latest reports.
- 2026-06-30 goal-level no-traffic release gates: `latest-real-task-coverage-roadmap.json`, `latest-real-approval-plan.json`, and `latest-goal-readiness.json` must be required passive no-traffic release gates, not only diagnostics summaries. Each needs a semantic validator, runtime-sync fingerprint entry, and no-traffic refresh action; otherwise the release status can understate missing goal evidence.
- 2026-06-30 installed-overall transient probe failure: a full installed overall no-traffic run can time out after long UI/idle checks and then report diagnostics/websocket fallback failures even when both probes pass immediately as standalone checks. Treat that combined timeout as non-release-gate evidence, rerun `test-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1` and `test-anticodex-websocket-fallback.ps1` narrowly, and do not classify it as a goal-level release gate regression if compact release gates stay clean.
- 2026-06-30 required real-traffic sequence support gate: the sequence report must also be a passive no-traffic release gate and diagnostics evidence field. Release gates should reject missing approval-plan evidence, required action count below one, mixed optional actions, missing `accountProxyReadiness`/`postVerification`, missing commands, or nonzero failures. Diagnostics should reject support bundles that omit `requiredRealTrafficSequenceEvidence`.
- 2026-06-30 inline PowerShell variable extraction gotcha: when using `powershell -Command` through the current shell, unescaped `$summary`, `$item`, `$p`, or pipeline variables can be stripped before PowerShell sees them, causing empty pipe/parser errors. Escape `$` with backticks or avoid variables; do not classify those helper extraction failures as Anti-Codex product evidence.
- 2026-06-30 sync-readiness parameter gotcha: `test-anticodex-sync-readiness.ps1` does not accept `-TimeoutSec`; passing it fails at parameter binding before product checks run. Rerun without `-TimeoutSec`, or use the script's existing `-FastMaxElapsedMs` only when doing fast-mode budget work.
- 2026-06-30 OpenCode worker partial timeout gotcha: OpenCode job `ocw_mr0hgm6m_deyrko` returned `partial_caller_timeout` / `caller_timeout_after_valid_changes` in an isolated worktree after changing only the static launcher test and a draft verifier script. Do not merge it blindly; inspect the diff and prefer the verified mainline implementation when it covers diagnostics/runtime/Manager/report-integrity surfaces.
- 2026-06-30 goal-readiness report semantics: `test-anticodex-goal-readiness.mjs` is a no-real-traffic aggregate and may report `ok:true` when the evidence bundle was generated correctly, but must keep `goalComplete:false` until separate Antigravity account proof and required real-traffic release gates pass. Diagnostics should reject summaries that omit `goalReadinessEvidence` or claim the goal/release is already complete.
- 2026-06-30 agentic capability matrix evidence: `test-anticodex-agentic-capability-matrix.mjs` is a no-real-traffic report over existing signed real desktop task reports. Release gates, diagnostics, goal-readiness, Manager latest reports, installed-overall inventory, and runtime sync fingerprints should all include `latest-agentic-capability-matrix.json`.
- 2026-06-30 agentic matrix verification gotchas: a mistyped focused `node --test --test-name-pattern` that matches no intended test is non-evidence, and broad parallel verification output can overflow and hide the useful result. Rerun the exact named test narrowly. When adding a required release gate, also add its ordered no-traffic refresh action and its runtime-sync fingerprint entry; otherwise `--self-test` or installed-runtime sync can fail even if the semantic gate code exists.

Release gates and diagnostics should reject missing/incomplete artifact proof coverage so task evidence is grounded in files, tests, CLI output, links, and exact markers rather than final assistant text.

Diagnostics and release gates should reject goal summaries that drop these fields.

- 2026-06-30 Kilo worker status gotcha: the current Kilo MCP namespace exposed `kilo_start_implementation` but not a status/tail tool in this channel. - 2026-06-30 command wrapper non-evidence in goal-readiness work: `%APPDATA%` is not expanded by PowerShell, `rtk Get-Content` cannot run PowerShell cmdlets directly, and nested `powershell -Command` snippets can strip `$path`/`foreach` variables. Treat those wrapper failures and output-overflow focused-test runs as non-evidence; rerun with absolute paths, no nested variables, and narrow commands.
- 2026-06-30 source-preflight profile-prepare JSON capture: `prepare-anticodex-antigravity-cli-profile.ps1` emits a long one-line JSON report. `test-anticodex-source-preflight.ps1` must read `latest-antigravity-cli-profile-prepare.json` from the temp report root instead of parsing `stdoutTail`, because a long caveat can be truncated and leave contract fields empty.

Diagnostics and release gates should reject goal-level worker evidence if any fixed worker has no real listed tools, even when the worker boolean or server-name list is present.
- 2026-06-30 Antigravity same-user login isolation failed in practice: launching Antigravity CLI from an isolated `ProfileRoot`/`APPDATA`/`LOCALAPPDATA` under the same Windows user still showed the already logged-in Antigravity account. Treat same-user file/AppData isolation as insufficient for account separation. Do not ask the user to log into a second Antigravity account there; use a separate Windows user or an official independent credential store, then require approved real traffic plus account identity comparison before any `separateAccountVerified:true`.
- 2026-06-30 Antigravity separate-account proof boundary: release gates must reject `separateAccountVerified:true` when `accountIsolationBoundary` is only `profile-and-appdata-only`. A valid proof needs `separate-windows-user` or `official-independent-credential-store` plus approved real traffic and distinct account identity comparison.
- 2026-06-30 Windows-user login DryRun source-preflight coverage: `start-anticodex-antigravity-cli-login-windows-user.ps1 -WindowsUser '.\AntiCodexModel' -DryRun` must remain no-traffic, not start `runas`, not capture login output, and report `credentialBoundary:"separate-windows-user"` plus `expectedCredentialStoreIsolation:true`. Source-preflight, release gates, summaries, diagnostics, and Manager text should all preserve this as preparation evidence, not login proof.
- 2026-06-30 Windows-user login readiness semantics: dry-run should report whether the target Windows user exists, whether it equals the current Windows user, whether it is ready for interactive login, and the blocking reason. A missing target user is an operator setup gap, not a short-path failure; targeting the current Windows user is a semantic failure because it would collapse credential isolation.
- 2026-06-30 Windows-user readiness report: `test-anticodex-antigravity-cli-windows-user-readiness.ps1` should stay report-only and call the Windows-user login launcher only with `-DryRun`. It may check whether `New-LocalUser` exists, but must not invoke it; missing target user plus non-elevated process maps to `open_elevated_windows_user_settings`. - 2026-06-29 conversation cleanup safety: Anti-Codex thread cleanup must match stable thread IDs, not Chinese titles passed through a PowerShell-to-Node stdin pipeline, because console encoding can make every title comparison miss. Stop the Anti-Codex desktop first, back up those files plus `logs_2.sqlite*` and `response-history.json`, clear only the isolated Anti-Codex state, then relaunch from the installed runtime script (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Anti-Codex\runtime\scripts\start-anticodex.ps1`). Running the source-tree launcher can infer the wrong install root. Verify with `test-anticodex-ui-state.ps1`: current clean success showed `threads=0`, `sessions_jsonl=0`, `local_thread_catalog=0`, no active reconnect, and visible `deepcodex暂无对话`.
- 2026-06-29 Antigravity entitlement route correction: Gemini OpenAI-compatible Chat API is useful only with a separate Gemini/Google API key; it does not consume the user's Antigravity subscription entitlement. With only Antigravity entitlement, prefer the local Antigravity CLI text transport as the immediate stability path. Real local proof: `runAntigravityTextTurn("只回复这六个字：CLI通路正常")` returned `CLI通路正常` in about 5.7s using `gemini-3.5-flash-low`. Direct `antigravity.exe --print` may exit 0 with empty stdout on Windows, but the model call still completes and the response is recoverable from the CLI conversation database; keep the SQLite fallback and make desktop streaming respect `ANTICODEX_ANTIGRAVITY_TRANSPORT=cli`.
- 2026-06-29 model-only launch mode: installed Anti-Codex Antigravity mode should default to `ANTICODEX_ANTIGRAVITY_TRANSPORT=cli` and `ANTICODEX_NATIVE_TOOL_BRIDGE=0`. The boundary is not "never touch the desktop process"; the boundary is "do not use Antigravity coworker/tool execution as the product path." Antigravity may supply model text/entitlement, while Anti-Codex owns tools, MCP, files, task state, and verification. `/health.upstreamProfile.transport` must be checked by the launcher fast path so an already-running native/Cascade gateway is not mistaken for the requested CLI-stable gateway.
- 2026-06-29 CLI long-prompt boundary: do not auto-fallback from `cli` to `desktop-cascade` only because the prompt crosses a local argv-size threshold. That silently reintroduces Antigravity Desktop/Cascade as the product path and can revive the coworker/tool-execution dependency the model-only direction is trying to remove. Long-prompt failures should be fixed inside the CLI transport or fail with explicit model-only diagnostics; `desktop-cascade` remains an explicit transport choice, not an implicit fallback.
- 2026-06-29 native bridge default correction: `isAntigravityNativeToolBridgeEnabled()` must default false. Native same-Cascade/MCP remains opt-in diagnostic or compatibility coverage only, enabled explicitly with `ANTICODEX_NATIVE_TOOL_BRIDGE=1`; launchers must not auto-enable it merely because the selected transport is non-CLI.
- 2026-06-29 account-direction correction: Gemini CLI is not usable for the MCP-side account split, so the previous single-Antigravity-account fallback is superseded. Anti-Codex should keep an isolated Antigravity CLI profile/account for model text while MCP can use a different Antigravity account. Keep existing CLI profile/isolation scripts as the Anti-Codex no-traffic preparation path; do not claim true separate-account proof without explicit approved real account/traffic evidence.
- 2026-06-30 separate-account proof approval guard: optional Antigravity CLI real-smoke must not set `separateAccountVerified:true` from traffic plus two different account IDs alone. It also requires `-SeparateAccountProofApprovalId`, and summaries must expose only approval/source presence booleans, never raw account IDs or raw approval IDs.
- 2026-06-30 dual Antigravity CLI lane preparation: no-traffic verification can prove only that Anti-Codex and MCP Antigravity CLI lanes have separate profile roots, APPDATA/LOCALAPPDATA, and GEMINI dirs, are not nested, and avoid known default Antigravity profile roots. It must keep `loginVerified:false` and `separateAccountVerified:false`; real account distinction still requires explicit user-approved real traffic and account identity comparison.
- 2026-06-30 DevEco dual-lane verifier timeout: DevEco job `dcw_mqzknfd3_p58ldn` was assigned the isolated no-traffic dual Antigravity CLI lane verifier task and ended with `caller_timeout_no_valid_changes`, zero diff, and no checks. Treat it as no-evidence worker latency; rely on mainline diff plus local tests.
- 2026-06-30 Kilo account-proof approval timeout: Kilo job `klw_mqzkdtbf_y11jw1` was assigned the isolated separate-account approval guard task in a separate worktree and ended with `caller_timeout_no_valid_changes`, zero diff, and no checks. Treat it as no-evidence worker noise; rely on mainline diff plus local tests.
- 2026-06-30 PowerShell parser-check quoting lesson: do not wrap parser checks in nested `powershell.exe -Command` with JSON/PowerShell mixed quotes; quotes can be stripped before the inner shell sees them. Run the parser check directly in the current PowerShell shell or use a checked script file.
- 2026-06-29 MiMoCode dual-account action timeout: `mcw_mqzbm972_wgzat8` ran the narrow optional Antigravity CLI real-smoke action hardening task in an isolated worktree for about 5 minutes and ended with `caller_timeout_no_valid_changes`, zero diff, and no checks. Treat this as slow-worker coordination noise; use the MIMO/mainline verified patch and do not retry MiMoCode unchanged for the same tiny release-action wording task.
- 2026-06-29 MiMoCode real-smoke summary timeout: `mcw_mqzbxhk5_b51o5d` ran the narrow Antigravity CLI real-smoke summary support-field task in an isolated worktree for about 5 minutes and ended with `caller_timeout_no_valid_changes`, zero diff, and no checks. Treat repeated MiMoCode zero-diff timeouts on these tiny account-proof support patches as worker latency/no-output, not product evidence; rely on MIMO/mainline diffs after direct review and tests.
- 2026-06-29 installed CLI-stable desktop proof: after syncing the four runtime files (`adaptive-server.mjs`, `antigravity-upstream.mjs`, `start-anticodex.ps1`, `start-deepcodex.ps1`) and restarting, `/health.upstreamProfile.transport` reported `cli`. Real visible desktop exact chat passed in about 21.8s with no active reconnect and clear queue/session lock. Real visible desktop repo-file-edit passed in about 33s: it modified `scripts/fixtures/desktop-coworker-live/acceptance-target.mjs`, ran the existing `node:test` file, passed 2/2, and emitted the required marker. This proves the Antigravity entitlement-only path can now do basic useful work without a Gemini API key.
- 2026-06-29 repo-multi step-1 prompt ambiguity fix: the README stage (Step 1) prompt originally said "Do not edit" source/data/test but did not forbid READING their contents. The AI coworker read those files, saw broken stubs and interesting data, and tried to "fix" everything at once — modifying data, editing source, and skipping the required README.md. The fix:
  1. **Prompt hardening**: Step 1 now explicitly says "You must NOT read, view, examine, or open any existing file inside this directory" and "You must NOT read, modify, replace, chmod, or delete" the fixture files. The "Do not edit" language was too weak because it allowed the AI to read first, then "helpfully" modify.
  2. **Read-only source barrier**: `status-dashboard.mjs` is now made `makeReadOnly()` during `prepareRepoMultiFileEditFixture()` (matching `status-dashboard.test.mjs` and `status-data.json`). Even if the AI ignores the prompt, the OS read-only flag prevents accidental writes during Step 1.
  3. **Writable before repair**: `makeWritable(repoMultiFileSource)` is called immediately before the repair/implementation turn (turn2), so the barrier does not block the legitimate code-writing step.
  4. **sourceUnchanged evidence**: `verifyRepoMultiFileEditFixture()` now captures `evidence.sourceUnchanged` so operators can tell from the report whether the coworker touched the source during the README-only phase without needing to compare manual hashes.
  5. **Repair prompt coverage**: `buildRepoMultiFileRepairPrompt(c)` still includes "Do not edit/restore data and test" language, and the `onlyReadmeMissing` branch explicitly forbids touching source/data/test/html. This guards the repair path when the AI gets most checks passing but omits the final README.
  - Together these changes close the "prompt ambiguity → unintended data/source modification" failure path that the real 2026-06-29 desktop repo-multi run exhibited (WebSocket/queue/lock OK, but data was modified and README was not properly created).
- 2026-06-29 repo-multi fixture hygiene and evidence enhancement:
  1. Reconstructed the fixture directory thoroughly before the test starts by recursively deleting `scripts/fixtures/desktop-coworker-project/` and then rebuilding it, avoiding pollution or untracked file accumulation from prior runs. Made known read-only files writable first to prevent Windows permission (EPERM) errors during deletion.
  2. Enhanced post-failure evidence collection in `verifyRepoMultiFileEditFixture()` to include actual contents of `status-dashboard.mjs`, `status-dashboard.test.mjs`, `status-data.json`, `README.md`, and `status-dashboard.html` when verification fails or mismatches are detected. This enables operators to immediately locate what the coworker modified or wrote.
  3. Simplified the README stage (Step 1) prompt and repair instructions by removing brittle command-execution and file-inspection constraints (e.g. banning `git status` or forcing an exact `node -e` shell command string), focusing instead on the key functional deliverables (creating `README.md` containing the Chinese title and the correct CLI command via any standard tool/command).
- 2026-06-28 desktop reconnect root cause update: after a Manager/local session reset, the cancelled-response guard used an overbroad recent-reset cooldown and rejected any fresh desktop Responses WebSocket request that still carried `previous_response_id`. That injected a local `anticodex_session_reset` failure into the live WebSocket stream, so the UI could show thinking/reconnecting even though translator health, account readiness, and upstream routing were green. Keep the exact cancelled response-id/fingerprint replay guard, but do not reject unrelated fresh `previous_response_id` requests just because a reset happened recently. Latest installed runtime sync plus real desktop UI smokes passed: plain exact-marker reply in about 28s, local HTML report project in about 36s, and Node CLI project with `node:test` plus CLI JSON verification in about 35s, all with no active reconnect and clear session lock.
- 2026-06-28 reset prewarm ordering fix: the local `generate:false` Responses prewarm branch must run after `resetCancelledPreviousResponseFailure(parsed)`, not before it. Otherwise a replay carrying a cancelled `previous_response_id` can be accepted as a local prewarm and bypass the non-retryable `anticodex_session_reset` guard. Keep prewarm before chat conversion/upstream work, but keep reset-cancel replay rejection before prewarm.
- 2026-06-29 UI smoke wait correction: after a visible assistant marker appears, the desktop UI smoke should observe briefly for active reconnect text, then enter diagnostics idle waiting. Do not keep the UI polling loop alive until the full task timeout just because diagnostics were not idle on the first few post-answer samples; that can turn a successful 20-40s desktop reply into a 90s+ outer-command timeout and produce a false "still thinking" diagnosis. Latest exact-marker and coding-project desktop smokes pass with clear session lock/queue after this correction.
- 2026-06-29 desktop capability split: exact-marker chat, coding-project, and complex-project smokes pass through the real visible desktop with no active reconnect and clear session lock/queue. The latest complex-project created seven files, produced six source URLs, passed `node:test`, and CLI-reported `sourceCount:6`. Do not generalize the failing repo-multi fixture into "all complex tasks fail"; the remaining weak spot is existing-repo multi-file repair under strict immutability constraints.
- 2026-06-28 repo-multi desktop reality check: after fixing the README command quoting, the real visible `repo-multi-file-edit` run still failed without active reconnect or stuck queue. The model wrote/broke `status-dashboard.mjs`, did not create README.md or status-dashboard.html, and the repair turn visibly replied `引导` instead of completing the missing artifacts. Treat this as a coworker execution-quality gap, not a transport P0: simpler chat, web-search/report-site, local project, coding project, and single repo-file edit pass, but the larger multi-file project task is not reliable yet.
- 2026-06-28 repo-multi desktop repair failure: a fresh real desktop repo-multi-file-edit smoke took 647s and still failed after code/test/CLI/html passed because README.md was never created; the model ignored the "first filesystem-changing action must create README.md" and later repair instructions by repeatedly listing/reading/running tests. Treat this as a P1 daily-usability gap, not a reconnect failure: diagnostics ended idle with no active lock, but the coworker did too much irrelevant work and missed a simple final artifact. The only-README repair prompt should prescribe the exact next Node write command and forbid any other command before it.
- 2026-06-28 desktop verifier fixture hygiene: real desktop repo-file-edit smokes intentionally let Anti-Codex modify `scripts/fixtures/desktop-coworker-live/acceptance-target.mjs` so the product can prove it edits files and runs tests. After using that smoke as acceptance evidence, immediately inspect `git status` and restore/clean only the verifier fixture artifact before staging product changes; otherwise a passing acceptance task can leak generated fixture edits into an unrelated checkpoint.
- 2026-06-28 node_repl report helper hygiene: the Node REPL MCP keeps top-level bindings across calls, so ad hoc report readers that use repeated `const fs = ...` declarations can fail with `Identifier 'fs' has already been declared`. Use block scope with fresh names or `var` for reusable helpers when collecting Anti-Codex report evidence through `node_repl`.
- 2026-06-28 multi-turn desktop tool-output gap: after the ambient-suggestion end-turn fix, a real visible multi-turn project run created `package.json`, `word-tally.mjs`, `word-tally.test.mjs`, and `done.txt`, and `node:test` plus CLI output passed, but the first turn still did not settle a `function_call_output` marker into `response-history.json`. Do not weaken the multi-turn gate into file-only success; same-chat follow-up requires durable tool-output history or an equivalent verified history item before turn 2 is allowed.
- 2026-06-28 multi-turn desktop continuation fix: Codex Desktop can send a tool-output continuation without a usable `previous_response_id`, while still carrying the original tool `call_id`. The Antigravity session lock must remember pending tool call IDs when it emits a tool call, then allow a matching `function_call_output` / `custom_tool_call_output` by `call_id`; otherwise the output request is queued behind its own active turn and eventually cancelled by idle cleanup. Latest real visible multi-turn project passed with `historyToolOutputSeen:true`, `continued:3`, follow-up files created, tests/CLI green, no active reconnect, and session lock clear.
- 2026-06-28 UI smoke text-scope correction: whole-page `document.body.innerText` is not reliable desktop-answer evidence because the left conversation list repeats the latest prompt after the assistant message. A valid visible assistant reply can be hidden from the verifier's `answerSample` when `lastIndexOf(prompt)` lands on the sidebar title instead of the user bubble. Desktop UI smokes must read visible main-pane text, excluding the sidebar and environment panel, before deciding whether the assistant reply appeared after the submitted prompt.
- 2026-06-28 reset replay correction: a Manager session-lock reset can look successful and then become active again a few seconds later because the desktop frontend replays an old `previous_response_id` continuation over the Responses WebSocket. Reset must remember the cancelled active response IDs for a short TTL and reject stale continuations locally with a non-retryable `anticodex_session_reset` failure before they enter the Antigravity queue; otherwise the user sees the same reconnect/thinking state return immediately after reset.
- 2026-06-28 desktop UI visibility correction: a backend `task_complete` with an assistant message is not enough. A smoke that starts from the global new-chat/list entry can produce a valid rollout and logs while the visible desktop remains on the project/list surface and never shows the assistant bubble. Real desktop usability gates must open the intended project chat, submit there, and require the assistant reply to appear after the user prompt in the visible UI; otherwise treat the run as a P0 usability failure, not a pass.
- 2026-06-28 websocket relay header correction: a real desktop retry storm can persist even after the WebSocket stays open if the relay drops the original desktop request headers. The internal `/responses` call needs `session_id` and `x-codex-window-id` so compaction/window turn logic can inject the same `end_turn` behavior as the HTTP path. Forward a narrow set of safe Codex/session headers through the WebSocket-to-HTTP relay; otherwise the UI may keep retrying or show thinking while the gateway has lost the desktop turn coordinates.
- 2026-06-28 durable-facts refresh correction: injecting durable tool facts only once is unsafe for desktop project tasks. A stale earlier compaction summary can block later, more specific facts such as "tests passed but README.md is still missing", so the model never sees the current pending deliverable. Before each relay, remove stale Anti-Codex durable-facts compactions and prepend freshly computed verified facts so the next model turn is guided by current tool evidence, not first-turn history.
- 2026-06-28 websocket streaming correction: keeping the outer Responses WebSocket open was necessary but not sufficient. The relay still awaited `response.text()`, buffering the entire internal HTTP SSE response. A slow Antigravity sampling request therefore produced no WebSocket frames for roughly 100 seconds, causing Codex Desktop to declare `stream disconnected` and retry even though the translator process and localhost connection were healthy. Parse the internal SSE body incrementally and forward each `data:` event as a WebSocket text frame as it arrives; also send bounded WebSocket ping frames during quiet model periods.
- 2026-06-28 desktop tool-history correction: after the persistent WebSocket fix, the next real desktop run had zero `stream disconnected` records and reused one WebSocket across many sampling requests, but still omitted README.md after its node tests passed. Durable tool facts only parsed Codex's older `exec_command` shape and only recognized `Process exited with code`; current desktop history uses `shell_command` with a `command` field and `Exit code: 0`. Parse both command shapes and both exit formats. For scoped tasks that explicitly require README.md, when node tests have passed but no successful README write is present in restored tool evidence, inject a durable pending-deliverable fact before the next model sampling request.
- 2026-06-28 desktop reconnect root cause: the first real repo multi-file run after prompt tightening still timed out, and the Codex desktop log showed `stream disconnected - retrying sampling request (1/5...)` after each tool result. The HTTP-relay WebSocket implementation explicitly called `socket.end()` after every relayed `/responses` request, so the desktop was correctly reporting reconnects even though localhost networking was healthy. A Responses WebSocket must remain open across multiple sampling requests/tool turns; queue incoming text/binary request frames and close only on client close, protocol failure, or idle timeout.
- 2026-06-28 desktop completion-loop correction: the repo multi-file desktop smoke can pass its code tests and CLI, then falsely claim completion while omitting README.md and spending the repair turn rereading tests/source, running git status, and inspecting already-passing HTML instead of creating the single missing artifact. When verifier evidence shows code and CLI already pass and every remaining failure is README-related, the repair prompt must prohibit further project inspection and source/test/data/html edits, require one UTF-8 README write, then require the exact final marker and stop. The desktop wait loop must also break once backend diagnostics are idle after a bounded grace period, even without the marker, so a completed-but-noncompliant assistant turn fails quickly instead of consuming the full task timeout.
- 2026-06-28 log-query failure: embedding Chinese alternatives directly in a PowerShell-piped JavaScript regular expression can arrive as `??` and make the regex invalid (`Nothing to repeat`). For compact Windows log filters, keep the regex/search needles ASCII and inspect UTF-8 payload slices separately.
- 2026-06-28 desktop task evidence correction: a desktop thread that reads files, runs tests, or emits tool calls is still not task-complete. The repo multi-file smoke proved Anti-Codex could enter the model/tool loop, but failed after malformed `apply_patch` retries, an unrelated Antigravity brain search, missing README, mojibake Chinese, test-file edits to match mojibake, and an English completion claim without the final marker. Acceptance must require final artifacts, unchanged fixture inputs/tests, passing tests/CLI, exact readable Chinese phrases, and the final marker. For explicit fixture/temp/example directories, feed failed `apply_patch` results back as recovery facts, allow PowerShell file writes after one failed patch attempt, forbid unrelated brain/transcript/log searches, avoid copying non-ASCII text from garbled PowerShell display output, use UTF-8 APIs or JavaScript Unicode escapes for Chinese fixture literals, and make acceptance tests/data read-only so model behavior cannot silently rewrite the oracle.
- 2026-06-28 desktop project-task correction: a fake `101 Switching Protocols` / idle Responses WebSocket is not a safe fallback. Real desktop project threads in the Codex app-server can choose `responses_websocket` even when `responses_websockets=false`, and an idle accepted socket makes the UI show thinking/retry while Anti-Codex's Antigravity queue stays at `accepted:0`. The safe direction is either explicit unsupported fallback or a real WebSocket relay into the working HTTP `/responses` path; do not treat an idle WebSocket handshake as task evidence.
- 2026-06-28 reconnect correction: explicit `501 unsupported_websocket` is not acceptable for the installed desktop when it keeps choosing the Responses WebSocket path; it surfaces as a visible reconnect loop even while HTTP diagnostics are ready. Use the real local WebSocket-to-HTTP relay (`101` plus `http_relay_websocket`) and keep the relay streaming SSE frames incrementally.
- 2026-06-28 multi-turn desktop correction: a marker printed inside a visible shell-command activity is not an assistant completion and must not release the next user turn. Before submitting a same-thread follow-up, require the first turn's `function_call_output` marker to appear in response history, the desktop stop/thinking state to clear, and translator diagnostics to be idle; otherwise the follow-up can interrupt tool-result continuation and leave the project half-updated.
- 2026-06-22 source and installed update: local connector/app-tool allowlist now has a script-level HTTP verifier. It creates an isolated temporary Anti-Codex state/Codex home, seeds connector and app-tool caches, verifies `/backend-api/aip/connectors` and both `GET`/`POST /backend-api/wham/apps`, and asserts link/noauth/oauth endpoints stay metadata-only unsupported. Installed overall no-traffic now runs this gate and passes. This proves local cached tool exposure, not remote OAuth or real connector authorization.
- 2026-06-23 release gate update: local connector and plugin capability reports are not enough when checked only by `ok:true`. Passive release readiness now also requires field-level evidence for ChatGPT install URL scrubbing, metadata-only link endpoints, zero upstream traffic during local endpoint checks, no native Codex writeback, zero unauthorized app tools, and shared/local connector plus app-tool parity.
- 2026-06-23 visibility update: release-gate-only field checks are not enough for operator trust. Diagnostic bundle summaries and Manager latest-report summaries now expose local connector and plugin capability evidence directly, including upstream request count, allowlist-not-authorization state, unauthorized/unattributed app-tool counts, native Codex writeback, and parity cleanliness.
- 2026-06-23 log DB guard update: runtime mitigation is not enough for the `logs_2.sqlite` TRACE-write bug. Anti-Codex requires signed no-traffic log-guard evidence in diagnostics, bundles, Manager latest reports, installed-overall integrity checks, and release gates; summaries must expose protected/trigger/no-traffic/WAL fields so missing protection is visible before release.
- 2026-06-23 Manager background update: front-end pending state and report files are not enough for long-running Manager actions. The server must keep a live background-action registry, expose it through diagnostics `longTasks`, and return existing PID/start-time evidence on duplicate clicks so operators can tell a command is still running.
- 2026-06-23 fast-check update: fast installed-overall should not rely only on a final elapsed-time failure. Reuse the same diagnostics snapshot inside one run and skip lower-priority late checks with explicit budget evidence when the remaining fast budget is too small.
- 2026-06-23 Manager foreground update: long foreground Manager checks also need server-side singleflight, not only front-end pending flags. Duplicate clicks should return an existing start timestamp and avoid parallel verifier processes.
- 2026-06-23 Manager visibility update: foreground singleflight is only useful to operators if it is visible. Active foreground checks now join background checks in diagnostics `longTasks.items`; diagnostic validators must require core items but allow extra running action items.
- 2026-06-22 source and installed update: local connector allowlists are metadata previews only. A local allowlist records `locallyAllowlisted` evidence and Manager warning text, but it must not change `isAccessible`, must not be counted as remote authorization, and must not expose app tools. Diagnostics separates remote authorization, local allowlisting, and callable app-tool counts; installed overall no-traffic passes with that boundary visible.
- 2026-06-22 source update: third-party provider verification now covers nine `previous_response_id` turns while stale Antigravity model IDs are normalized to the configured provider model. It proves continuity after third-party profile save, temporary translator restart on the same state root, OpenAI-compatible provider switch, OpenAI-compatible to Anthropic protocol switch, and Anthropic back to OpenAI-compatible switch. The verifier also restores Antigravity mode without rewriting history, rejects stale Antigravity model IDs in provider requests/route audit, and checks saved/restored/public/report/error payloads do not leak API keys.
- 2026-06-23 provider continuity update: late provider-switch turns must preserve the full prior marker set, not just first/nearby turns. The local fake-provider verifier now asserts all history markers on turn 7, turn 8, and turn 9, including structured tool-call history, and records marker counts in the report.
- 2026-06-22 branding residue update: fix only user/model-visible legacy brand text, not internal compatibility names. `scripts/start-deepcodex.ps1` now reports Anti-Codex in launch/error/close prompts and message boxes; `scripts/deepcodex-setup-ui.mjs` and `scripts/DeepCodexSetup.swift` now show Anti-Codex titles and fallback setup messages. Restored-history target hints preserve real `deepcodex\...` paths but no longer capture bare `deepcodex-*` tokens into model-visible compaction context. Keep `DEEPCODEX_*`, `start-deepcodex.ps1`, `deepcodex-translator.exe`, state/log path names, and Antigravity `CORTEX_*` constants unless a separate migration plan exists.
- 2026-06-17 source-only update: the Manager is now treated as an in-app surface. The app shell exposes a visible `管理` button and the external manager command injects the same overlay into the existing Anti-Codex window, avoiding a separate browser/page as the primary path.
- 2026-06-17 source-only update: the copied host is launched from its own executable directory, closing the Windows icon/resource resolution gap for the patched relative `icon.ico` Electron option.
- 2026-06-17 source-only update: local snapshot refresh cleans stale transaction residue and does not create empty parent directories for missing shared startup sources. Fresh artifacts are retained to avoid deleting another active process's work.
- 2026-06-17 source-only update: Anti-Codex installed-runtime detection is now path-specific to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Anti-Codex\runtime`; a development checkout whose parent folder is named `Anti-Codex` is no longer treated as the installed runtime.
- 2026-06-17 source-only update: critical visible host patches now include package product name, webview title, window title, Manager/Profile menu entry, BrowserWindow icon, auth/route gates, model availability, and local plugin catalog patches. The brand patcher, installer manifest, candidate validator, and source tests are synchronized.
- Latest source proof after the 2026-06-19 native-tool default closure: 491 translator tests total with 457 passed, 34 skipped, 0 failed; all six changed PowerShell scripts parsed as explicit UTF-8; `git diff --check` passed.
- Latest full verification pass completed on 2026-06-16 after deployment to the isolated installed runtime. Source regression passed with 454 tests total, 420 passed, 34 skipped, 0 failed. Installed no-traffic checks, real account/proxy readiness, all-eight-model routing, MCP/skills inheritance, plugin capability grading, cold relaunch, translator recovery, UI idle stability, WebSocket fallback, tool/custom/MCP/parallel/streaming/compaction/cancellation flows, post-verification, and stability soak all passed.
- Historical native-bridge conclusion superseded on 2026-06-29: native MCP same-Cascade continuation is opt-in diagnostic coverage only. The default product path is now Codex-owned tools plus Antigravity model text/entitlement, with `ANTICODEX_NATIVE_TOOL_BRIDGE=0`.
- Historical prompt-mediated/native fallback wording is superseded on 2026-06-29: native bridge failures should not be part of the normal default flow unless the runtime was explicitly started with `ANTICODEX_NATIVE_TOOL_BRIDGE=1`.
- Antigravity thinking depth is account-model metadata, not a verified `plannerConfig.reasoningLevel` switch. Live `GetAvailableModels` exposes `supportsThinking`, `thinkingBudget`, and `minThinkingBudget`; the visible Low/High/Thinking model IDs route to distinct internal models and native budgets.
- The generated Codex model catalog and local `/models` endpoint now retain those native thinking fields without changing model identity. Manager `native-panel-v54` displays the exact native budget for all eight visible models.
- Anti-Codex now imports shared Codex skills plus `AGENTS.md`, `DEFAULT_SKILL_ALLOWLIST.md`, `RTK.md`, and `MCP_WORKER_USAGE_GUIDE.md` as independent local snapshots. This keeps the original Codex install untouched while allowing Anti-Codex to inherit the same working rules and key skills after an explicit refresh.
- Account hot-refresh now requires two consecutive observations of the new Antigravity identity before replacing the model catalog. A one-probe identity wobble is ignored.
- The shared Antigravity request queue is bounded and exposes privacy-safe count-only diagnostics. Queue overload fails fast with a retryable response instead of growing until the gateway or desktop becomes unresponsive.
- A complete internal-tool turn now holds the Antigravity request queue across model call, local tool execution, and model continuation. Other messages can no longer interleave between steps of the same logical turn.
- Shared Codex local snapshots are refreshed through sibling staging paths and rename swaps. Existing local-only skills are retained, shared updates win on conflicts, file/directory type changes are handled, and a failed build leaves the active snapshot untouched.
- Local snapshot refresh has a stale-safe cross-process lock, so startup recovery and a manual Manager refresh cannot modify the isolated Codex home concurrently.
- Graceful translator shutdown now cancels active Cascades plus queued and non-streaming Antigravity work before exit, reducing stale background work after a normal gateway restart.
- Normal startup and translator-only recovery no longer recopy full plugin/skill snapshots. They keep existing essential local directories, refresh only small guidance files, fill missing essential caches, and leave full snapshot updates to the explicit Manager refresh path.
- Install and guarded Codex-host sync perform one full transactional local-feature snapshot, so routine startup can stay lightweight without leaving a fresh install or updated host missing shared skills/plugins.
- Historical native MCP evidence remains useful only for opt-in diagnostics. It is not the default user path after the 2026-06-29 model-only correction.
- Translator-owned `web_search` and `web_fetch` remain local tools. Known internal tools retry one failed execution once and preserve the concrete final error instead of misreporting a transient failure as an unavailable tool.
- Older statements below that describe native MCP or prompt-mediated Antigravity tooling as the default are historical. The authoritative default contract is Codex-owned tools with Antigravity supplying model text only; native Antigravity tooling is explicit opt-in.

- 2026-06-30 desktop-task-suite release evidence: the agentic desktop task suite summary report must be directly verified by release/report surfaces; do not rely solely on scattered individual UI smoke reports.
- 2026-06-30 diagnostic collector summary-field migration: after adding a new safe summary object such as `desktopTaskSuiteEvidence`, a first installed collector run can fail while reports/runtime files are being refreshed; rerun once after runtime sync before treating `The property 'Count' cannot be found on this object` as product evidence. The real fix is to add the field to `Read-JsonSummary`, then verify a fresh bundle's `reports-summary.json`.
- 2026-06-30 MCP worker real-list support evidence: diagnostics must validate the seven fixed worker MCP servers by passed `mcpRealListPassedServers`, not only `mcpRealListPassed >= 2`. Release gates should also reject MCP/Skill reports missing passed real `tools/list` evidence for any of `antigravity-code-worker`, `deepseek-code-worker`, `deveco-code-worker`, `kilo-worker`, `mimo-claude-coworker`, `mimocode-worker`, or `opencode-worker`.
- 2026-06-30 local-feature refresh sequencing: do not run refresh-features, MCP/Skill, and plugin-capability verification in parallel after shared connector cache drift. A parallel batch can leave release gates reading old failed plugin reports while another process is repairing the local snapshot. Run refresh/verify-only first, then MCP/Skill and plugin checks, then release gates.
- 2026-06-30 refresh-features skills directory lock recovery: a full local-feature refresh can repair connector/app-tool drift but still fail because Windows locks the local `skills` directory rename. Treat the full-refresh failure as non-release evidence, then rerun `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1 -VerifyOnly`; if verify-only is clean, the snapshot is usable.
- 2026-06-30 PowerShell regex quoting: when searching test names with regex containing `("` or `|`, use single-quoted PowerShell strings. A double-quoted `rtk rg -n "test\(\".*release|..."` command can fail in the shell before `rg` runs.

## Confirmed Facts
- Anti-Codex now has an installed runtime-config verifier. It reads `%APPDATA%\anticodex\codex-home-deepseek-app\config.toml` and fails if the isolated config points at remote ChatGPT/OpenAI account endpoints, uses non-file auth, loses the generated model catalog, or re-enables Responses WebSocket / remote plugin feature flags.
- The installed overall verifier now runs that runtime-config check by default. Latest installed proof reports `runtimeConfig.ok:true`, local gateway URLs, file auth, isolated Anti-Codex Codex home, and `responses_websockets`, `responses_websockets_v2`, `plugins`, `plugin_sharing`, and `remote_plugin` all set to `false`.
- Anti-Codex mode now treats the original Codex config as import-only during startup. It can still read global MCP/config blocks into the Anti-Codex config, but it no longer writes restored plugin registrations or permission defaults back into `%USERPROFILE%\.codex\config.toml`.
- The installed translator-only restart after this change left the original Codex config file's length and `LastWriteTime` unchanged while Anti-Codex health returned ready with eight Antigravity models.
- The installed translator-only recovery verifier now enforces that boundary automatically through before/after file snapshots and reports `originalCodexConfigUnchanged:true` on success.
- Anti-Codex skills are now a local installed copy instead of a live junction to the original Codex skills directory. The installed check reports `skills.linkType:null`, and sampled skill directories have no link targets.
- The skills import path is deliberately merge-based after the first link-to-copy conversion. Existing Anti-Codex-local skills are preserved instead of deleting the whole local skills directory during normal startup or skills-only sync.
- Small plugin-host snapshots now use Anti-Codex local-copy semantics: `skills`, `cache\codex_app_directory`, and `cache\codex_apps_tools` are copied into the Anti-Codex state home. Live links remain only for larger plugin host sidecars where full copying would increase launch time, disk use, and stale-cache risk.
- Translator-only recovery now refreshes those local snapshots before starting the gateway, so connector metadata and skills do not depend on a full desktop relaunch.
- Latest installed MCP/skills proof reports `connectorDirectory` under the Anti-Codex state home, `cacheLinkType:null`, `cacheFileCount:23`, 1494 connectors, 178 plugins, and three MCP servers.
- The installed overall verifier now checks external Windows branding, not just ASAR strings. Latest proof shows the live main process is `Anti-Codex.exe`, the visible window title is `Anti-Codex`, and the executable metadata fields `FileDescription`, `ProductName`, and `OriginalFilename` are all Anti-Codex values.
- The lower-right/taskbar naming path is covered in two layers: the ASAR tray tooltip still resolves through Electron `app.getName()`, and the app name is patched to `Anti-Codex`; the user-visible Windows main entry point is separately verified through process/window/executable metadata.
- Anti-Codex now reads its own config home before the shared original Codex home when projecting local plugins/connectors. The shared Codex home remains a fallback/import source rather than the first source of truth.
- Duplicate plugin projection keeps the richer metadata record instead of whichever record is discovered first. This preserves skill, app, and MCP metadata when the Anti-Codex home and shared Codex home both contain the same plugin key.
- Latest installed local capability proof after the projection and local-snapshot fixes: 178 installed plugins, 69 plugins with skills metadata, 151 with app bindings, 3 with MCP declarations, 1494 connector directory entries, 0 accessible remote connectors, and 0 exposed app tools.
- Anti-Codex now has a local `/anticodex/diagnostics` endpoint for troubleshooting. It summarizes process, account/circuit, model catalog, latest model route, and isolated state paths while explicitly avoiding prompt/message/tool-output transcript fields.
