- `/anticodex/diagnostics.toolBridge` now makes the current tool bridge contract explicit: `mode:"native-mcp-same-cascade"`, `nativeAntigravityToolEvents:true`, and `nativeBridgeDefaultEnabled:true`. Native capability diagnostics also report `nativeMcpPermissionMode:"automatic-before-codex-execution"`.
- `test-anticodex-tool-bridge-mode.ps1` is the installed read-only proof for that contract and fails if the native default, automatic permission mode, or prompt fallback declaration drifts.
- The installed overall check now includes diagnostics verification, so the internal overall verifier proves health, UI, branding, model routing, MCP/skills, plugin capability, account/proxy readiness, and local diagnostics in one pass.
- The installed overall verifier now checks the real desktop UI through the isolated CDP port. The latest live UI proof shows page title `Anti-Codex`, no ChatGPT login text, no reconnect text, and a visible Antigravity model label `Gemini 3.1 Pro (High)`.
- The internal installed overall verifier catches three user-visible branding/model regressions automatically: patched ASAR app identity, Windows/window process identity, and rendered UI text.
- The installed tray tooltip path uses Electron `app.getName()`, so patching only the window title is insufficient. The installed ASAR now sets the app name to `Anti-Codex`, removes the old dynamic app-name setter, and keeps the tray tooltip resolving to Anti-Codex.
- The installed Windows app user model ID is now a separate `AntiCodex` value, so taskbar grouping is separated from the original Codex while the visible main window title remains `Anti-Codex`.
- `/anticodex/account` returns Antigravity account readiness with a privacy-preserving fingerprint. This is stronger than comparing model catalogs, because two accounts can expose the same eight models.
- Account-change hot refresh must observe a comparable fingerprint before acting. Snapshots without identity proof are intentionally ignored to avoid refreshing on transient signed-out or partial-status responses.
- The latest installed overall proof after deployment: account ready, eight models, one true Anti-Codex main window, one translator, one launcher, original Codex still separate, local MCP/skills inherited, plugin tool exposure filtered, tool continuation working, and account/proxy readiness returning a real marker.
- Anti-Codex now has a local hot-refresh endpoint at `/anticodex/models/refresh`; it refreshes the Antigravity account model list without closing the Anti-Codex window or restarting the translator.
- The installed model refresh script tries the hot-refresh endpoint first. If it succeeds, it exits immediately; if unavailable, it falls back to the older restart-based refresh.
- Installed hot-refresh proof: the endpoint returned eight live Antigravity model IDs, `/models` reflected the same list, a real selected-model request succeeded, and translator PID stayed stable at 9068.
- Installed shortcut proof: running the installed refresh script kept translator PID 9068 unchanged, proving it did not use the restart fallback in the healthy runtime.
- A real installed stability soak is now available at `scripts/test-anticodex-stability-soak.ps1`; the installed copy is deployed under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Anti-Codex\runtime\scripts`.
- PowerShell functions unwrap single-item arrays unless explicitly returned with a unary comma. Without this, Responses `input` can be sent as a single object instead of an array and the Antigravity bridge may answer with startup/readiness text instead of the actual user prompt.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 does not support `ConvertFrom-Json -Depth`; using it in SSE parsing silently produced null event data when errors were caught. Removing that parameter fixed streaming final-text and cancellation parsing in the soak script.
- Antigravity Cascade trajectories can contain stale readiness/welcome planner responses. The bridge now prefers planner text containing the current prompt marker and otherwise skips readiness boilerplate when another completed response is available.
- The installed runtime passed a 90-second mixed stability soak on port 8484 with 17 health checks, stable translator PID 2252, eight visible models, successful long-context, tool-loop, stream, and cancel paths.
- Codex 26.609 does not reliably derive the Windows window title from the patched ASAR `productName`; before the fix, executable metadata and the web page title were `Anti-Codex` while Win32 `MainWindowTitle` was still `Codex`.
- The 26.609 main bundle supplies titles through both `title:fallback??electron.app.getName()` and `createPrimaryWindow({title:electron.app.getName(),...})`. Both sites must be replaced with fixed-length `Anti-Codex` literals.
- After the corrected host sync, the installed main process is `Anti-Codex.exe`, Windows reports `MainWindowTitle: Anti-Codex`, and the original Codex process remains separate and unchanged.
- The current installed host bundles `codex-cli 0.140.0-alpha.2`; its translator is account-ready with eight visible Antigravity models.
- Earlier builds were not native-equivalent: they used Codex Desktop as the UI, translated Responses payloads to a text prompt, launched Antigravity CLI per turn, and reconstructed a response from CLI state.
- The current Antigravity turn path now calls Antigravity Desktop's local Cascade service directly: `StartCascade` -> `SendUserCascadeMessage` with `cascadeConfig.plannerConfig.planModel` -> `GetCascadeTrajectory`.
- The working model field is the Antigravity internal enum from `GetAvailableModels.response.models[id].model`, not the public display ID by itself.
- CLI/SQLite is now a fallback path only; the default route should be the desktop local service.
- Tool, cancellation, streaming, and full session semantics are still partial compatibility behavior until Codex core/app-server is patched to use the native Antigravity event model directly.
- Original Codex and Anti-Codex use separate executables, user-data directories, state roots, ports, and shortcuts.
- Anti-Codex's local translator is healthy on `192.168.1.2:8484`; the user's external network is not the source of the reconnect loop.
- Codex Desktop attempts a Responses websocket connection before HTTP fallback.
- The gateway currently returns HTTP 501 for that websocket path.
- Codex plugin endpoints currently return shapes that fail Codex deserialization.
- Antigravity Desktop's `GetAvailableModels` currently returns eight account-visible model IDs.
- The generated catalog contains those eight exact IDs and display names.
- Runtime config has been corrected from legacy `gpt-5.5` to a generated Antigravity model ID (`gemini-pro-agent`).
- Startup now removes an invalid Antigravity-mode `model = ...` when the slug is absent from the generated model catalog.
- Exact Antigravity model IDs work through the standalone CLI.
- Chinese text can pass end to end when it enters Node correctly.
- The old per-turn CLI bridge has high startup latency and depends on internal conversation SQLite layout; it should remain emergency fallback, not the normal transport.
- Default prompt clipping has been raised to 1,000,000 characters so Anti-Codex no longer self-truncates normal long-context usage unless an explicit safety budget is passed.
- Antigravity Desktop's Cascade protocol accepts a planner `toolConfig`; disabling Antigravity-owned tools plus `askPermission` prevents Antigravity from asking for local approvals in the model bridge.
- `WaitForConversationFullyIdle` is the reliable completion gate. A DONE planner step can arrive before tool/checkpoint activity is finished and must not be treated as final completion.
- Installed Anti-Codex long streaming proof: first text delta arrived at 8.759s, completion at 13.785s, with 6 text delta events and Chinese output.
- Installed Anti-Codex cancellation proof: `/v1/responses/{id}/cancel` returned 200 and the stream ended with `response.cancelled`, not `response.completed`.
- The previous startup recovery failure was caused by the shared launcher mutex being held for the desktop lifetime; translator-only recovery now bypasses that mutex and does not restart the desktop window.
- Installed Anti-Codex has a real copied host at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Anti-Codex\codex-patched-app\Anti-Codex.exe`; Windows version metadata is `Anti-Codex`, and the running main process is `Anti-Codex.exe`.
- Installed auth state is local-key only: no ChatGPT OAuth token shape is written in Antigravity mode.
- Current installed model catalog exposes eight account-visible models with numeric display names, including Gemini 3.5 Flash, Gemini 3.1 Pro, Claude Sonnet 4.6, Claude Opus 4.6, and GPT-OSS 120B.
- Anti-Codex inherits global MCP server blocks and links skills from the original Codex home. Remote plugin marketplace features remain disabled for startup stability.
- The installed UI currently reports no reconnect text and no login text through CDP, and displays the model label `Gemini 3.1 Pro (High)`.
- Runtime translator failure no longer requires a shortcut relaunch: the long-lived desktop launcher watchdog restarts the translator while keeping the Anti-Codex main process alive.
- In Antigravity mode, Codex tool definitions must be forwarded as prompt-level tool-call protocol hints because Antigravity Desktop's native tool execution remains disabled. The translator now converts returned `<tool_calls>` markup into Responses `function_call` items and suppresses markup leaks during streaming.
- MCP namespace tools need reverse-routing after Antigravity returns a flattened tool name. The installed bridge now maps `mcp__node_repl__js` back to Codex's `js` subtool before returning the function call to the client.
- Custom/freeform tools use the same bridge and return Responses `custom_tool_call` items; installed `apply_patch` verification preserved the raw patch body without leaking protocol markup.
- Responses history is now persisted under the isolated Anti-Codex state directory with bounded entry and character limits. Continuation by `previous_response_id` survives translator and desktop restarts.
- Anti-Codex main-process crashes and translator crashes are independently recoverable without touching the original Codex installation.
- The earlier Antigravity restart loop was caused by process-name-only detection, one hard-coded install path, no stability gate, and no cooldown during an Antigravity updater race.
- Antigravity Desktop recovery now adopts the actual running desktop executable, excludes the CLI worker, supports `Antigravity.exe` and `Antigravity IDE.exe`, discovers registry installations, and requires eight seconds of process stability before declaring success.
- Installed Antigravity failure recovery is proven end to end: Anti-Codex remained alive while Antigravity restarted, then a real Chinese model request succeeded.
- Streaming retries are safe only before a Codex-visible event is emitted. The bridge now retries one transient desktop-service failure in that window and never retries after output becomes visible.
- The local Cascade transport accepted a single 900,013-character message with both boundary markers intact; long context is not being clipped by the local transport.
- The first real host-sync attempt exposed a launcher race: killing the desktop host triggered the old launcher's crash recovery before the active directory could move. Host sync now uses a maintenance marker, stops legacy launchers, waits for all active-host processes to release, preserves rollback, and automatically reopens Anti-Codex.
- A real post-fix host sync passed candidate validation and activation. The active and rollback hosts both report `codex-cli 0.138.0-alpha.7`, and the original Codex stayed running from its separate installation.
- A second consecutive real host sync also passed, leaving no update-candidate directory or maintenance marker; the update flow is repeatable rather than a one-off success.
- Post-sync UI verification shows title `Anti-Codex`, no login or reconnect state, and the exact eight-model Antigravity menu. Installed auth contains only `OPENAI_API_KEY`.
- A native-command nonzero exit does not enter PowerShell `catch` automatically. The previous model-refresh logic could therefore silently fall back to the bundled catalog after discovery failed; it now explicitly checks the exit code and preserves the last real account catalog.
- Proxy and gateway failures may surface through Antigravity as network/proxy errors, gateway status, deadline exceeded, `EOF`, or `Failed to generate plan`. These now receive at most three total attempts with progressive delay, only before any output is visible; quota, auth, model-not-found, cancellation, and post-output failures remain non-retryable.
- Account switching now has a guided local workflow. After the user changes the signed-in Antigravity account, the model refresh script safely restarts only Anti-Codex when needed and regenerates the model catalog.
- Real refresh verification left original Codex and Antigravity Desktop process identities unchanged, refreshed the model catalog, removed the maintenance marker, and restored a healthy Chinese request.
- Tool continuation must preserve the assistant call record and the returned result as one logical pair. Flattening the result to plain `[TOOL]` text loses the tool name, arguments, and call identity.
- Completed tool output is now carried as a single-line JSON data record with an explicit untrusted-data warning. A per-result 120,000-character head/tail guard prevents one tool dump from consuming the full model context.
- Parallel tool results can arrive out of order; pairing by `call_id` is covered by regression tests.
- Antigravity Desktop can be process-healthy while its model endpoint returns an empty response. On 2026-06-13 its UI target was `onboarding?login=true`, confirming that an empty model list can mean the Antigravity account is signed out rather than an Anti-Codex transport failure.
- The installed gateway now returns an actionable sign-in/proxy error for this state instead of the ambiguous “did not report any available models” message.

- `GetAvailableModels` currently contains 20 service entries, but only eight IDs are present in `agentModelSorts` and therefore visible in the Antigravity desktop model menu. Anti-Codex now uses the sorted visible set for both its UI catalog and readiness count.
- The recurring translator restart was reproducible as a watchdog false positive: desktop connection discovery used synchronous PowerShell process and port scans while the watchdog allowed only a two-second health response.
- The translator now caches the discovered local Antigravity connection, invalidates and rediscovers after failure, and the launcher requires three failed health probes before restart.
- A 45-second installed soak crossed multiple readiness intervals with five real Chinese turns. Every turn returned HTTP 200 with the expected Chinese text, health stayed ready with eight models, and the translator PID did not change.
- The installed model menu was inspected through the desktop debug endpoint and showed exactly the eight live Antigravity display names, including their numeric versions and reasoning variants.
- The installed two-turn tool loop now passes against the live account, preserving tool-call identity and consuming the returned value on continuation.
- Streaming cancellation is now a mandatory installed contract check rather than evidence hidden inside an optional soak. A successful cancellation produces `response.created`, `response.in_progress`, then `response.cancelled`; the cancel endpoint and SSE share the same response ID, no completed/failed terminal event follows, and readiness remains healthy.
- The installed compaction contract is now proven end to end rather than inferred from unit tests: compact responses contain one readable `context_compaction`, compact mode suppresses supplied tools, direct compaction-item replay works, and persisted `previous_response_id` resume from the compact response preserves the active task.
- A fake-tool-claim guard false positive could previously break legitimate long-session continuation when a recalled next action contained words such as `Next Action`, `verify`, or adjectival `installed`. The guard now recognizes explicit compaction-state recall only when the request asks for recall and the response preserves a marker from the compacted summary; ordinary future-action promises remain blocked.
- Antigravity Desktop's current Cascade schema contains structured tool-call proposal and choice step types, but the ordinary CLI/Cascade client path does not accept arbitrary external tool definitions.
- `CustomAgentSpec.customTools` is a repeated string field containing tool names, not full JSON tool definitions. Submitting it through `SendUserCascadeMessage` from both CLI and SDK trajectory sources is rejected with `CustomAgentSpec is only allowed to be specified by or forwarded to SDK executables`.
- The binary also contains `SdkCustomizationConfig` and `SdkToolOverride` schemas, but no callable Cascade request field has been confirmed that accepts those definitions from an external client.
- Antigravity tool calls are prompt-mediated bridge, not native Antigravity custom tools. Codex tool definitions are converted to Chat function calls and forwarded as prompt-level protocol hints. The model's `<tool_calls>` DSML markup in its text output is parsed and converted back into Responses `function_call` items. This is now explicitly exposed through `diagnostics.tools.bridge: "prompt_mediated"` at `/anticodex/diagnostics`.
- Google Antigravity SDK 0.1.3 provides native structured custom tools, MCP, streaming text/thought/tool events, cancellation, compaction tracking, model selection, and thinking levels through its bundled `localharness.exe`.
- The public SDK is not a replacement for the account-backed desktop route: its local connection explicitly requires a Gemini API key or Vertex credentials and calls the Gemini backend directly. It therefore would not consume the signed-in Antigravity account's model allowance.
- For the current product requirement, Antigravity Desktop Cascade remains the only confirmed account-backed model route. Until Google exposes an account-backed SDK/custom-tool entry point, the Codex tool bridge must remain external to Cascade and should be hardened rather than represented as native Antigravity tool transport.
- The Antigravity-mode external tool bridge is now strict by default in the Responses conversion path: it only emits Codex tool calls for names present in the routed Codex tool map, rejects missing required schema parameters, rejects extra parameters when `additionalProperties: false`, and coerces object, array, numeric, and boolean XML parameter bodies according to the real routed schema.
- Non-Antigravity compatibility behavior remains permissive for older DeepCodex/Chat-style routes, so the stricter bridge does not break the legacy unknown-tool pass-through tests.
- Antigravity's non-blocking streamed Cascade path can expose transient planner text before the final completed planner response is visible. The installed bridge now performs one final `GetCascadeTrajectory` after `WaitForConversationFullyIdle` and treats `extractAssistantText` from the completed planner response as authoritative.
- Test harnesses that pipe Chinese JavaScript through PowerShell stdin can corrupt the prompt into question marks. Installed streaming validation should construct UTF-8 request bodies explicitly or use Unicode escapes; otherwise model behavior can be misdiagnosed as a product bug.
- Antigravity Desktop/Cascade behaves like a single shared interactive lane for this bridge. Anti-Codex now serializes all account-backed Antigravity requests through one in-process FIFO queue so simultaneous Codex UI requests do not overlap inside the same desktop service session.
- Queued streamed Responses are registered before upstream start, so `/responses/{id}/cancel` can cancel a request while it is still waiting in the Anti-Codex queue; cancelled queued work must not enter Cascade later.
- Fake tool-execution guarding can overfire on synthetic long-context marker echoes when the model says it "found" both markers. The safe carve-out is limited to text containing both `LONGCTX_HEAD_<digits>` and `LONGCTX_TAIL_<digits>`; ordinary search/run/install claims remain blocked.
- The latest installed queue/cancel soak passed with the translator PID stable at 25684 and Antigravity readiness still reporting eight account-visible models.
- The installed queue smoke proved queued cancellation at the HTTP/SSE layer: a second streamed response cancelled while a long first request was active returned `response.cancelled` without ever emitting `response.completed`.
- Worker note: Antigravity CLI worker failed this queue task with exit code 2 and no useful logs; DeepSeek produced a partial queue module but exceeded the requested file scope. Main-agent review kept only the validated queue/test semantics.
- Proxy or Google-side network loss can surface as retryable Antigravity errors such as `proxy error`, `Failed to generate plan`, `UNAVAILABLE`, or `DEADLINE_EXCEEDED`. Repeated failures should not keep entering the shared Cascade queue; the bridge now opens a short in-process circuit and returns retryable 503 metadata.
- Translator process health must stay separate from Antigravity account/proxy readiness. The launcher watchdog checks only `/health.ok`; `accountReady:false`, `accountState:"transient_error"`, or an open `antigravityCircuitState` must not trigger a translator restart.
- Installed deployment gotcha: `start-deepcodex.ps1` runs `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Anti-Codex\runtime\translator\adaptive-server.mjs`, not only the root-level runtime copy. Deploying translator changes must update the `runtime\translator` files.
- Installed MCP/skills verification should be a local read-only check, not a UI guess. The installed runtime currently proves three MCP servers (`node_repl`, `deepseek-code-worker`, `antigravity-code-worker`), an Anti-Codex-owned local skills directory, an Anti-Codex-owned local connector directory snapshot, 178 locally projected installed plugins, and 1494 cached connectors through the local Anti-Codex backend endpoints.
- Plugin and connector catalog endpoints may contain cached ecosystem URLs such as app install links, but Anti-Codex still keeps ChatGPT login/auth routes blocked and plugin marketplace startup disabled. The important invariant is that installed verification uses only the local gateway with `Proxy = $null` and does not require ChatGPT account login.
- Installed plugin capability grading distinguishes local visibility from authorization. Current installed numbers: 178 projected plugins, 69 with skills metadata, 151 with app bindings, 3 with MCP declarations, 1494 enabled connector directory entries, 0 connectors marked `isAccessible:true`, and 0 exposed app tools. The 52 cached app tools are historical cache findings and are not currently exposed or active capabilities.
- Because connector `isAccessible` is currently false for all cached connector directory entries, Anti-Codex should not claim remote app connectors are actually linked. The safe current product surface is local plugin/skill/MCP discovery plus connector directory display as unavailable; connector app tools are filtered out unless the connector is accessible.
- Cached connector directory entries can include `chatgpt.com/apps/...` install URLs from the original Codex cache. Anti-Codex now scrubs those URLs to `null` in the local projection; installed verification showed 1494 connector entries with zero ChatGPT install URLs after translator restart.
- `/backend-api/wham/apps` is a tool-surface endpoint, not just a directory endpoint. Exposing cached app tools for `isAccessible:false` connectors creates fake capability. Anti-Codex now returns app/connector directory entries but filters tools to accessible connector IDs only; current installed result is 1494 connector entries and 0 exposed app tools.
- Translator-only recovery needs a real installed-build check, not only static launcher assertions. `test-anticodex-translator-recovery.ps1` now invokes the installed recovery path in a bounded child process so `exit` inside `start-deepcodex.ps1` cannot terminate the verifier early, then confirms health, plugin projection, and app-tool exposure.
- Latest installed translator-only recovery proof: runtime root is `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Anti-Codex\runtime`, health is `antigravity-desktop`, account is ready with eight models, 178 installed plugins are projected, and app-tool exposure remains 0.
- `start-deepcodex.ps1 -TranslatorOnly` should be a gateway recovery path, not a full desktop boot path. It now returns before Codex executable discovery, onboarding state seeding, model catalog refresh, and plugin sync; the remaining installed verifier time is dominated by cold gateway readiness plus plugin/tool endpoint checks.
- Installed `start-anticodex.ps1` must account for its deployed `runtime\scripts` location. Using two parent directories from there resolves to `runtime`, not the install root, so the launcher missed an already-open Anti-Codex window and fell into the full startup/monitor path. The resolver now treats a parent named `runtime` specially and returns its parent as the install root.
- Re-launching Anti-Codex while it is already open is now an explicit fast path: installed verification returned in about 1.3s, kept a single visible `Anti-Codex` main process, and left the translator healthy.
- Live Antigravity model discovery should not block every normal startup. The launcher now uses the last successful generated model catalog when present; the manual refresh shortcut and account-recovery refresh remain the paths that update the catalog from the signed-in account.
- Cold relaunch must be verified separately from repeated launch. `test-anticodex-cold-relaunch.ps1` now stops only Anti-Codex host/launcher/translator processes, starts the installed launcher, confirms the original Codex main process set is unchanged, and waits for a healthy Antigravity gateway.
- Latest cold relaunch proof: installed Anti-Codex relaunched in about 29.5s with one visible main process, one translator, one launcher, health ready, and eight visible models. A follow-up repeated launch returned in about 1.45s and a 45-second installed soak passed with stable translator PID 7740.
- Overall installed verification should count the actual desktop process by exact `codex-patched-app\Anti-Codex.exe`, not every executable under `codex-patched-app`. `resources\codex.exe app-server` is expected as a child host process and must not be treated as a duplicate Anti-Codex window.
- Latest overall installed proof: health is ready with eight models and circuit closed; one real Anti-Codex main window, one translator, one launcher, and the original Codex main process set remain separate. MCP/skills and plugin capability checks pass, and a 45-second optional soak kept translator PID 7740 stable.
- The tightened cold-relaunch verifier passed after deployment: a full Anti-Codex-only restart produced one true main window, one translator, one launcher, and the immediate overall verifier confirmed MCP/skills plus plugin capability recovery.
- Account/proxy readiness is now verified by a real request, not only cached health. The installed script hot-refreshes models, uses `/models`, sends a marker turn, and classifies failures as account/no-model, proxy/network transient, or circuit-open while keeping local Anti-Codex requests proxy-bypassed.
- Latest account/proxy proof: the installed verifier returned diagnosis `ready`, hot refresh succeeded, eight models were visible, `gemini-3.5-flash-low` returned the exact marker, and the circuit stayed closed.
- The desktop surface is intentionally minimal: only `Anti-Codex.lnk` remains on the desktop. Manual check, account-switch, proxy-drill, model-routing, refresh, and sync entry points remain as installed/project scripts instead of visible desktop helpers.
- Account switching still has a guided local workflow instead of a memory-only procedure. The script opens Antigravity Desktop for the manual account change, then refreshes Anti-Codex models and runs the installed overall verifier. The verified non-interactive tail proves the refresh/check half works without touching original Codex.
- Proxy outage testing still has a guided local workflow. The script records baseline readiness, lets the user break Antigravity's proxy, captures the outage diagnosis without treating the translator as crashed, then waits for recovery after the proxy is restored. The verified non-interactive baseline proves the evidence collector and readiness probe work; the actual outage phase still requires the user to toggle the proxy.
- Installed manual checks now leave durable evidence under `%APPDATA%\anticodex\reports`. The latest overall, account-switch, and proxy-drill reports are overwritten for easy lookup, while timestamped history files preserve previous runs.
- Model routing is now directly auditable. Each successful Antigravity request records requested model ID, resolved model ID, Antigravity display name, internal `planModel` enum, cascade ID, and status under `%APPDATA%\anticodex\model-route-audit.json`; it deliberately does not record prompt text.
- The installed account/proxy verifier now treats model-route mismatch as a failure. Latest proof selected `gemini-3.5-flash-low`, resolved the same public ID, and sent Antigravity internal model `MODEL_PLACEHOLDER_M20`.
- Full model routing is now repeatably verifiable. The model-routing verifier runs one marker request through every visible model and fails if any selected public ID does not match the resolved Antigravity route or lacks an internal model enum.
- Latest full-model proof covered all eight visible account models; every model returned its marker and recorded a matching resolved model plus cascade ID. This is the strongest current evidence that the UI model menu is not a fake shell.
- Tool continuation is now part of the default installed health proof, not only an optional soak. The verifier requires a real first-turn `function_call`, feeds back the completed result by `call_id`, and requires the second turn to answer from that result without repeating a tool call or leaking tool markup.
- Latest installed tool-loop proof used `gemini-3.5-flash-low`, produced a `marker_tool` call, preserved `MARKER_731=42` through continuation, and recorded matching Antigravity route metadata for both turns.
- Account switching cannot be proven solely from model IDs when two accounts expose the same catalog. The workflow now reports before/after fingerprints and uses `model_catalog_changed` or `model_catalog_unchanged` instead of falsely claiming account identity changed.
- The model refresh fallback had a lifecycle bug: invoking the long-lived launcher with PowerShell's call operator transferred the refresh process into launcher monitoring and prevented the caller from returning. Detached launch plus bounded health wait fixes this while preserving restart recovery.
- Forced installed fallback proof stopped the local translator/launcher, made hot refresh unavailable, completed the detached restart in about 32.3s, restored eight models, and left the original Codex main process set unchanged.
- Worker edits can be actively unsafe even when the task is narrow. In this run, DeepSeek overwrote the recovery script with an old direct-call pattern and changed forbidden docs, so the worker was cancelled and the script was rebuilt by the main agent before validation.
- Translator-only recovery must load the cached/generated Antigravity model catalog before starting the gateway. Otherwise `/models` can briefly fall back to the bundled two-entry catalog while health already reports the account as ready.
- The installed recovery path now preserves the original Codex config timestamp and length while restoring Anti-Codex health with eight real models.
- Current installed app connector state is directory-only, not callable-tool-ready: 1494 connectors are visible, 1494 are enabled, 0 are `isAccessible:true`, and cached `codex_apps_tools` files contain 0 tool definitions.
- Local projection now reads connector directory and app-tool cache from the first available snapshot home only. With an Anti-Codex local snapshot present, shared original Codex connector/app-tool caches are not mixed into the runtime response; shared is fallback only when the local snapshot is absent.
- The installed independence audit now makes this boundary explicit. Required local snapshots are `skills`, `cache\codex_app_directory`, and `cache\codex_apps_tools`; the latest installed run reports all three as present and not links. It also reports `remainingSharedLinks:[]` for the current machine.
- `/backend-api/wham/apps` should keep returning 0 tools in that state. Exposing tools for inaccessible connectors would be fake capability; the verifier should fail only when accessible connectors exist but no tools are projected.
- Responses WebSocket is still not natively implemented, but the fallback is now explicit for both HTTP GET prewarm and real WebSocket Upgrade requests. The installed gateway returns `HTTP/1.1 501 Not Implemented` with `unsupported_websocket` for `/responses` and `/v1/responses`.
- Do not fake `101 Switching Protocols` until the native Codex Responses WebSocket frame protocol is captured. A guessed WebSocket can make the client believe the transport works and then fail later in the event/state machine; explicit 501 is safer and now verified.
- Installed overall verification now includes the WebSocket Upgrade probe, so future Codex syncs that change reconnect behavior will surface in the default check instead of being diagnosed by UI symptoms only.
- Installed overall verification now includes `test-anticodex-sync-readiness.ps1`, a read-only sync readiness verifier. It checks the sync entry, host candidate validator, active/rollback markers, maintenance marker absence, and `Anti-Codex.exe` identity without executing sync or modifying the installed host.
- Added `test-anticodex-mcp-tool-loop.ps1`, an installed MCP namespace tool-loop verifier. It uses the namespace tool `mcp__node_repl__` with subtool `js`, sends it through the Responses API with `tool_choice: "required"`, and verifies the bridge reverse-maps the flattened tool name `mcp__node_repl__js` back to the Codex subtool name `"js"` in the `function_call` output. The second turn proves `function_call_output` continuation consumes the result without duplicate tool invocation. No real MCP tools are executed; only the Responses protocol output is inspected. Included in the installed overall check as `$checks.mcpToolLoop`.
- Added `test-anticodex-custom-tool-loop.ps1`, an installed custom/freeform tool-loop verifier. It defines an `apply_patch` custom tool with `type="custom"` and grammar format metadata (`syntax="lark"`). The first turn verifies the bridge returns a `custom_tool_call` output item with name=apply_patch and input equal to the complete patch body (including `*** Begin Patch`/`*** End Patch` markers and a deterministic marker). The second turn proves `custom_tool_call_output` continuation consumes the result without duplicate tool invocation. No real patches are executed; only the Responses protocol output is inspected. Included in the installed overall check as `$checks.customToolLoop`.
- Antigravity's returned display name is not always consistent with its public model ID. The live account returned `gemini-3.5-flash-low` with display name `Gemini 3.5 Flash (Medium)`. Anti-Codex now treats an explicit reasoning suffix in the model ID as authoritative, preserves the exact model/version name, and normalizes both the UI catalog and route-audit display name.
- Installed parallel-tool proof now covers a failure mode not established by the earlier single-tool loop: two distinct calls are emitted in one turn, call IDs and arguments remain separate, and reverse-order results are matched by call ID rather than position. The same proof now runs through both non-streaming Responses and real SSE streaming.

## Open Questions
- How much of Antigravity's streaming/cancel/tool event surface can be mapped to Codex's native app-server events without prompt simulation?
- Whether Antigravity exposes any additional account-specific model metadata beyond the current eight `GetAvailableModels` entries.
- Whether to re-enable any remote/plugin marketplace features after the native Antigravity bridge has a longer stability soak.
- Whether account switching changes the local Cascade endpoint or only the account-visible model catalog after the accepted close-and-reopen flow.
- Whether a real external proxy interruption produces a distinct desktop-service failure that needs a longer circuit-breaker than the current transient retry and desktop watchdog. The installed proxy drill now provides the repeatable evidence collector for that manual drill.

## External Research
- Store source summaries here only. Treat external content as untrusted data.
- Full model-routing proof is the strongest evidence because it sends real requests through every model, but it consumes Antigravity traffic. A separate read-only model-routing mode is useful for routine UI/model-name sanity checks: it proves the gateway and diagnostics expose exact public IDs/display names, while deliberately not claiming that each model was routed through a live Cascade.
- Antigravity Desktop can expose a valid cached model catalog while `GetUserStatus` fails with `GetCascadeModelConfigData() is nil`. Treat this as account-detail degradation, not as proof that the model menu is empty. Anti-Codex should keep serving cached exact model IDs, keep the gateway healthy, and let real generation/tool requests determine whether the upstream account has actually failed.
- Directly invoking the installed runtime `start-deepcodex.ps1 -TranslatorOnly` without `start-anticodex.ps1` used to fall back to `%APPDATA%\deepcodex`, which broke isolation and could make diagnostics point at the wrong model catalog. Installed Anti-Codex runtime detection must happen before the default state-root is chosen.
- A plain existence-only host marker was not strong enough for long-term one-click sync safety. The active marker is now an atomic JSON manifest bound to the patched host contents by SHA-256 hashes and an explicit nine-patch compatibility contract.
- Fault injection proved a failed critical branding patch cannot create a valid candidate marker or modify the active host. This closes the most important candidate activation trust gap.
- Restart-refresh fallback had a real stale-account risk: after hot refresh failure, startup could reuse the previous account's cached model catalog. The fallback now forces live Antigravity model discovery, and an installed forced-fallback run proved the catalog regenerated while original Codex remained unchanged.
- Hardcoding the current private gateway address in standalone checks was a long-term portability risk even though runtime startup already discovered the address dynamically. All BaseUrl-bearing scripts now use one shared resolver, so shortcuts and direct checks follow network changes.
- The translator watchdog previously retried a long failed restart on every short monitor interval. Failed restarts now enter a 30-second cooldown, preserving responsiveness to window closure, host sync, and Antigravity Desktop recovery during persistent faults.
- Antigravity may ignore a prompt-mediated web tool request even when the tool definition is present. Explicit URL fetch requests therefore need a translator-owned deterministic prefetch and an evidence fallback.
- Web-specific system instructions must not appear in ordinary requests. Flash models can latch onto unrelated `web_search` wording and derail compaction/session-resume turns.
- Translator restart must tolerate both the dedicated translator executable and the Node fallback process. Runtime log truncation is best-effort and must never block gateway recovery.
- Antigravity can answer a direct compaction-resume request with either pseudo XML tool markup (`<tool_call><tool_name>web_search</tool_name>...`) or a missing-tool blocker while still echoing the marker. Marker presence alone is not enough to accept the answer as recall; compaction recall must reject pseudo tool markup and tool-unavailable detours, then return the saved summary deterministically.
- The installed runtime process does not hot-reload `translator\adaptive-server.mjs`. After deploying translator code, stop only the Anti-Codex runtime translator process and relaunch through `start-anticodex.ps1`; then verify the running process points at `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Anti-Codex\runtime\translator\adaptive-server.mjs`.
- A product-level sync affordance should not be another desktop helper icon. The safer current surface is Start Menu `Anti-Codex\Sync Anti-Codex with Codex.lnk`, which calls `sync-anticodex-launcher.ps1`; the launcher catches failures and the underlying sync script still performs host-only candidate build, manifest validation, maintenance marker handling, rollback preservation, and bounded restart.
- A one-shot UI check is not enough evidence for the user's reconnect complaint. The installed verifier now samples UI text and process identity repeatedly; it fails on reconnect/login text or PID churn, and it classifies "no Anti-Codex process" as the app being closed rather than a gateway reconnect loop.
- WebSocket support needs capture before implementation. The current safe step is to keep returning explicit `501 unsupported_websocket` while recording real Upgrade/prewarm attempts in diagnostics. This prevents guessed `101 Switching Protocols` behavior and gives future native WebSocket work exact path/header evidence from the installed client.
- The account-backed Antigravity Desktop Cascade path currently sends tools only as Antigravity-owned disablement config under `cascadeConfig.plannerConfig.toolConfig`; Codex external tools have no verified native schema field there. Keep `askPermission` and all Antigravity-owned local tools disabled to avoid double execution and permission popups, and expose Codex tools only through the audited bridge until a real native event protocol is captured.
- Compaction recall acceptance must check both the active marker and next-action terms. Marker-only recall is too weak because the model can drift into environment/status summaries while still echoing the marker; in that case the deterministic saved summary is safer.
- Recovery entry points must not assume the outer launcher set `TRANSLATOR_URL`. Directly invoking the installed runtime's `start-deepcodex.ps1 -TranslatorOnly` can happen from verifiers or manual repair flows, so the shared launcher now detects the installed Anti-Codex parent directory and defaults to the same 8484/private-address endpoint as `start-anticodex.ps1`.
- The current copied Codex host contains `responses_websockets` feature gates, an app-server `websocket_url` / `CODEX_APP_SERVER_WS_URL` marker, and `/codex/responses` HTTP/SSE usage. It does not contain verified native model Responses WebSocket evidence: no `supports_websockets` field, no `/v1/responses` literal, and the only file with both WebSocket and `/codex/responses` markers is the dictation/audio module. Keep the `501 unsupported_websocket` contract until this audit changes or the real frame protocol is captured.
- PowerShell scripts should not rely on `$PSScriptRoot` inside parameter default expressions for installed direct execution. Resolve script-relative defaults after the `param` block so manual runs and wrapper-invoked runs behave the same.
- Antigravity Desktop Cascade can HTTP-accept `customAgentSpec` and schema-like fields without proving native tool support. The live probe produced only `CORTEX_STEP_TYPE_USER_INPUT` and `CORTEX_STEP_TYPE_CONVERSATION_HISTORY` steps for name-only, schema-object, and OpenAI-style tool arrays; no native-looking tool event appeared. Treat field acceptance as inconclusive/ignored unless a trajectory contains real tool event steps and a result-return protocol.
- The installed gateway recovered from the observed Google-side `EOF` condition after deployment of bounded retries. The next account/proxy check, real tool-loop check, all-eight-model route check, and full installed overall verification passed without reconnect text or process churn.
- Retry diagnosis must not copy raw exception text because upstream errors could eventually include sensitive request details. The installed diagnostics therefore record only a coarse reason (`eof`, `proxy`, `desktop_service`, `timeout`, `connection`, `gateway`, `plan_generation`, or `transient`) plus bounded numeric metadata.
- Codex package version is the fast update signal; source host and CLI SHA-256 fingerprints are the authoritative drift proof. When the package version is unchanged and the prior result was up to date, startup can safely reuse the cached result and avoid repeatedly hashing large binaries.
- A host manifest that only records patched output hashes proves internal integrity but not provenance. New manifests also record the source Codex package version and original host/CLI fingerprints, and candidate validation checks those fingerprints against the copied source files before activation.
- Earlier conclusions that account-backed Cascade could not accept external custom tools are obsolete. `CustomAgentSpec` is accepted on `StartCascade`, and `customizationConfig.customizationServerUrl` causes Antigravity to perform native MCP `initialize` and `tools/list`.
- The decisive native evidence is a structured trajectory step with type `CORTEX_STEP_TYPE_MCP_TOOL`, status `CORTEX_STEP_STATUS_WAITING`, server `_custom`, an Antigravity-generated call ID, tool name, JSON arguments, and an MCP permission resource. This is not prompt XML.
- Antigravity's own MCP permission gate pauses before `tools/call`. Anti-Codex intentionally extracts the waiting event, returns it to Codex, and cancels the Cascade so the user sees only Codex's normal approval/execution flow.
- Non-streaming requests must delegate tool-bearing turns to the streamed native trajectory reader. Before this fix, non-streaming requests silently fell back to pseudo tool-call parsing even though the native MCP bridge was active.
- The native MCP permission boundary exposes one waiting call at a time. Stable multi-tool behavior is therefore serialized across Codex continuations; requiring two calls in one response tests the old prompt bridge rather than the native Antigravity path.
- Latest installed proof returned native single-tool call ID `9wfg8wkd` and distinct serialized multi-tool call IDs `81yenywi` and `xm8a3w4x`, all without `pseudo_` prefixes.
- The diagnostic collector should remain install-root restricted. Source-tree verification must call the installed collector instead of weakening that boundary; Windows PowerShell 5.1 also needs JSON arrays flattened after `ConvertFrom-Json` before reading report summaries.
- The current Codex WindowsApps package exposes one directory that is enumerable but not readable under `app.asar.unpacked`; recursive copies must explicitly exclude such phantom directories.
- Windows security scanning can deny repeated writes of top-level Electron DLL names during an independent PowerShell candidate build. Reliable sync copies the directory tree first, writes protected top-level DLLs through neutral temporary names, and promotes a verified staging host only after the copy is complete.
- A failure-safety drill must verify the intended injected failure text. Merely observing a nonzero candidate build can hide an earlier copy/setup failure and falsely claim transaction safety.
- A rollback is not successful merely because the directory swap and manifest validation succeeded. The rollback command must wait until both the Anti-Codex process and local gateway are healthy; otherwise it can return success while leaving the app offline.
- The safest rollback failure behavior is transactional recovery: retain the pre-rollback active host, attempt the requested rollback once, and automatically swap back if the rolled-back host cannot become ready within the bounded health window.
- Account-switch workflows cannot claim identity change from a healthy refresh alone. They must compare an anonymous account fingerprint or an observable catalog change, and report an unchanged result honestly.
- Windows icon changes must be deployed through host sync or an equivalent manifest update. Copying `resources\icon.ico` alone is not enough because the executable resource, app-root icon, active marker hashes, rollback marker hashes, desktop shortcut, Start Menu shortcuts, and shell icon cache can drift independently.
- `accountReady:true` can mean "cached model catalog is available" in no-traffic mode; it is not proof that live Antigravity account traffic is working. The real account/proxy verifier must inspect account status, refresh, real marker request, route audit, and Antigravity logs before calling the account/proxy path ready.
- Current external failure pattern: Antigravity Desktop has eight cached visible models, but account status times out, refresh can return an empty model list, real requests can abort, and logs show OAuth/general network failures. Diagnose this as Antigravity-to-Google proxy/OAuth trouble, not an Anti-Codex local gateway reconnect failure.
- Runtime health and diagnostic scripts have different evidence levels. `/health` can truthfully report cached model catalog plus the last live traffic error, but it does not inspect Antigravity Desktop logs. `test-anticodex-account-proxy-readiness.ps1` is the stronger account/proxy diagnostic because it combines account status, refresh, real request, route audit, and fresh Antigravity log signals.
- Manager button verification must not query action buttons immediately after opening the embedded panel. The panel can be present while local status is still rendering, so the verifier must select the visible Anti-Codex CDP page context, wait for `button[data-action=...]` plus the action bridge, then click and wait for an action-specific result.
- Windows PowerShell can misdecode direct Node UTF-8 JSON output before `ConvertFrom-Json`. Manager button verification now emits ASCII JSON with `\u` escapes, so reports preserve Chinese labels/results after PowerShell parses them.
- Fast no-traffic overall checks are weaker evidence than the full installed overall gate. They must write `latest-installed-overall-fast.json` and history `*-installed-overall-fast.json`, leaving `latest-installed-overall.json` reserved for the full gate so daily fast checks cannot overwrite release-grade proof.
- Diagnostic bundles should summarize both `latest-installed-overall.json` and `latest-installed-overall-fast.json`; otherwise the bundle loses the distinction between release-grade evidence and daily fast-check evidence.
- The in-app Manager snapshot is an outage fallback, not a freshness source. Live local endpoint reads must be attempted first or recently created reports and account state can appear stale in the embedded panel.
- Antigravity quota/account/proxy failures should be visible from the main Anti-Codex window, not only inside Manager. The current v39 notice is state-keyed, clickable, and removed after recovery.
- Account-switch automation must not infer success merely because Antigravity Desktop opened. The current in-app flow requires a changed anonymous account fingerprint or changed sorted model-ID set; otherwise it times out to an explicit manual refresh fallback.
- Update visibility belongs on the main Anti-Codex surface, but it must not hide a more urgent account-limit condition. The Manager badge therefore uses the fixed priority `quota > update > normal` and still opens the guarded sync surface instead of replacing the host directly.
- Installed v45 verification is stronger than source-only proof: after deploying v45 runtime files and restarting only the local translator, `/backend-api/wham/apps` GET returned 1494 directory connectors and 0 callable tools, matching the current authorization state. Installed UI verification passed with embedded Manager `native-panel-v45`, no separate manager page, no ChatGPT login/reconnect text, no endpoint fetch failure, eight visible exact-version models, local Skill 100 / MCP 3, and Anti-Codex synced to Codex package `26.616.3309.0` / `codex-cli 0.142.0-alpha.1`.
- Installed overall checks previously could look frozen because PowerShell subchecks were launched with an unbounded wait. The verifier now has a per-subcheck timeout and only stops the child verifier process it started; it does not stop Anti-Codex, Antigravity, or the translator. Fast no-traffic overall passed in about 19.1 seconds after deployment.
- The Electron tray icon is a separate host resource at `resources\codex-tray.ico`; changing only `resources\icon.ico`, the executable icon, or shortcuts can leave the Windows notification-area icon white. Install, sync, candidate validation, active marker hashes, rollback marker hashes, and sync readiness now include `trayIcon`, and the active/rollback tray palettes are verified as the pink Anti-Codex icon.
- Installed Manager `native-panel-v50` treats `/backend-api/wham/apps` as optional real app-tool evidence. If the endpoint is temporarily unreadable and no connector is authorized, the panel keeps showing honest `0` callable app tools without surfacing raw `Failed to fetch`; if authorized connectors exist, it shows a Chinese supplemental warning instead.
- A direct installed overall check without `-EnsureRunning` is allowed to fail when Anti-Codex is closed; that is not the same as a gateway reconnect loop. Daily/Manager fast checks should use `-EnsureRunning`, while direct no-autostart failures now include an explicit "start Anti-Codex first or rerun with -EnsureRunning" hint.
- Manager button checks share one embedded panel and must not treat arbitrary finished text as the current action's result. The verifier now uses a process lock plus `lastAction`, empty queue, and empty pending-count checks so parallel smoke tests cannot cross-read MCP/Skill versus plugin/application results.
- Manager action summaries must include durable evidence, not just "passed" or "failed". For installed overall checks and diagnostic bundles, the embedded panel should show the report name plus latest/history report paths, and include bounded failure items when present.
- Overall checks launched from inside the embedded Manager must not recursively run the Manager UI-state probe. Use `-SkipUiStateProbe` for Manager-triggered fast/full overall actions, while keeping the normal UI-state probe enabled for external/direct overall verification.
- Keep Manager result summaries consistent across all entry points: injected in-app panel, CDP bridge, and backend `/anticodex/manager` page. If one path shows report evidence while another only shows a broad pass/fail line, diagnostics will look fake even when the underlying check is real.
- Manager watcher reinjection must be idempotent. A pre-injection cleanup that removes `#anticodex-manager-overlay` can close the embedded panel shortly after the user opens it because the manager watcher loops in the background. Let `anticodex-manager-inject.js` handle stale-version cleanup itself; repeated same-version injections should return without touching the open overlay. Timeout/background action summaries also need to update `__ANTICODEX_MANAGER_LAST_ACTION_RESULT__` so button verifiers and user-visible status do not read a stale previous action.
- Embedded Manager verifiers mutate one shared in-app panel. Any verifier that opens, closes, or clicks Manager actions must share the same lock as `test-anticodex-manager-button.mjs`; otherwise parallel no-traffic checks can cross-read stale action state and produce false failures even when the product UI is healthy.
- Manager action results should not preserve stale failures across reopen. Success and pending summaries are useful context, but an old `ok:false` bridge-timeout result should be replaced by fresh status on the next panel load. Also, once an action has timed out into background/pending mode, repeated clicks for that same action should be blocked until the late bridge result arrives or the pending TTL expires; otherwise users and verifiers can enqueue duplicate long tasks.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` writes JSON files with a BOM, which can make direct Node `JSON.parse(readFileSync(..., "utf8"))` fail on installed reports. Core Anti-Codex report writers should use `[System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false)` and `WriteAllText` so Manager/report tooling can read evidence without special-case stripping.
- Compaction resume cannot accept any response that merely preserves the marker while changing the next action or inventing a scheduled/background task. Context summaries often put `Continuation Instruction:` on one line and the actual action on the next line, so the recall guard must extract next-line terms and require them in the restored answer.
## 2026-06-19 JSON Writer Encoding
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 `Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` writes JSON files with a BOM, which can make direct Node readers fail unless they strip the prefix. Treat every machine-read Anti-Codex JSON writer as explicit UTF-8 without BOM using `[System.Text.UTF8Encoding]::new($false)` and `WriteAllText`; plain logs/text do not need this rule.
- A verifier must distinguish fresh evidence from legacy evidence. Failing the daily fast check for an old report creates a false product failure; the correct contract is strict failure for reports written during the current run, plus a visible legacy issue list for older files.
- In PowerShell, assigning an empty generic list through an `if` expression can yield `$null` because the empty collection emits no pipeline output. Add directly to the selected collection inside each branch instead of assigning the branch result.
- Native model metadata needs a separate no-traffic regression gate in addition to UI text checks. Exact model IDs, display names, reasoning labels, thinking support, budgets, and Gemini minimum budgets are now verified directly from `/v1/models` and included in the fast installed overall check.
- Antigravity omits `min_thinking_budget` for Claude Thinking and GPT-OSS models instead of serializing it as JSON `null`; the verifier accepts omission only for those three exact routes and rejects any invented non-null minimum.
- In-app traffic alerts must distinguish a confirmed healthy snapshot from a failed health read. `safeJson` converts fetch exceptions into `{ ok:false }`, so relying on an outer `catch` still allowed empty fallback data to remove the alert; the notification update is now gated on `healthResult.ok`.
- Traffic alert dismissal is keyed by diagnosis plus `accountTrafficLastFailureAt` (falling back to the message). This prevents a 30-second poll from reopening the same dismissed warning while still allowing a later quota or proxy failure to notify again.
- Host process plus gateway health is necessary but insufficient evidence after a Codex host sync. The guarded sync now treats the installed fast no-traffic overall verifier as an acceptance test and rolls back when UI, branding, auth isolation, model metadata, local features, or protocol compatibility regress.
- Windows PowerShell `Start-Process -PassThru` may expose a null `ExitCode` after `WaitForExit(timeout)` when the process handle was never read before exit. Reading `$process.Handle` immediately after launch retains the handle and makes bounded child-process exit codes reliable.
- The `Local\AntiCodexHostSync` mutex must cover candidate build, activation, restart, acceptance, and automatic rollback. Releasing it immediately after file activation permits two user-triggered updates to overlap during the most important validation window.

## 2026-06-20 Anti-Codex Manager Watcher Stability
- Explicit `--watch` is now used to ensure manager watcher stability under various file systems.
- Watcher reuse and de-duplication mechanisms are active to prevent multiple instances from running for the same path.
- Watchers enforce bounded exit on disconnection to prevent orphaned processes from consuming resources.
- One-shot actions are guaranteed to terminate instead of turning into persistent resident processes.
- The idle stability verifier must count manager injection processes, not just the main app and translator. A stale no-`--watch` injector can keep running after a one-shot probe and make later Manager behavior look random; a duplicate official `--watch` injector can also fight the in-app panel. The verifier now records both lists and requires exactly one official watcher plus zero legacy watchers.
- PowerShell array splatting does not bind named parameters from strings like `"-InstallRoot"`; it passes them positionally. For child script calls that need named parameters, use hashtable splatting or direct named arguments. This caused fast sync readiness to hand `check-anticodex-update.ps1` shifted paths until the call was changed.
- Fast readiness checks should read trusted cached update status and skip icon-palette pixel scans. Full release checks still force fresh fingerprints and palette validation, but daily/interactive checks must stay comfortably under the 30-second progress rule.
- UI model evidence must include the embedded Manager panel, not only the compact main window body text. The main window can show just the currently selected model while the Manager contains the full Antigravity model catalog, plugin/MCP state, and thinking-budget evidence; checking only one surface creates false failures.
- Embedded Manager verification must not synthesize overlay success from surrounding page text. The verifier needs real overlay DOM evidence, real close/reopen evidence, and real action-specific result evidence; otherwise a closed or broken Manager panel can look healthy.
- Manager action checks must prove the visible button hit area, not just call the bridge function. Use a center-point `elementFromPoint` hit test before dispatching the click so layout regressions in the close/action buttons are caught.
- A verifier timeout should be a hard product contract. CDP status polling must use the remaining deadline for each page evaluation; otherwise an outer 30-second timeout can still hang behind an inner 8-second evaluation and feel like Anti-Codex is frozen.
- Manager script version bumps must update every static gate that checks the version, including sync readiness. A stale readiness assertion can turn a successful runtime deployment into a false "needs attention" result.
- When a pending Manager action completes after the panel was closed and reopened, the late result must update the currently connected result node, not the stale DOM node captured by the original click handler. Internal `refreshStatus` calls must be suppressible so they do not overwrite the late action's visible summary.

## 2026-06-20 Local Snapshot Sync Consistency Hardening
- Added `computeSnapshotManifest` to both `sync-shared-codex-plugin-host.mjs` and `sync-shared-codex-sidecars.mjs`. It recursively scans the shared source and records type, SHA-256 partial hash (16 hex chars), and mtime for each file/directory.
- Added `pruneStaleManifestEntries` to remove from the local snapshot any entry that existed in the previous source manifest but is no longer present in the current shared source. Local-only entries (not tracked by either manifest) are preserved.
- Added `trackManifest` support to `syncDirectoryTransactional`. After a successful directory swap, the new source manifest is written to `.anticodex-source-manifest.json` inside the target directory, and stale entries from the old manifest are pruned.
- The startup snapshot marker (`writeStartupSnapshotMarker`) now includes `sourceFingerprint` (32 hex chars from SHA-256 over sorted source file paths + hashes) and `sourceMtimeMax` (ISO date of the newest source file mtime). This binds the marker to the exact source content, so a later process can detect whether the shared source has changed since the last snapshot.
- Tests confirm: deleted shared skills are removed from the local snapshot on the next sync, local-only entries survive pruning, and the marker fingerprint changes when source content changes while remaining stable when source is unchanged.
- Sidecar sync (`sync-shared-codex-sidecars.mjs`) also tracks source manifests and prunes stale entries per plugin directory.
## 2026-06-20 Manager Page Verification
- A healthy Manager action endpoint is not sufficient proof that the backend page is usable. The verifier must also assert the page's real button inventory, click binding, busy disable/re-enable behavior, result target, Chinese product notice, and embedded-only navigation policy.
- Keep destructive or traffic-consuming action names out of the executable action probe. Their presence is covered by UI-state/source gates; the live Manager action probe should remain on a safe local action such as `refreshStatus`.

## 2026-06-20 Native Bridge Process Inheritance
- Updating launcher files does not change environment variables already captured by a long-running desktop monitor. Its watchdog children inherit the monitor's original values.
- Formal Anti-Codex entry and recovery paths must set the native bridge value explicitly before process creation. A missing-value default inside the translator is not enough to override a stale explicit `0`.
- A full monitor restart is required once after changing this startup contract; translator-only recovery cannot repair the parent monitor's inherited environment.
- Cold relaunch readiness should not use an 18-second hard failure on this host. The application became healthy at about 23 seconds; short staged probes correctly showed that the launch succeeded and avoided another long blocking wait.

## 2026-06-20 Manager Risk Grouping
- User-facing risk is broader than destructive data loss. Actions that restart/sync host state, write local feature snapshots, run long blocking checks, or consume real model traffic must be visually separated from routine status and refresh actions.
- The Antigravity account-switch entry is safe in Anti-Codex because it opens Antigravity for manual switching and observes for a change; it does not log the account out by itself.
- Current v60 injected Manager renders all 14 actions in one flat pill row; only the real-traffic action is red. The backend fallback page uses the same flat-row pattern and does not expose the exact same action inventory.
- The redesign needs an explicit action-group contract rather than CSS selectors based on individual action names. Static and live UI tests should prove group membership and one-time rendering.
- The backend action router already supports `accountSwitch`; the fallback page simply omitted its button. The redesign can make the two Manager surfaces consistent without adding a new backend capability.
- Existing launcher tests already extract the backend HTML template and inspect injected Manager source, so the new risk contract can be added to the focused 75-test suite before production edits.
- `native-panel-v61` now enforces the same exact inventory in both Manager surfaces: 9 routine actions in the neutral first group and 5 high-impact actions in the dark-red second group. `accountSwitch` stays routine because it only opens/focuses Antigravity for manual switching and observes the account/model change.
- Live computed styles for all five high-impact buttons are `rgb(75, 34, 40)`. The first live verifier false-failure came from a missing escape layer in a regular expression embedded inside a page-evaluation template, not from the page CSS.
- The translator recovery verifier still waits up to 60 seconds for its launcher wrapper even after `/health` is already green. It hit the 30-second outer limit during this deployment while the gateway itself was healthy with eight models; this verifier timeout contract needs separate hardening.

## 2026-06-20 Translator Recovery Timeout
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 `WaitForExit(ms)` was not a reliable bounded wait on this host. A sleeping child process reproduced the same behavior: the call did not return at the requested 12-second deadline.
- `Start-Process` without stdout/stderr redirection made the translator-only launcher much slower and opaque. Running the same launcher with redirected stdout/stderr returned in about 8.6 seconds and printed the expected ready line.
- Recovery verifiers should use explicit child stdout/stderr files, short tails in failure messages, `HasExited` polling, and phase timings. This turns "looks frozen" into a specific slow phase.
- Installed proof after the fix: translator recovery passed in about 9.4 seconds with `accountReady:true`, `modelCount:8`, and `originalCodexConfigUnchanged:true`.
- Worker dispatch proof: the first broad Antigravity documentation task failed with no file, then the narrower single-file task succeeded. This confirms the new rule: failure should trigger a smaller re-dispatch before giving up on MCP.

## 2026-06-20 Child Process and Script Encoding
- Any user-facing or launcher-facing Windows PowerShell 5.1 script that waits for a child process should avoid `WaitForExit(timeout)`. Use a `HasExited` polling helper after reading the process handle, then kill and poll briefly on timeout.
- This rule now covers the main launcher CDP injections, the Start Menu manager gateway recovery and script actions, host-sync post-acceptance, translator recovery, and installed overall subchecks.
- PowerShell scripts that are executed directly by Windows PowerShell 5.1 and contain Chinese UI strings need UTF-8 with BOM. `manage-anticodex.ps1` without BOM was parsed as mojibake and failed before any UI/status logic could run.
- JSON/report files remain UTF-8 without BOM for Node readers; this encoding rule is only for direct PowerShell script source with non-ASCII UI strings.
- Installed proof: `manage-anticodex.ps1 -Action Status` now returns Chinese status lines, gateway ready, account ready, and eight models instead of parser errors.

## 2026-06-20 Third-Party Upstream Safety
- A successful HTTP status is not enough to prove a model provider is usable. The connection test must validate the minimum chat response shape, parse exact model IDs, and separately check streaming/tool-call capability evidence.
- Basic chat failure should block saving a provider profile. Missing streaming or tool-call evidence should be surfaced as a warning, because some OpenAI-compatible gateways can still be useful for chat but are not full Codex parity.
- The server must rerun the connection test at save time. Client-provided `stages` are display evidence only; accepting them as authority allows stale or forged tests to activate a broken upstream.
- Provider/model switching must not move `DEEPCODEX_STATE_ROOT`, `CODEX_HOME_DIR`, project directories, or response history. The only thing that should restart is the local model gateway.
- For Manager forms that gate a save on a prior test, every editable field must invalidate the test result. Otherwise users can test one endpoint, edit the model/key, and save a different untested configuration.
- Worker lesson: do not give a nominally read-only task to a worker with edit permissions. DeepSeek edited `scripts/anticodex-manager-inject.js` during a review task; the change was reviewed and tests passed, but future review-only worker tasks should use a mode that cannot write.

## 2026-06-20 Local Feature Truthfulness
- A local connector/app cache is not equivalent to an active callable tool. The model prompt, diagnostics, and Manager should all phrase connector/app snapshots as metadata unless the current host exposes real app tools.
- Skills and MCP servers are different from remote app connectors: local Skill/MCP inventory can be surfaced as available Anti-Codex local context, but actual MCP execution still depends on the host exposing the corresponding MCP tools in the current tool list.
- The system prompt should tell the model about local Skill/MCP inventory to reduce false "not supported" answers, while still forbidding invented MCP/plugin calls.

## 2026-06-20 Third-Party Upstream Local Action Safety
- Treat third-party provider tests as authenticated Manager actions, not harmless reads. If the test endpoint can reuse the saved encrypted key, an unauthenticated localhost request can consume third-party traffic or produce confusing provider errors.
- The correct contract is: no `x-anticodex-manager-token` means 403 before reading the body, before loading a saved key, and before contacting any upstream provider.
- Manager UI tests must prove both halves: the in-app test button sends the token, and unauthorized direct POSTs do not reach the provider.
- Manager token issuance is also a local action surface. `/anticodex/manager/bootstrap` and JSONP `/anticodex/manager/action-script` must reject untrusted browser origins/referrers; otherwise a random web page could try to obtain or spend the token through the local gateway.
- Health payload tests should validate stable contract fields and then explicitly assert additive summaries such as `upstreamProfile`. Whole-object equality against an older health shape turns legitimate diagnostics additions into false regressions.

## 2026-06-20 Embedded Upstream Config Verification
- A Manager entry is not enough proof that third-party API configuration is usable. The installed UI verifier must click `upstreamConfig` inside the embedded Manager and assert the real panel content, disabled-save state, warning text, and controls.
- Third-party provider configuration is a high-impact action because it writes provider state and can restart the local gateway. It belongs in the dark-red/high-impact group even though the test itself should not consume Antigravity traffic.
- The verifier should never click Test/Save/Restore during a read-only UI state check. It should only open the form and inspect safe local DOM state.
- An Antigravity worker that exits code 2 with no stdout/stderr and no file diff should not be retried with the same broad task. Prepare a smaller patch-shaped task or let the main agent finish the isolated change, then verify with source tests.

## 2026-06-20 Plugin Callable Boundary
- App-tool filtering needs two tests: accessible connectors must not lose their tools, and inaccessible connectors must never leak cached tools.
- A local connector allowlist is not real authorization. It would make the UI look richer while creating a false callable claim, so it is not an acceptable production shortcut.
- Plugin/connector inventory, locally verifiable configuration, and currently callable tools are separate tiers. Manager wording and diagnostics should keep those tiers explicit.
- Worker audits are advisory. The DeepSeek audit's local-allowlist recommendation was rejected and rewritten because it conflicted with the project's no-fake-capability foundation.

## 2026-06-20 Capability Tier Performance and Wording
- Capability classification must not add plugin-directory scans to every model request. Expensive projection counts belong in diagnostics/Manager requests; the model system block should keep a lightweight Skill/MCP/snapshot summary.
- "Configured MCP" is not the same as "callable MCP". The only honest runtime statement is that callability follows the current session tool list.
- "Installed plugin" is UI/cache terminology in this project, not proof of executable plugin runtime. Manager should label it as metadata inventory.
- Manager injection versions must change whenever injected behavior or wording changes. Otherwise an existing window can keep an old panel while readiness checks incorrectly treat it as current.

## 2026-06-20 Snapshot Freshness Contract
- Reuse the launcher's existing startup snapshot marker and 24-hour maximum age instead of inventing another staleness policy.
- Marker age can prove that a snapshot is old, missing, or malformed. It does not by itself prove the shared source changed immediately after a fresh sync, so the UI should call this "freshness", not exact source equality.
- Snapshot status is advisory and local. It should never restart the model gateway or interrupt the current conversation; the user can trigger the existing local-feature refresh action.

## 2026-06-20 Persistent Update Operation Contract
- Update detection and update execution are separate facts. `updateNeeded` cannot represent candidate build, validation, activation, failure, or rollback; those states need a persistent `operation` object.
- Atomic file replacement prevents partial JSON but does not prevent a stale writer from overwriting a newer stage. All update-status writers must share the `Local\AntiCodexUpdateStatus` mutex and merge the latest operation while holding it.
- Status persistence is observability, not the update mechanism. A status write or lock timeout must warn without blocking candidate validation, activation, recovery, or mutex release for the real host update.
- A terminal result is truthful only after post-sync acceptance. Activation alone is still `running`; acceptance failure followed by successful recovery is `rolled_back`; recovery failure is `failed`.
- Manager must evaluate operation state before `updateNeeded`. Otherwise a failed check, active sync, failed sync, or rollback can all be misreported as "already latest".
- Worker failure learning is now durable in `MCP_WORKER_RULES.md`: classify the concrete cause, change the retry shape, and do not silently move the same task back to the main agent after the first failure.
- Final source proof for this batch: 581 total tests / 547 passed / 34 skipped / 0 failed, plus parser checks for every affected update, rollback, installer, readiness, Manager, and diagnostics file.
- An injected Manager version bump must clear global timers created by the previous version before installing new timers. Reusing `__ANTICODEX_MANAGER_STATUS_TIMER__` without clearing it leaves the old interval active and makes the new refresh cadence ineffective.

## 2026-06-20 Real MCP and Shortcut Truth
- The previous MCP verifier only proved that the model emitted a flattened `mcp__node_repl__js` call; it then supplied a synthetic `$marker=42` result. That was not proof that the configured MCP server could actually start or execute a tool.
- Added a real stdio MCP client and smoke verifier that asks the installed Codex CLI for `node_repl`, performs `initialize`, `tools/list`, and `tools/call`, and requires the returned marker before reporting success.
- `test-anticodex-mcp-skills.ps1` now distinguishes configured MCP entries from a real `node_repl` tool call. `test-anticodex-mcp-tool-loop.ps1` now feeds the assistant the real local MCP output instead of a handwritten fake result.
- Desktop shortcut diagnosis on the installed machine showed `C:\Users\XD\Desktop\Anti-Codex.lnk` points to PowerShell with `runtime\scripts\start-anticodex.ps1`, and that launch path successfully started Anti-Codex with gateway health `ok:true`, eight models, and `antigravity-desktop`.
- The installed fast sync-readiness failure after that launch was not a shortcut failure. It reported stale `update-candidate`, manager injection version drift, and installed Codex drift requiring Anti-Codex sync.
