- Source readiness checks now verify that the Start Menu and desktop launch shortcuts target PowerShell, pass the installed runtime `start-anticodex.ps1`, and expose the target/arguments in the report so shortcut drift is visible instead of guessed.

## 2026-06-20 Manager v68 Sync Failure Clarity
- Manager injection moved from `native-panel-v67` to `native-panel-v68` after the sync-readiness result contract changed.
- Embedded Manager and the fallback Manager bridge now include the first sync-readiness failure items in the visible result text instead of only saying `需要处理`.
- This specifically addresses installed/source drift cases such as stale update candidates, shortcut target drift, or Manager version drift: the user should see the concrete failing checks directly in Anti-Codex.

## 2026-06-21 Manager v69 Shortcut Repair
- Shortcut drift is now treated as a repairable local UX issue, not something the user has to diagnose manually. Anti-Codex has a dedicated `repairShortcuts` Manager action that rebuilds only the Anti-Codex desktop and Start Menu shortcuts.
- The repair script writes only `.lnk` shortcut metadata, points launchers at the installed runtime scripts, uses the verified Anti-Codex icon assets, and avoids host sync, recursive delete, model traffic, or ChatGPT login paths.
- The fallback Manager page should keep every daily action in the static HTML inventory. Dynamically inserting one action after the generic click binding works, but it is too easy for tests and live behavior to drift.
- A shortcut repair action should leave durable evidence. The latest repair summary now lives in the standard reports directory and is surfaced by diagnostics, Manager local checks, and diagnostic bundles.
- Repair actions should report partial environment problems as data whenever possible. Missing installed runtime targets now become a failed `shortcut-repair` report, not just a thrown script exception.
- Manager injection moved to `native-panel-v69` because the embedded action inventory changed. Opener, sync-readiness, UI-state, and launcher gates now reject stale v68 injection.
- Worker note: DeepSeek docs-only review misread the dirty worktree as policy violations; Antigravity timed out on broad, tiny, and read-only audit tasks. The failures were recorded in `MCP_WORKER_RULES.md`, and source verification was completed by the main line.
## 2026-06-21 Manager and Shortcut Hardening
- The embedded Manager close button must use the real button box as the visual hit target. Pseudo-element-only button backgrounds can make the visible target and clickable target drift; the verifier now checks the center plus four inner corner points.
- Manager actions must remain embedded in the Anti-Codex window. Source tests now reject a separate `/anticodex/manager` page target and reject iframe/new-page regressions.
- Shortcut repair and sync-readiness should share the same evidence shape: display name, expected script, expected icon, actual target, actual arguments, actual icon, and boolean match fields. This makes wrong desktop shortcuts and wrong icons visible in diagnostics instead of becoming a vague launch failure.
- Manager version strings are part of the stability contract. `native-panel-v69` must stay synchronized across injection, opener, readiness, Manager entry, and tests; stale v56/v68 references are treated as regressions.

## 2026-06-21 Manager Surface Consistency
- Embedded Manager and fallback Manager must have the same formal action inventory for user-visible actions. A missing fallback button is still a product bug even when the embedded path is the preferred one, because recovery and diagnostics may expose the fallback surface.
- Third-party API configuration is high-impact because it writes provider profile state and can switch the model gateway. It belongs with other muted-red actions even though the configuration panel itself should not clear history or call Antigravity.
- Provider switching is not session switching. `DEEPCODEX_STATE_ROOT`, response history, project state, and `previous_response_id` continuity must survive save, apply, and restore transitions between third-party and Antigravity modes.
- Fallback Manager actions that cannot safely host the full form should return a precise embedded-Manager instruction instead of opening a separate web UI or pretending the action completed.

## 2026-06-21 Parallel Worker Discipline
- In a dirty long-lived workspace, parallel coding workers should use forked workspaces or strictly disjoint write scopes. Otherwise one worker can mistake another worker's diff for its own result.
- Read-only review tasks for Antigravity need a concrete artifact or machine-verifiable output; empty success output is not useful work.
- The main agent must keep a green source-regression checkpoint before integrating each larger subagent batch.

## 2026-06-21 Local Snapshot Only Boundary
- Anti-Codex must not silently fall back to the user's native/shared Codex connector, app-tool, or plugin caches at runtime. Local snapshots are the isolation boundary; if they are missing or stale, the UI should say the capability is degraded and offer refresh/sync, not fake inherited callability.
- Tests should cover both halves of this rule: local Anti-Codex snapshots are usable when present, and shared-only Codex caches are ignored when the local snapshot is absent.
- Local connector projection should not expose ChatGPT install URLs. A clickable ChatGPT app authorization path in Anti-Codex would violate the no-ChatGPT-login product promise.

## 2026-06-21 Shortcut Target Evidence
- Windows `.lnk` target paths can correctly point to `powershell.exe`; the real product launcher is the `-File` argument. Shortcut diagnostics must parse and report that launcher target explicitly.
- Shortcut repair and sync-readiness reports should use the same fields for human diagnosis: expected launcher, actual launcher, launcher match, expected icon, actual icon, and icon match.
- Desktop icon assets need separate verification from Start Menu/taskbar/tray icon assets because Windows may render different ICO frames in those surfaces.

## 2026-06-21 Update UX Guardrails
- Version drift is a user-facing state, not just a hash mismatch. Manager/status output should say whether Anti-Codex is synced, behind native Codex, syncing, failed, or rolled back.
- Host sync must keep explicit brand/icon protection in its reported guardrails. A Codex host refresh must not overwrite Anti-Codex identity, icon assets, no-ChatGPT-login routing, or Antigravity model path.

## 2026-06-21 Cached Status, Rollback, and Shortcut Residue
- Cached update status is only an acceleration hint. It must never bypass active host manifest/hash/brand/icon validation, because a stale JSON file can otherwise hide a broken or partially overwritten Anti-Codex host.
- Rollback hosts must be validated like candidate hosts. A rollback point without tray icon, source hash, FileDescription, OriginalFilename, or critical patch evidence is not safe to activate.
- Shortcut repair should remove or report stale user-facing launch entries, not only recreate the expected Anti-Codex shortcuts. Residual `Codex.lnk`, `Codex Desktop.lnk`, or `DeepCodex*.lnk` entries can make the product look broken even when the runtime is healthy.
- Parallel agents must be closed after completion. Completed agents still occupy capacity, and leaving them open blocks the final review pass.

## 2026-06-21 Final Guardrail Review Lessons
- A self-consistent manifest is not enough evidence. Update and rollback safety must verify both signed/recorded metadata and actual runtime artifacts: source fingerprints, icon contents, executable identity, and app package patch markers.
- Shortcut cleanup must distinguish "foreign user shortcut" from "our stale shortcut". Names like `Codex Desktop.lnk` are not ownership proof on the desktop; deletion needs target/argument/description evidence.
- String-contract tests are useful as cheap guards, but at least one bad-sample behavior test is needed for each safety claim that can be falsified by a malformed manifest or stale state file.

## 2026-06-21 Stability Findings

- High-impact Manager actions need a real interaction contract, not only red styling. The contract is now: first click confirms, second click runs; tests cover both embedded and fallback surfaces.
- The local Manager token is not enough as a boundary if bootstrap accepts headerless local HTTP. Empty source headers are now rejected; this reduces accidental or bare local-process triggering of sync/post-verification actions.
- Third-party API mode must not trust stale model ids from old Antigravity sessions. Known Antigravity model ids are normalized to the configured third-party model so chats keep the same history while switching providers.
- Connector link endpoints are metadata-only in Anti-Codex. Returning success-shaped link payloads creates false confidence, so they now report unsupported explicitly.
- Response history eviction must be user-visible. Silent hydrate miss looked like the chat continued but actually dropped context; now it returns a 410-style expired-history error.

## 2026-06-21 Plugin Projection Closeout

- Local plugin projection now honors `[plugins."name@market"] enabled = false` from Anti-Codex `config.toml`.
- Plugin list and installed-plugin endpoints keep the plugin visible as installed, but expose `enabled:false` instead of falsely reporting it active.
- Verified with local projection, pseudo-tool, and launcher plugin-focused tests.

## 2026-06-21 Startup Snapshot and Plugin Unlock Findings

- Startup snapshot freshness must be source-bound, not only age-bound. Anti-Codex now recomputes the shared Codex source fingerprint before trusting the marker, so changed skills/MCP guidance/cache snapshots trigger a refresh even inside the 24-hour window.
- `DEEPCODEX_SKIP_PLUGIN_SYNC=1` should mean "skip slow plugin marketplace/sidecar sync", not "skip lightweight local feature freshness". Translator-only recovery now keeps the lightweight snapshot check before gateway start.
- The plugin UI unlock injection is not a production plugin path because it spoofs ChatGPT UI auth state. It is now guarded as unsafe debug-only, while production plugin behavior stays on local projection, Skills, MCP, and real connector authorization boundaries.
- Diagnostics and Manager must use the same source-bound freshness contract as the launcher. A fresh marker with a mismatched shared-source fingerprint now reports `state:"stale"` and `staleReason:"source_changed"` instead of misleading the UI as fresh.

## 2026-06-21 Manager Chinese Text Guardrail

- A static "contains CJK" assertion is too weak for Anti-Codex Manager surfaces: mojibake Chinese also contains CJK codepoints and can pass while the UI is unreadable.
- Fallback Manager and embedded Manager coverage should assert exact readable labels for key actions such as `Anti-Codex 管理`, `刷新状态`, `检查插件/应用`, `配置第三方 API`, and `快速检查（不耗流量）`, then reject known mojibake fragments.
- Runtime UI checks must scan rendered text, not only source templates. The UI verifier now fails if the main page or embedded Manager text contains known mojibake fragments.
- Third-party API configuration safety text is part of the product contract: test connection does not save config or switch the current session; saving does not clear chat/project/workspace state; third-party failures must not silently consume Antigravity traffic.

## 2026-06-21 Source Preflight and Capability Findings
- Install/sync updates need a source gate before host copying. A source-only preflight now blocks installation or sync candidate creation when script syntax, JS syntax, UI self-test, source regression, or whitespace checks fail.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 compatibility matters for every product verifier. Avoid PowerShell 7-only helpers such as [IO.Path]::GetRelativePath and compute report fields before constructing pscustomobject payloads.
- App-tool callability must be connector-attributed. A tool without connector_id, or with a connector_id outside the accessible connector set, is a fake callable capability and must be rejected.
- /codex/responses is part of the Responses boundary. WebSocket Upgrade attempts there must fail with the same explicit unsupported_websocket fallback and diagnostics evidence as /v1/responses.
- Worker delegation must get narrower after failure. Empty Antigravity output and DeepSeek contract failure are not usable results; the next worker task should be single-file or single-question with a concrete final artifact.
- Diagnostic bundles should carry source-gate evidence, not just runtime evidence. `latest-source-preflight.json` is now part of the reports summary so a support bundle can prove whether the source passed JavaScript syntax, PowerShell syntax, UI state self-test, optional translator regression, and diff-whitespace checks before install or sync.
- A diagnostic bundle entry that only says a report exists is too weak. The collector now keeps source-preflight summary booleans, and the diagnostics verifier checks them when bundle verification is enabled.
- Source preflight is useful enough to be a Manager daily action. It is low risk because it only runs local syntax/UI-state checks and writes reports; it should not sit in the high-impact group with sync, third-party API changes, or real-traffic post-verification.
- Third-party provider mode must fail closed. If a saved profile or encrypted key is unreadable, Anti-Codex must show an error and must not silently consume Antigravity traffic.
- A healthy translator is not necessarily the configured translator. The launcher must compare active mode, protocol, URL, and model before reusing an existing process.
- Native third-party streaming must persist the completed response before closing the event stream so `previous_response_id` remains valid.
- Third-party error bodies are untrusted and may echo credentials; redact configured keys, bearer tokens, and credential query values before UI responses or debug summaries.
- Fail-closed must still leave an escape hatch: an unreadable third-party key blocks third-party runtime, but explicitly restoring Antigravity must not require decrypting that stale key.
- Cached Manager snapshots are read-only fallbacks. POST actions such as test/save/restore/apply must never treat a startup snapshot as a successful live response.
- Source-preflight evidence must include mode as well as result. `skipFullRegression` tells whether the preflight was fast or full, so diagnostics and bundles now treat it as a required field alongside `uiStateSelfTestOk`, JavaScript, PowerShell, translator regression, and diff-whitespace checks.
- Third-party provider mode must be a first-class verification mode. MCP/Skill, plugin, installed-overall, and diagnostics checks now read the active upstream profile; Antigravity-only UI catalog, native bridge, account/proxy, and thinking-budget checks are marked not applicable instead of failing third-party runs.
- Diagnostics must report the active provider model, not a stale Antigravity catalog. The provider-native bridge is validated as `provider-native-or-compat` over `upstream-provider`, while the original Antigravity native-bridge contract remains intact.
- Third-party provider mode must still run UI and Manager verification. It may skip Antigravity catalog-only and native thinking-budget assertions, but it must still prove the app title, no ChatGPT login, no reconnect banner, embedded Manager, third-party API panel, high-impact confirmations, plugin/Skill/MCP summaries, and current provider model evidence.
- Third-party provider mode must also run streaming cancellation verification. Cancellation readiness is generic provider behavior, not an Antigravity-only check; the verifier now resolves the active mode/model from `/health` and records `providerReady` while preserving `accountReady` evidence for Antigravity runs.
- Optional real-traffic stability checks are also provider-generic. Stability soak and cold relaunch now resolve the active upstream mode/model from `/health`, validate third-party readiness through `upstreamProfile.protocol/model`, and keep Antigravity `accountReady` only as Antigravity-specific evidence instead of a universal gate.
- Third-party provider mode must also verify model routing. The local route audit now records provider transport, provider protocol, requested model, and resolved model for third-party calls; installed overall no longer skips model-routing just because the active upstream is not Antigravity.
- Route audit naming must not imply an Antigravity-only transport once third-party provider mode is supported. The audit object is now `anticodex.model_route_audit`, with `legacyObject:"antigravity.model_route_audit"` kept only for backward compatibility.
- 2026-06-21 verification: upstream integration 2/2, local access 47/47, launcher contracts 82/82, four PowerShell parser checks, and full source preflight 608 total / 574 passed / 34 skipped / 0 failed. Installed deployment and live desktop checks remain deferred for the combined test pass.
- Manager route UI must be provider-neutral too. If the route display only shows Antigravity-style internal model fields, third-party mode looks fake even when the gateway is correctly using the configured provider.
- "Refresh models" is a mode-specific operation. In third-party mode it must not call Antigravity, consume Antigravity traffic, or force a restart fallback; it should report the configured provider model and mark the Antigravity refresh as skipped.
- Third-party API URL inputs should tolerate what users actually paste. OpenAI-compatible full `/chat/completions` endpoints and Anthropic `/v1` or `/v1/messages` endpoints must be normalized before both connection testing and real chat calls.
- A real tool loop can still be falsely blocked if the final formatter only checks the original Responses input and ignores translator-owned internal tool results produced during the current upstream loop. Internal tool loops must mark `_internalToolEvidence`, and the guard must treat that as legitimate evidence while still blocking future-action promises without calls.
- When the latest user explicitly asks to search the web or provides a URL, Anti-Codex should bias the first model action to translator-owned `web_search` or `web_fetch` instead of leaving generic shell tools equally attractive. After that internal web tool runs, `tool_choice` must return to auto so the model can summarize or continue with normal Codex tools.
- User-visible guardrail and empty-response text must use Anti-Codex branding. Leaving DeepCodex in blocker text makes the product look like a thin wrapper and also makes debugging trust worse when the underlying route is actually Antigravity.
- MCP/Skill migration must be checked as full shared-to-local parity, not a small allowlist smoke test. The verifier now fails when any shared skill directory or shared MCP server name from `C:\Users\XD\.codex` is missing in Anti-Codex, while still reporting local extras separately.
- MCP config sync is intentionally not byte-for-byte destructive: Anti-Codex safety flags, local state roots, and Windows command normalization must survive. The enforceable contract is "everything shared is present and usable in Anti-Codex", not overwriting Anti-Codex into native Codex or spoofing connector/app-tool authorization.
- TOML section scans must anchor to real section lines. A loose `[mcp_servers.*]` regex can count commented examples as configured MCP servers and create false missing-parity failures; verifier behavior tests should include comments plus `.env` and `.tools.` subtables.
- In this workspace, the active repo is the `deepcodex` subdirectory. Patch tools invoked from the parent path need `deepcodex/...` targets; otherwise a valid file name can fail as missing.
- Third-party provider "save and restart" is not the same as proving provider switching. A verifier must actually continue from Provider A's last `previous_response_id` after loading Provider B, then inspect Provider B's received request for Provider A history and the new provider model/key.
- Manager action tokens are process-local. Any verifier that restarts the translator must fetch a fresh Manager bootstrap token before the next protected Manager action.
- Manager `ui-only` button smoke can leave a fake action bridge on the live Manager page. Any later real-click verifier must restore the saved real bridge before dispatching non-ui-only actions.
- Some real Manager actions prove completion through visible result text without writing a `lastAction` record. Button verifiers should accept action-specific visible success text when the hit area, busy state, and failure checks all pass.
- Real Manager button verification must reject stale `ui-only manager button smoke` result text in non-ui-only mode. The 2026-06-23 standalone gate now requires current `lastAction` evidence for `mcpSkills` and `pluginCapabilities`, so stale visible summaries are recorded as failures instead of real coverage.
- `viewLatestReports` has a valid empty state. The text `还没有可显示的验收报告` is a successful real result for that action, not a timeout or missing report failure.
- Cross-protocol provider continuity needs protocol-specific request inspection. OpenAI-compatible to Anthropic is only proven when the final `/v1/messages` request contains the earlier OpenAI-compatible user/assistant history and uses the Anthropic key/model.
- Reverse provider continuity needs the same proof in the other direction. Anthropic to OpenAI-compatible is only proven when the final `/v1/chat/completions` request contains the earlier Anthropic assistant response and uses the new OpenAI-compatible key/model.
- A one-off `spawn EPERM` while saving the temporary upstream profile can be a Windows child-process launch hiccup; rerun before treating it as a continuity regression. The useful invariant is whether the verifier reaches its report with `crossProtocolWithoutHistoryLoss:true`.
- Manager diagnostic bundle results can be nested as `payload.result.result`; summaries and verifiers must unwrap that layer before looking for `zipPath` / `bundleRoot`.
- Manager snapshot refresh uses the same action bridge as user actions. When refresh is only feeding status cards, suppress `refreshStatus` as a last-action result so it cannot overwrite a just-finished button summary.
- The Manager bridge must include trusted local `Origin` and `Referer` headers for bootstrap token fetches as well as protected action calls; strict source validation should remain enabled.
- A real diagnostics button success is `诊断包：已生成` plus visible `ZIP` or `目录`, and the verifier should treat `已生成` as a finished state.
- Connector/app-tool parity must not use PowerShell 5.1 `ConvertFrom-Json` on huge connector catalogs; it can fail on large valid JSON and silently collapse a parity report to 0/0 if errors are swallowed.
- A connector parity verifier is only useful if it reports nonzero `sharedCount` / `localCount` on a populated machine and fails on `missingShared`; otherwise it can miss real drift.
- `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1` is the safe repair path when shared connector cache has entries missing from Anti-Codex local cache. It copies metadata snapshots only and does not grant connector authorization.
- Manager button verifiers share a local lock and must not be run in parallel with UI-state/other Manager button checks. A real diagnostics bundle can return a visible ZIP success before the page-side pending counter clears, so the verifier should trust explicit action success text while still rejecting ui-only smoke text.
- Full installed overall should not click Manager actions that start background update/source/sync/report/bundle work in the middle of the combined suite. Those actions can leave the embedded panel busy or temporarily restart the gateway, causing unrelated UI idle or provider-continuity checks to fail; keep them as standalone button/action verifiers.
- Manager action-script JSONP verification should avoid Windows PowerShell 5.1 `Invoke-WebRequest` for valid `application/javascript` responses. Raw .NET HTTP plus inner-exception unwrapping preserves 403/400/200 status evidence and prevents the standalone verifier from exiting silently before printing its JSON report.
- Any real Manager button verifier must treat `lastActionResult.ok === false` and visible `需要处理` text as failure, even if the text contains action-specific completion phrases. This prevents degraded plugin/connector parity from being counted as a real green click.
- Shared Codex connector/app-tool caches can drift during the day. When plugin capability parity reports `missingShared`, rerun `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1`; this refreshes Anti-Codex-owned metadata snapshots only and keeps callable app tools at 0 unless connectors are actually authorized.
- Wrong diagnostics entry points waste time and create false failures. `-NoPopup` belongs to `collect-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1`, not `test-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1`, and diagnostic bundle collection is designed to run from the installed runtime copy where install-root detection is authoritative.
- Runtime sync with `-NoRestart` copies files but does not reload long-lived server code. When Manager action inventory or adaptive-server behavior changes, restart the Anti-Codex translator or run sync without `-NoRestart` before judging the installed UI.
- Manager real-button verifiers must reset both pending promise maps and action queues before dispatch. Otherwise a previous timeout or fake bridge can contaminate the next click, especially after ui-only smoke tests.
- Fast sync-readiness checks that skip icon palette decoding must also skip palette-derived frame assertions. A skipped expensive probe should be reported as skipped, not converted into a false missing-frame failure.
- Latest report readers must distinguish missing reports from damaged reports. If a `latest-*.json` file exists but cannot parse, Manager should surface `invalid_report` / damaged-report evidence instead of hiding it as "not generated"; installed overall and diagnostic bundles should treat damaged latest reports as actionable evidence.
- Report writers should publish `latest-*.json` through same-directory temp files plus atomic replacement, with JSON validation before the latest file becomes visible. Direct partial writes can create damaged latest reports that Manager and diagnostics must then treat as real failures.
- PowerShell atomic replacement should pass a real same-directory backup path to `[System.IO.File]::Replace`; passing `$null` can fail on Windows PowerShell 5.1/.NET combinations with "path is not of a legal form".
- Shared report helpers should resolve relative paths through PowerShell session path semantics, not raw .NET current directory semantics, because `Push-Location` does not necessarily change `[System.IO.Directory]::GetCurrentDirectory()`.
- Manager latest-report inventory and script outputs must stay synchronized. If Manager lists a report such as `latest-account-proxy-readiness.json`, the corresponding verifier must actually write both latest and history reports.
- Report integrity should live outside the JSON payload as a sidecar hash. Embedding a hash inside the report creates circular hashing and would require every report schema to change; `.sha256` sidecars keep existing report consumers compatible.
- Unsigned legacy reports are not the same as damaged reports. They should be surfaced as unsigned/legacy evidence, while a present sidecar with a hash mismatch should be treated as damaged even if the JSON still parses.
- Runtime sync can leave an older node-based translator process behind when switching back to the packaged translator. After restart, check port ownership and stop only the stale Anti-Codex runtime node process if the packaged translator owns the active localhost listener.
- Manager theme verification must follow the same priority as Manager theme injection. Explicit Electron host classes such as `electron-dark` / `electron-light` and data theme attributes are stronger evidence than body background color, because Codex can render a light background while the Electron theme class remains dark.
- Third-party provider tool-history continuity must be verified structurally. Text markers alone can survive while the request shape is wrong; OpenAI-compatible requests need the assistant `tool_calls` plus paired `role:"tool"` message, and Anthropic requests need `tool_use` plus paired `tool_result`.
- Fake provider history tests should derive the synthetic answer from the latest user message, not the whole hydrated history. Whole-history scans can accidentally reuse an older `turn-N` after a `function_call_output`-only request and create a false verifier failure.
- Fake provider tool-call triggers must be scoped to the current request's tool definition or tool choice. A hydrated prior user message can still contain the trigger phrase, so matching only the text history can repeatedly emit tool calls instead of testing the paired tool result.
- A one-off `fetch failed` in the third-party verifier inside installed overall is not enough to call provider continuity broken. Re-run the installed verifier directly and then rerun installed overall; accept it as transient only when both produce fresh green reports.
- The local skills contract must include both `C:\Users\XD\.codex\skills` and `C:\Users\XD\.agents\skills`. On this machine the `.agents` root contains 26 Lark skills; checking only `.codex\skills` leaves Anti-Codex missing capabilities that are available to the current Codex session.
- The enforceable skill parity number is the union of shared skill roots, not the raw local child count. The local skills directory also contains snapshot metadata such as `.anticodex-source-manifest.json`, so verification should compare skill directory names and report `expectedSharedCount` / `missingShared`.
- Diagnostics freshness must use the same shared-root set as the snapshot sync script. After adding `.agents\skills`, both the marker writer and adaptive-server fingerprint reader include that root; installed diagnostics now reports `sourceFingerprintMatches:true` for the 126-skill union.
- Local connector/app-tool verification now proves three separate boundaries: local allowlisting does not set `isAccessible`, inaccessible/orphan cached tools never reach `/backend-api/wham/apps`, and blocked connector install URLs are scrubbed from the local HTTP projection.
- Installed plugin capability verification must compare GET and POST `/backend-api/wham/apps`. A mismatch means one entry point can bypass app-tool filtering even if the other remains safe.
- `latest-local-connectors.json` is release evidence and must be included in installed overall report-integrity checks, not only written by the verifier itself.
- Nested PowerShell passed through `rtk proxy ... -Command` is fragile when it contains `$null`, `$_`, script blocks, arrays, or quoted regexes. Prefer short script files, single-quoted outer `-Command '& { ... }'`, `rg`, `curl.exe`, or direct focused commands; record wrapper-induced parse errors as command-construction failures, not product failures.
- Runtime sync should judge translator-only restart by returned `/health`, not by the helper process exiting. A launcher can keep running while the packaged translator is already healthy; waiting only on process exit creates false "restart timed out" failures and can leave duplicate localhost listeners.
- Real Manager button verification must not accept stale visible result text. Non-ui-only success for MCP/Skill and plugin/application actions needs the current `lastAction` plus no pending queue/promise entries; otherwise a previous `refreshStatus` or stale summary can be mistaken for the requested result.
- Shared connector caches can drift within a single work session. On 2026-06-23, shared connector parity moved to `5866` while the Anti-Codex local cache still had `5841`, causing plugin capability checks to correctly fail. The safe fix is `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1`, which copies metadata snapshots only and still leaves callable app tools at `0` without real connector authorization.
- Long verification commands should be split by purpose. For routine progress, prefer parser/self-test/contract checks and standalone MCP/plugin gates; reserve source preflight full regression and installed overall for checkpoint or release acceptance.
- User-visible native launcher errors are part of branding. `src\deepcodex-launcher-win.cs` may keep legacy internal class/script names for compatibility, but every `MessageBox.Show` title must say `Anti-Codex`; launcher contract tests should reject `DeepCodex` in those dialog titles.
- Audit documents can become active misinformation after coverage lands. `docs\audits\real-usage-test-coverage.md` must track the current verifier set: Chinese response checks, real MCP smoke coverage, final completion assertions, and legacy-brand rejection are no longer 0% gaps.
- Legacy-brand sweeps should separate user-visible text from compatibility names. It is safe to keep names like `start-deepcodex.sh` or `deepcodexPluginLabelled`, but alerts, console warnings, tooltips, setup messages, and model-visible workflow labels must say Anti-Codex.
- Marketing/docs copy is user-facing product surface. If it still says DeepCodex or describes the product as DeepSeek-only, it conflicts with the current Anti-Codex provider model and safety boundaries; refresh it or mark it explicitly historical.
- Provider stack docs are user-facing setup surface, not historical evidence. If a current-looking doc still presents one-api or DeepSeek relay setup as the default path, rewrite it around the current Anti-Codex default provider, explicit third-party profile flow, metadata-only MCP/skill/connector parity, and no fake connector authorization.
- Historical marketing material should be moved or clearly marked instead of silently rewritten when it records an older launch. Keep the current marketing entry points Anti-Codex-branded, and put old DeepCodex/DeepSeek launch scripts under a legacy path.
- Protocol compatibility docs should describe the current provider contract even when code retains legacy internal prefixes. `deepcodex.reasoning.hex.v1:` is a persisted internal replay token; it should be documented as compatibility state, while the user-facing route remains Anti-Codex with Antigravity default and explicit third-party providers.
- Top-level DeepSeek failure notes are useful historical lessons, but without a legacy marker they look like current Anti-Codex runtime guidance. Keep the observations, and add a visible status line when the current default provider has moved on.
- Windows setup docs may need to mention legacy path segments such as `codex-home-deepseek-app`; when they do, explicitly say these are compatibility state and do not imply the current provider route or connector authorization.
- Public documentation regressions should be tested through an explicit current-doc whitelist, not recursive `docs/**` scans. Audits, legacy marketing, prompt samples, and historical specs may legitimately contain DeepCodex/DeepSeek evidence, while README, current marketing, provider-stack, and compatibility docs must preserve current Anti-Codex provider and connector boundaries.
- README is the public entry point and must not lag behind deeper docs. It should state the active default provider, the explicit third-party profile path, and the connector authorization boundary, not only the historical Antigravity-only shape.
- Fast source preflight must include cheap public-doc contracts because full translator regression can be skipped during routine install/sync checks. Keep that check whitelist-based so archived DeepCodex evidence is not treated as a current product regression.
- Any new source-preflight check should be surfaced through diagnostics summaries, not only buried in the raw latest report. Otherwise Manager and diagnostic bundles can show a green source-preflight summary while omitting the newly added evidence dimension.
- Installed overall fast mode skips the standalone diagnostics verifier, so source-preflight evidence that matters for release confidence should also have a direct fast-safe installed-overall check through `/anticodex/diagnostics`.
- Manager latest-report summaries should expose field-level evidence for newly added source-preflight gates. A report-level green state is weaker than showing that the public-documentation subcheck itself passed.
- Manager latest-report button acceptance should require the same field-level evidence as the summary payload. If source-preflight appears in the latest-report text, the text must include the public-documentation result; otherwise a UI smoke can pass while hiding the new gate.
- Public documentation gates must include every current public entry point, including `README_EN.md`. Historical or legacy folders can remain out of the current-doc whitelist only when they are clearly treated as archive material.
- When wrapping a PowerShell verifier to suppress large JSON output, avoid `-Command` strings where nested quotes can strip variables or array string literals. Prefer direct script redirection plus reading the generated latest report summary; record wrapper parser errors as command-construction failures, not product regressions.
- Legacy marketing material is acceptable only when it is unmistakably marked as historical and excluded from current launch surfaces. A legacy folder can keep DeepCodex / DeepSeek evidence, but the marker itself should be enforced by the public-documentation gate.
- Diagnostic bundles should not merely carry Manager real-button reports as opaque files. The summary and verifier need to preserve the requested actions plus per-action real-bridge evidence so support bundles can prove `mcpSkills` and `pluginCapabilities` were clicked without relying on a long installed overall rerun.
- Diagnostic bundle summary parsing should use a small Node helper over each report, not Windows PowerShell 5.1 `ConvertFrom-Json` over full report files. Large but valid reports can be misreported as damaged by PowerShell, while a compact Node-produced summary keeps diagnostics stable.
- Avoid embedding large multi-line Node programs in PowerShell native-command arguments. On Windows this can collapse into opaque `[eval]:1` failures; keep the Node logic in a checked-in `.mjs` helper and pass only file paths.
- Provider continuity evidence should prove structure, not only text markers. OpenAI-compatible history needs ordered `assistant.tool_calls` plus the paired `role:"tool"` result with the same id, Anthropic history needs ordered `tool_use` plus paired `tool_result`, and each protocol should reject the other's tool shape.
- Short installed overall should keep UI-state evidence but bound its budget. For Manager `fastOverallNoTraffic`, prefer `-Fast -NoTraffic -SubcheckTimeoutSec 30 -FastMaxElapsedMs 90000` instead of skipping UI state entirely; long real traffic, soak, cold relaunch, and disruptive Manager actions remain separate gates.
- Fast or skipped verification reports should explicitly mark skipped behavior as not covered. Use fields such as `behaviorCovered:false` and `coverageKind:"skipped"` so dashboards do not confuse a planned skip with behavioral proof.
- Release readiness should be summarized passively from latest reports before running long gates. A `shortReady:true` / `releaseReady:false` summary is useful progress evidence: it proves the short gate is green while listing the exact long/real-traffic/soak/cold evidence still missing.
- Cold relaunch evidence must be a first-class signed latest report before it appears in dashboards or release summaries. Keep `latest-cold-relaunch.json` optional in passive release readiness because producing it restarts the isolated Anti-Codex runtime.
- PowerShell `>` redirection can write captured Node JSON as UTF-16, causing a false `Unexpected token` parse failure when Node reads the temp file. For short JSON summaries, let Node capture child-process stdout directly or read the signed latest report file.
- `rg` regexes that mix quoted literals, C:\ paths, and backslashes are easy to mangle in PowerShell. For installation checks, prefer `rg -F` with fixed strings and quote each path separately.
- Shared MCP config can point at versioned native Codex bin paths that disappear after an app update. Anti-Codex config sync should repair a missing absolute MCP command by finding the latest existing sibling executable with the same file name under the same `bin` root, instead of blindly copying a stale path.
- Manager button probes can exceed 90 seconds when a previous Manager action is still draining or queued. Installed overall should derive a larger Manager button timeout from `SubcheckTimeoutSec` and keep a grace process timeout, while standalone button gates remain the clearer diagnostic when debugging.
- Fresh green legacy reports that predate `.sha256` sidecars can be signed in place by hashing the current JSON bytes. This preserves behavior evidence without rerunning real-traffic gates, but only do it when the report parses and `ok:true`; never alter the JSON payload to manufacture success.
- Report sidecar backfill should be a dry-run-first tool. Default to no writes, require an explicit `--write`, and skip `ok:false` or unparseable reports so the operation cannot turn a failing report into release evidence.
- Installed diagnostics verification intentionally prefers scripts from the installed runtime. After changing a source-side summarizer or verifier, sync the runtime before treating an installed verification failure as product evidence. The diagnostics collector also rejects direct execution from a development checkout by design because install-root detection is part of its contract.
- Do not combine `fork_context:true` with an explicit subagent `agent_type`. Full-history forks inherit the parent type; use a fresh self-contained explorer prompt when a specialized agent type is required.
- Before prefixing a PowerShell batch with `rtk`, classify each command as an external executable or a shell-native cmdlet. `Get-Content` and other cmdlets must run directly; one invalid cmdlet can hide the useful results from sibling parallel checks.
- Do not derive a script-relative default parameter from `$PSScriptRoot` inside the PowerShell `param(...)` block. On Windows PowerShell 5.1 it can still be empty while defaults are evaluated; accept an empty parameter and resolve it immediately after the block.
- A subagent usage-limit failure is not review evidence. The read-only security reviewer `019ef0d6-1d71-7893-9fc8-175cc2b3e8bb` returned only an account usage-limit error, made no changes, and was replaced by direct diff review plus focused negative tests.
- A history-continuity prompt must not contain the expected history values. The first turn-10 draft listed every marker in the current prompt, so a model could pass by echoing the instruction without reading prior history. Ask for semantic positions instead, then compare the response against values known only from earlier turns.
- Optional real-provider and provider-to-Antigravity reports should be visible in Manager and diagnostic bundles when generated, but their skipped/optional status must not be promoted into release-required evidence.
- DeepSeek job `dsw_mqpna7wl_sxri13` produced no changes after repeated HTTP 502 retries. Treat this as worker service failure, preserve the clean worktree, and retry the tiny RED-only task with a healthy worker rather than inferring anything about the code.
- The existing upstream profile storage test already proves corrupt profile fail-closed, unreadable DPAPI key fail-closed, explicit Antigravity recovery without decrypting the bad key, response-history preservation, and no real APPDATA profile mutation. The missing piece was short-gate execution, now provided by source preflight as `checks.upstreamProfileStorage`.
- A `node --test-name-pattern` run is valid evidence only when the output names the intended test. A non-matching filter can load the file and look green without exercising the target assertion.
- The Responses HTTP catch previously lost `response_history_missing` by routing third-party errors through generic `fail()`, producing outer `error.type:"gateway_error"` even with HTTP 410. Handle history-missing before provider-specific error classification so the desktop client receives a stable non-retryable type, guidance, and original response ID without any upstream call.
- Ordinary reviewer `019ef101-0626-7db3-95c9-6762e613141f` was stopped after a bounded wait with no result; no review claim relies on it.
- The installed missing-history verifier now supplies direct no-traffic evidence for the 410 recovery contract. A green HTTP response alone is insufficient: the report also records unchanged route count/latest request/transport/status so the local rejection cannot be mistaken for an upstream provider attempt.
- Two JavaScript orchestration attempts failed before product commands ran because nested PowerShell command strings were quoted incorrectly. PowerShell-heavy checks should be dispatched as separate shell calls instead of embedded in one `Promise.all` source string.
- Optional stability-soak reports must be semantically checked when present. `ok:true` alone is not enough; require readiness before/after, stable translator PID, health samples, zero failed turns, and mode/model evidence, while keeping the soak optional because generating it can be long and may use real upstream traffic.
- PowerShell parser checks launched through an outer PowerShell need backtick-escaped `$` variables and single-quoted file paths inside the inner script block. Otherwise the outer shell strips variables/quotes and creates a false parser failure before the product script is read.
- Report inventory parity matters independently from semantic gates. When a report becomes release-required, make sure it is also listed in diagnostic bundle collection, Manager latest reports, and installed-overall report-integrity checks; otherwise release readiness can be green while the UI/support bundle hides the evidence.
- Release-gate group summaries should show required and optional counts separately. A group like cold relaunch can be `0/1` satisfied but still have `required 0/0`, and operators need that distinction to avoid false alarm.
- Optional real-traffic skip reports should be explicit no-traffic evidence, not just absence of a request. Require `requestedRealTraffic:false`, `usedRealTraffic:false`, `behaviorCovered:false`, and `coverageKind:"skipped"` for skipped `ok:true` optional reports.
- The external Manager bridge has its own latest-report summary renderer separate from `translator/adaptive-server.mjs`. Any new Manager latest-report evidence field should be added to both renderers and both contract tests, or the in-app UI and bridge path will diverge.
- Static hand-maintained latest-report lists drift easily. Keep a dynamic contract that parses `test-anticodex-release-gates.mjs` for `requiredForRelease:true` report files and checks diagnostic bundle collection, Manager latest reports, installed-overall report-integrity checks, and launcher contract coverage together.
- The injected Manager panel (`anticodex-manager-inject.js`) also renders latest-report summaries. It must mirror the bridge/adaptive summaries for release groups, source preflight, model thinking, MCP/Skill, optional real-provider, Antigravity return, cold relaunch, and stability soak evidence.
- Manager latest-report summaries need failure detail parity, not only success evidence parity. Keep `missingShort`, `damagedReports`, release-gate semantic failures, source-preflight subcheck details, and model-thinking reasoning levels visible in the in-app summary, external bridge, and injected panel together.
- Optional latest reports should have visibility parity without becoming release blockers. Optional real-provider reports, cold relaunch, and stability soak should stay optional in release gates but still appear in diagnostics, Manager, and installed-overall report-integrity checks. `latest-refresh-features.json` is different: it proves current shared-to-local feature synchronization and should block release when stale or missing.
- MCP/Skill and plugin/app-tool evidence should not be summarized differently between the embedded Manager, external bridge, and injected panel. Keep `.agents` skill counts, MCP smoke, real node_repl smoke, skill readability, and dirty parity samples visible across all three surfaces.
- MCP server inventory parsers must ignore nested TOML subtables such as `[mcp_servers.name.tools.tool]`. Use an anchored `[mcp_servers.<name>]` parser and filter `.tools.` / `.env` entries consistently between refresh and verification scripts.
- Worker MCP smoke should prove the exposed tool surface, not only that `tools/list` returns something. Require the expected DeepSeek and Antigravity worker tool names so a partial health-only MCP server cannot pass synchronization verification.
- Global guidance files are part of the inherited runtime surface. Anti-Codex should hash-compare `AGENTS.md`, `DEFAULT_SKILL_ALLOWLIST.md`, `RTK.md`, and `MCP_WORKER_USAGE_GUIDE.md` against the shared originals, not merely check that local copies exist.
- Connector/app-tool parity by key is not enough. The same connector or app-tool ID can drift in content while key counts stay green, so plugin capability verification must compare per-record hashes and expose `changedShared` in summaries and release-gate semantics.
- Manager protected-action results are shaped as `payload.result.reports`, and report entries use `fileName`. Earlier probes against `items`, nested `result.result`, or `file` were inspection mistakes and are not product evidence.
- A background `Start-Process` runtime-sync probe returned no process identity and wrote empty logs. The direct sync was rerun in bounded 10-second waits and completed successfully in 38.7 seconds; empty background logs were not accepted as deployment evidence.
- The real `viewLatestReports` button returned `lastOk:true` and displayed the new missing-history report, but its verifier returned false because the already-open desktop panel retained an older `native-panel-v84` renderer that omitted public-documentation field text. Backend payload inspection proved `publicDocumentationOk:true`; panel code changes require a version bump so existing windows reinject the renderer.
- Ordinary explorer `019ef103-73d5-7720-9b67-6190515f4865` declined to inspect the local workspace and returned no usable Manager endpoint evidence. It was closed and is not cited as review coverage.
- A combined `rg` inspection included a nonexistent `launcher` path and exited 1 after returning useful matches. Search-command exit status with a bad path must not be treated as a product failure; keep search paths bounded to existing roots.
- Manager latest-report acceptance should align with the new evidence being shipped. During the missing-history batch, the real `viewLatestReports` click already displayed `历史过期恢复：通过` and the structured payload carried `publicDocumentationOk:true`; keeping the verifier blocked on stale public-documentation text turned a good missing-history UI proof into a false failure. For new gates, assert the required new report/semantic evidence directly.
- Same `native-panel-v85` renderer updates can still leave an already-open window with old closure state if the reload guard only checks the panel version. Add a narrower refresh marker such as `__ANTICODEX_MANAGER_SUMMARY_VERSION__` when changing Manager summary behavior so same-version pages reinject the updated renderer.
- Bash here-doc syntax such as `node - <<'JS'` fails in PowerShell before Node runs. Use `node -e`, a checked-in helper script, or native PowerShell string handling in this Windows workspace.
- Passive release gates must validate missing-history semantics, not only signed `ok:true`: HTTP 410, `response_history_missing`, `retryable:false`, original ID match, recovery guidance, unchanged route fingerprint, `usedRealTraffic:false`, and installed no-traffic coverage are the actual proof.
- Passive release-gate semantic checks need negative self-tests. A signed fresh `ok:true` report can otherwise drift into release evidence without proving no-real-traffic, no-fake-connector-authorization, or plugin/app-tool parity boundaries. Keep forged-report self-tests for each high-risk semantic class.
- No-traffic release gates should reject explicit contradictory fields such as `usedRealTraffic:true`, `behaviorCovered:false`, or `coverageKind:"real-traffic"`, while tolerating absent legacy fields when another script-specific semantic check supplies the no-traffic proof.
- MCP/Skill parity now means no missing, changed, or extra inherited-surface entries for release readiness. Anti-Codex-local skills may exist only outside the inherited snapshot contract; any local skill under the inherited surface is treated as drift, and custom agents skill roots used by verification must also be passed into the snapshot copy step.
- Duplicate skill names across shared skill roots are not harmless when content differs. Detect same top-level skill names across `.codex\skills` and extra `.agents\skills` roots and fail conflicting copies explicitly, rather than relying on overlay order to choose a winner.
- MCP/Skill verification should treat same-name skill content drift as a runtime failure too. Directory-name union alone can miss stale local skill content, so reports need duplicate/conflicting duplicate fields and failure text.
- MCP inheritance checks should compare important same-name server fields, not only server names. At minimum, compare `command`, `args`, and `env` and report `changedShared`; command comparison may normalize absolute executable paths by leaf name to allow version-path repair without hiding argument/env drift.
- Release-gate report consumers must not equate `ok:true` with releasable. Non-strict release-gate summaries may be `ok:true` while `releaseReady:false`; Manager/diagnostics/summaries should use an effective pass field that requires release readiness and no damaged reports.
- Release-gate `--self-test` should be run by a focused routine test, not only string-matched. Otherwise semantic guard names can remain present while forged-report rejection logic silently breaks.
- Provider-switch continuity should prove ordered role/message shape, not just marker presence in serialized text. Require array-level user/assistant ordering across late turns, alongside protocol-specific OpenAI/Anthropic tool-pair shape checks.
- Startup snapshot fast paths should compare source-manifest fingerprints before keeping an existing local directory. Non-empty local snapshot alone is not enough because it can preserve stale skill/plugin cache content while writing a fresh marker.
- Duplicate skill-name checks should include linked directories. `.agents\skills` may contain symlink or junction entries; resolve them with `stat` for inventory purposes so linked same-name content cannot silently override or escape conflict checks.
- Refresh-feature reports should verify connector/app-tool cache content, not just existence/counts. After local snapshot copy, compare shared and local directory fingerprints and fail if they diverge.
- Third-party provider continuity release evidence must remain structural. Require late-turn all-marker coverage, OpenAI `tool_calls` plus paired `role:"tool"`, Anthropic `tool_use` plus paired `tool_result`, protocol-shape cleanliness, provider-switch reachability, stale-model rejection, and secret redaction at the passive release gate.
- Any field newly required by passive release gates should also be visible in report summaries or diagnostic bundles. Otherwise operators can see a green gate without seeing the concrete continuity evidence, such as late-turn provider marker counts.
- Release gate failure details shown to operators should exclude optional missing reports that are not required for short/release readiness. Otherwise `releaseReady:true` can appear to conflict with a displayed optional cold-relaunch miss.
- Optional real-traffic reports need a three-way distinction: not requested, requested and covered, requested but uncovered. A skipped report may be `ok:true` only when real traffic was not requested; once the user opts in, missing args, missing key, or a busy real-traffic lock must produce `ok:false` and a real failures array.
- PowerShell JSON reports should force empty lists through an explicit array variable before `ConvertTo-Json`. Inline conditional empty arrays can serialize as `{}` in Windows PowerShell 5.1, which breaks downstream machine readers.
- Connector cache drift is expected during long sessions. If plugin capability verification reports many `missingShared` connector IDs while app tools remain unauthorized, run `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1` and then rerun plugin capability verification; do not weaken parity checks or mark missing shared connectors as acceptable.
- A report field being present in raw JSON is not enough operational evidence. For each new verifier dimension, add summary/diagnostic/Manager surface plus at least one dynamic fixture test that proves values survive the summarizer, not just static string assertions.
- MCP `changedShared`, conflicting duplicate skills, and refresh-feature connector/app-tool parity are operational drift signals. They must be consumed by release gates and visible in summaries/diagnostics/Manager panels; otherwise raw verifier failures can be hidden from the operator path.
- Local connector/app-tool verification should explicitly state no-real-traffic coverage. A local fake-provider endpoint test must report `reportName`, `usedRealTraffic:false`, `behaviorCovered:true`, a local coverage kind, GET/POST tool parity, allowlist-not-authorization evidence, and zero upstream requests so passive gates do not infer safety from `ok:true` alone.
- Provider restart continuity should be proven at the first post-restart turn, not only by a later provider-switch turn. Record ordered role history and all available pre-answer markers for turn 6 immediately after translator restart, then gate that direct evidence separately from turn 7 provider-switch evidence.
- Source preflight should refresh short-gate evidence that it depends on. If a latest-report verifier is cheap and local-only, invoke it from source preflight with the same report root instead of trusting a possibly stale `latest-*.json` from an older run.
- Optional report slots should validate report identity, not only evidence shape. A payload with marker/route fields but the wrong `reportName` must fail the optional real-provider or provider-to-Antigravity gate.
- Feature synchronization evidence should be release-required once it carries content fingerprints. If shared MCP/skills/connectors/app-tools can change after older reports were generated, `latest-refresh-features.json` must be fresh, signed, and semantically clean before passive release readiness is true.
- After semantic hardening, old signed reports can fail because they predate newly required fields, not because the runtime is broken. Prefer bounded reruns of the specific short verifiers before changing gate logic; source preflight can refresh local connector evidence, MCP/Skill can refresh inherited tool/skill fields, and the local third-party provider matrix can refresh restart/switch history evidence without real traffic.
- Optional real-traffic hardening must stay opt-in. Default `--strict` should remain a stable release-required gate, while `--strict-optional-real-traffic` may require true real-traffic optional reports and fail skipped optional reports.
- In inline PowerShell commands launched from PowerShell, variables inside the `-Command` string must be escaped or protected. Otherwise `$tokens`, `$errors`, and `$_` can be expanded by the outer shell into empty text before the parser runs.
- When a PowerShell parser check is wrapped inside `rtk powershell -Command`, protect both `$tokens`/`$errors` and the script path in the same quoting layer. A safer shape is `-Command "& { `$tokens=`$null; ... ParseFile('scripts\file.ps1',[ref]`$tokens,[ref]`$errors) ... }"`; mixed outer single quotes can still arrive with path quotes stripped.
- Optional real-traffic gates should be report-specific once traffic is actually used. A generic `usedRealTraffic:true` check is too weak; real-provider reports need marker/history/redaction evidence, and provider-to-Antigravity return reports need turn-10 history, previous response ID, marker count, and route evidence.
- Manager latest-report summaries should show enough parity drift detail to act without opening raw JSON. For connector/app-tool parity, include counts plus a short sample of missing or mismatched IDs; clean/dirty booleans alone are too lossy during cache drift.
- Diagnostic verifier requirements should track newly surfaced summary fields. If Manager/report summaries expose MCP/Skill or optional real-traffic evidence, `test-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1 -VerifyBundle` should also require the field when the source report exists; otherwise the bundle can regress while static launcher contracts stay green.
- MCP/Skill release readiness must be semantic, not only signed `ok:true`. Enforce missing shared MCP/skills, `.agents` skill counting, node_repl presence, real node_repl MCP smoke, and skill readability at the passive release gate.
- Source-preflight release readiness must include upstream profile storage evidence. A stale source-preflight report that predates `checks.upstreamProfileStorage` should fail strict passive gates until the no-traffic source-preflight verifier is rerun.
- Every required no-traffic release gate should have a Manager latest-report entry. If a report is required by passive release gates but absent from Manager latest reports, users cannot diagnose release readiness from the UI.
- Model-routing no-traffic evidence must be per-model, not just `skipRequests:true`. Require mode, model count/list consistency, per-model results, every result marked `skippedRequest:true`, no failed results, and no result errors; surface the same counts in summaries, diagnostics, and Manager latest reports.
- Tool-bridge release readiness must prove the current default model-only contract, not only report success: Codex owns tool execution, Antigravity is model text/entitlement only, native bridge defaults disabled, and native same-Cascade evidence is accepted only when explicitly opted in.
- Model-thinking readiness must prove every catalog entry has usable thinking metadata. Require expected/actual count agreement, one unique ID per entry, display name, reasoning level, `supports_thinking:true`, and `thinking_budget >= min_thinking_budget`; expose compact counts so UI/diagnostics show what failed.
- UI-idle readiness should summarize sample health, not only elapsed time. Require every sample to be health/account/UI green, have model count and matched model text, and show no reconnect or login text; surface the counts in diagnostics and Manager summaries.
- Sync-readiness should carry its own no-traffic attestation. A green install/sync report must state report name, full/no-traffic mode, behavior coverage, active/source hash match, operation success, rollback host readiness, shortcut inventory, Manager bridge base URL, and absence of stale sync/candidate/swap state.
- Installed-overall readiness needs top-level no-traffic semantics. A signed `ok:true` combined report is not enough; require `usedRealTraffic:false`, `behaviorCovered:true`, a specific `coverageKind`, all checks `ok:true`, skip metadata, and explicit skipped real-traffic checks.
- Combined installed-overall should not inline every long Manager button click. The plugin/application button can stall the local bridge and then poison UI-idle sampling; use the standalone plugin capability report as the authority and record the skip as covered, not as fake connector authorization.
- Third-party provider save/restart continuity should be gated structurally, not only by booleans. Require save/restart tool evidence, restart tool-pair preservation, `previous_response_id` continuity, and marker coverage before treating a signed fake-provider report as release evidence.
- Optional real provider reports are sanity checks, not the full fake-provider matrix. When no real traffic is requested, generate explicit skip reports for operator visibility; when real traffic is requested, keep marker/history/route/redaction requirements but do not claim nine-turn provider-switch coverage unless that flow actually ran.
- Optional real provider sanity checks need route evidence too. Marker echo proves text continuity, but release gates should also require `/anticodex/model-routes` to show `upstream-provider`, the expected provider protocol, requested/resolved model agreement, and success status.
- Optional cold-relaunch evidence should be semantic when present but not release-required when absent. A valid cold report should prove health/provider readiness, one main process, translator presence, bounded launcher count, required stages, and explicit original Codex PID-set preservation.
- Source snapshot evidence must be shared across related reports, not checked one report at a time. `latest-refresh-features.json`, `latest-mcp-skills.json`, and `latest-plugin-capabilities.json` can all be individually green while describing different inherited Codex surfaces; release gates should compare their source fingerprints together.
- For large inherited skill trees, content-hashing every file is too slow for routine verification. Use content hashes for compact high-risk sources such as config, connector/app-tool caches, and guidance files, and metadata signatures for huge skill directories so the source snapshot remains bounded.
- Full local feature refresh is a repair path, not a cheap health check. On this machine it can take about 72 seconds after source-snapshot hardening because it copies/verifies roughly 15.6k inherited files; prefer narrow verifier reruns or `-VerifyOnly` unless parity drift needs repair.
- `test-anticodex-plugin-capabilities.ps1` needs the same `.agents\skills` default as the refresh and MCP/Skill verifiers. Without an `AgentsSkillsPath` default, source snapshot generation can fail even though connector/app-tool parity itself is healthy.
- A verifier can time out after emitting useful JSON if the timeout is too tight for process cleanup and source scanning. Treat the JSON as a clue, then rerun with a bounded but realistic timeout before changing product logic.
- Manager latest-report evidence must stay in parity across adaptive server, external bridge, and injected panel. When adding a field such as `sourceSnapshotEvidence`, add a rendered line and tests in all three surfaces.
- In PowerShell, `Select-Object -Index 38..55` passes the range as a string. Use an array first, such as `$lines = Get-Content file; $lines[38..55]`, for bounded line reads.
- Provider continuity gates should cover the whole `previous_response_id` chain, not only save and restart. Provider switch, protocol switch, and reverse protocol switch each need continuation IDs, and summaries should expose a compact `previousResponseContinuity` object so diagnostics can verify them without raw report spelunking.
- Manager provider-continuity summaries should show restart tool-pair evidence and marker threshold status, not just later switch tool pairs. Otherwise a failed restart bridge can be hidden behind green provider/protocol switch evidence.
- Optional real-traffic skipped reports need diagnostics, not only release-gate checks. When a skipped optional report exists, verify `requestedRealTraffic:false`, `usedRealTraffic:false`, `behaviorCovered:false`, `coverageKind:"skipped"`, `reason`, and `coveredBy`; otherwise a requested-but-skipped or mislabeled optional report can look harmless in support bundles.
- Manager optional real-provider summaries should show both requested and resolved route models plus redaction evidence. A resolved-only route line proves less than the release gate requires and makes route/model mismatches harder to diagnose.
- Optional skipped-report metadata should be a release-gate requirement too. Diagnostic-only checks are useful for support bundles, but passive release gates should also reject skipped optional real-traffic reports without `reason` and `coveredBy`.
- Release-ready output should keep optional gaps separate from required failures. Use `missingOptional` for absent non-blocking reports so operators can see what is still unproven without reading it as a release blocker.
- Embedded Manager JavaScript can accumulate mojibake when copied between generated and standalone renderers. Keep a static guard for known mojibake tokens in `translator/adaptive-server.mjs` and `anticodex-manager-inject.js` whenever Manager text is edited.
- `-VerifyOnly` commands must not repair or rewrite local state. If a verifier needs feature-flag restoration, keep that in the refresh/repair path and let verify-only report current drift without mutation.
- Connector/app-tool parity must reject extra local cache records too. Missing/changed shared records catch stale inheritance, while extra local records catch Anti-Codex-only residue that would violate the "same inherited surface" contract.
- Skill inheritance needs content fingerprints, not just names. Compare each local inherited skill directory against the shared `.codex\skills` or `.agents\skills` fingerprint and gate `changedShared` the same way as missing shared skills.
- PowerShell `Get-FileHash` is too slow for 15k-file skill trees in routine verifiers. Use .NET SHA256 streaming and cache per-directory fingerprints when hashing inherited skills.
- Startup snapshot markers must record the extra skill roots used at marker creation. Diagnostics and Manager follow-up actions should prefer marker roots over current environment defaults to avoid false stale/source-changed results when custom `.agents` roots are used.
- Optional cold-relaunch should be easy to run but not accidental. Expose it as a high-impact Manager action with confirmation and singleton background locking, while keeping the actual restart verifier manual/operator-triggered.
- Release-gate status display must use `effectiveOk` everywhere. Bridge/inject summaries can otherwise show a non-strict `ok:true` / `releaseReady:false` gate as passing even though the adaptive Manager correctly marks it as needing action.
- Optional real-traffic success should prove intent and execution. For optional real-provider and provider-to-Antigravity reports, `usedRealTraffic:true` alone is not enough; successful non-skipped reports also need `requestedRealTraffic:true`.
- Current real-provider evidence is two separate optional proofs, not one continuous real chain: real third-party provider smoke covers two real provider turns, while provider-to-Antigravity return covers local fake-provider continuity plus an optional real Antigravity return turn. Do not describe it as a single real third-party-to-Antigravity chain until a dedicated same-state real return branch exists.
- The dedicated same-state real return branch now exists, but remains default-off. Treat `realProviderToAntigravityReturnEvidence.requested:true` as the only signal that real provider turn 2 actually chained into a real Antigravity return turn; otherwise the older two optional reports are still separate proofs.
- Do not run the same-chain real return branch during routine checks. It requires both `--allow-real-traffic` and `--allow-real-antigravity-return`, plus an explicit `--antigravity-model`, because it can consume both external provider traffic and real Antigravity traffic.
- Long-task diagnostics should expose freshness without dumping raw logs. For sync and post-verification actions, use structured status fields as `latestLogLine` plus `logUpdatedAt`/`logAgeMs`; keep raw transcript-like output out of diagnostic payloads and bundles.
- PowerShell 5.1 does not accept `&&` as a command separator. Keep staging and commit commands as separate shell calls in this workspace.
- Native continuation cleanup must be proven at the `NATIVE_CONTINUATIONS` layer, not only at the MCP bridge HTTP pending-call layer. The bridge may clean stale `tools/call` requests while an upstream retained continuation still leaks unless both layers have tests.
- Same-Cascade resume retries are only safe before any Codex-visible event. Keep `eventEmitted` as the boundary: pre-event transient errors may retry with `cancelOnError:false`, but errors after text/tool output must not silently replay.
- Manager long-task status should show structured freshness, not raw log tails. Use `latestLogLine` plus `logAgeMs`/`logUpdatedAt` from diagnostics, and keep rendered values escaped in the fallback HTML Manager.
- Manager action tokens change after translator restarts. Long fake-provider verifiers that restart or apply upstream profiles must re-bootstrap the Manager token before later `/anticodex/*` Manager calls; reusing an early token causes a fast 403 false failure.
- Static launcher tests contain several similarly named source fixtures. Assertions for fallback Manager HTML belong against `translator/adaptive-server.mjs` source, not the standalone PowerShell Manager verifier; otherwise a good UI change can produce a false failure.
- Foreground Manager singleflight has two user-visible surfaces. When adding duplicate-action copy or long-task rows to the fallback Manager in `translator/adaptive-server.mjs`, mirror the same visibility in `scripts/anticodex-manager-inject.js`; otherwise the in-app injected panel can hide active foreground checks that `/anticodex/diagnostics.longTasks` already reports.
- A spawned multi-agent explorer can return `not_found` when waited on even after a spawn id was emitted. Treat that as no usable worker evidence, continue with main-agent inspection, and record that no review claim depends on the missing worker.
- When a wrapper invokes a Node verifier with its own `--timeout-ms`, the wrapper process timeout must be derived from that inner budget plus cleanup margin. Otherwise a healthy no-traffic verifier can be killed by the wrapper's default subcheck timeout.
- In PowerShell JSON writers, do not put conditional array expressions directly inside `[pscustomobject]` properties. Materialize them into variables first, then assign `@($variable)` so Windows PowerShell 5.1 keeps empty arrays as arrays.
- Refresh-feature parity must include inherited skill and guidance content, not only counts or connector cache fingerprints. A local copy can have every expected name while still carrying stale `SKILL.md` or global rule content.
- When Manager UI gains a result field, update the read-only UI-state verifier in the same patch. The launcher contracts intentionally assert those verifier strings, so stale verifier coverage can create confusing false failures after a correct Manager change.
- Manager refresh-feature summaries must display skill and guidance drift, not just connector/app-tool parity. Otherwise `latest-refresh-features.json` can correctly flag stale inherited skills or `AGENTS.md`/`RTK.md` copies while the operator UI still looks clean.
- Windows linked-directory tests may fail with `EPERM` even when using `junction`. Treat that as an environment capability gap and skip only the link-specific fixture; keep normal duplicate-skill and linked-directory coverage active on machines that can create links.
- Response-history continuity is intentionally provider/protocol neutral. Lightweight unit tests should lock this at the store layer so OpenAI-compatible -> Anthropic -> OpenAI-compatible switching cannot accidentally make `hydrate()` key off provider metadata instead of `previous_response_id`.
- Cross-report source snapshot mismatch needs dynamic negative coverage. Static strings for `mcp_skills_source_snapshot_mismatch` and `plugin_capabilities_source_snapshot_mismatch` are not enough; release-gate self-test should forge semantically clean but fingerprint-divergent refresh/MCP/plugin reports.
- Verifier tests should key on the report contract (`ok:false`, semantic fields, `usedRealTraffic:false`) before assuming process exit semantics. Some source verifiers return structured `ok:false` with exit 0, while real-traffic safety failures intentionally exit 1 after writing the no-traffic failure report.
- Anti-Codex can inherit Codex `logs_2.sqlite` TRACE rows and WAL residue under its isolated `codex-home-deepseek-app`. Runtime mitigation should back up `logs_2.sqlite` plus `-wal`/`-shm`, install a `BEFORE INSERT ON logs WHEN NEW.level='TRACE' SELECT RAISE(IGNORE)` trigger, run `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)`, then sample `MAX(id)` and WAL size.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 `New-Item` may not accept `-LiteralPath`; use `New-Item -Path` for backup directory creation. For inline SQLite probes, avoid SQL string literals using double quotes because newer SQLite treats them as identifiers.
- Product-level log protection belongs in the Anti-Codex startup path before launching the desktop process, after `CODEX_HOME_DIR` is known. Keep source-level guard scripts in the runtime sync required-file list, or installed Anti-Codex can miss a source fix.
- Passive MCP/Skill release readiness must reject extra Anti-Codex-local skills as well as missing or changed inherited skills. Otherwise the inherited surface can drift even when every shared skill exists.
- Third-party provider continuity reports should carry explicit previous-response match booleans for save, restart, provider switch, protocol switch, and reverse protocol switch. Requiring only the `previous_response_id` fields to exist is weaker than proving each one equals the immediately prior response ID.
- `rtk` is useful for external binaries but cannot run PowerShell built-ins such as `Get-Content` directly because they are not PATH executables. Use native PowerShell for bounded file reads when `rtk Get-Content` fails.
- Running several PowerShell-backed launcher fixture tests in parallel can produce transient `spawnSync powershell.exe EPERM`. Treat that as a parallel test-run artifact and rerun the affected launcher contract sequentially before changing product logic.
- Diagnostic bundle report summaries are already sanitized machine summaries. Do not run the transcript-keyword redactor over the whole `reports-summary.json`; instead use an explicit safe-summary writer and keep sensitive raw diagnostics redacted separately.
- When a PowerShell verifier captures `Select-Object -First 1` through `@(...)`, index `[0]` before property checks. Otherwise helper functions can inspect the array wrapper and falsely report missing fields that are present on the selected object.
- Strict passive gates should require top-level `reportName` on refreshed provider-continuity reports, including failure paths. Without it, a valid fresh report can fail identity checks even when its continuity evidence is correct.
- Provider/protocol switch history tests should model tool results as next-request `function_call_output`, not only as prior response output. That proves the real Responses tool loop chain: previous assistant `function_call`, next request tool output, then the next provider turn.
- Connector/app-tool sync tests should prove nonempty directory content parity, not just directory existence or counts. Compare representative files byte-for-byte and compare whole-directory fingerprints while excluding Anti-Codex local manifest files.
