- App-tool migration scripts should report their own shared/local cache fingerprints and `content_matches`, not only copied top-level names. Outer refresh/verifier reports can catch drift, but the migration artifact itself should be self-diagnosing.
- Installed app-tool registries can predate new fingerprint fields even when the cache directory itself is current. MCP/Skill verification should reject missing registry fingerprints and content mismatch, then a bounded `sync-deepcodex-app-tools.mjs` refresh can repair the registry without granting connector authorization.
- New verifier evidence must survive the support path. For app-tool registry parity, expose `appTools.registry` through `mcpSkillEvidence` and have diagnostics verify the summary; otherwise the raw verifier can fail correctly while bundles and Manager summaries still hide the drift.
- Inherited skill fingerprinting must match the snapshot copier's exclusion rules. `sync-shared-codex-plugin-host.mjs` skips `.git` directories, so refresh-feature and MCP/Skill verifiers should also ignore `.git`; otherwise repository-backed skills can look changed even after a correct local copy.
- Full refresh-feature repair is expensive on this machine, around three minutes when it hashes and copies the 15.6k-file inherited surface. Prefer narrow connector/app-tool sync plus targeted report reruns, and only run full refresh when the local copy or source snapshot evidence actually needs repair.
- Log database protection should fail closed at product startup. A guard script that returns `false` but allows the desktop process to continue can still leave Anti-Codex exposed to the same TRACE write loop; startup should abort before launching the UI if trigger installation or WAL truncation cannot be proven.
- `installed-overall-fast` is not a cheap substitute for a synchronized installed runtime. If source UI contracts changed but installed runtime has not been synced, the UI-state portion can fail for stale Manager source; sync runtime first, then run fast overall with realistic MCP/plugin timeouts.
- Full `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1` and even `-VerifyOnly` can exceed 90 seconds on the inherited 15.6k-file surface because directory fingerprinting dominates. Do not use it as the routine way to refresh short-gate evidence; rerun the narrow MCP/Skill, plugin-capability, local-connector, and source-preflight reports instead.
- Cross-report inherited-source fingerprints should compare reports with the same source domain. MCP/Skill and plugin-capability reports snapshot the global Codex/agents inherited surface and should match each other; an older refresh-feature report can remain semantically green without being the latest short-gate snapshot.
- Anti-Codex `node_repl` MCP env values are intentionally rehomed into the isolated runtime. Parity checks should compare required env keys for `node_repl`, while still comparing non-rehomed MCP server env bodies strictly.
- Installed verifier auto-start should skip update checks. Otherwise a source/install verification can enter the sync/update path, fail to launch the desktop within a short timeout, and look like a product startup failure.
- `Get-FileHash` is too slow for refresh-feature inherited-surface verification on this Windows workspace. Use a streaming .NET SHA256 helper for per-file directory fingerprints and include `elapsedMs` in reports so future slowdowns are visible without rerunning noisy probes.
- PowerShell `Get-Content -Tail` objects can carry provider metadata when embedded inside JSON objects, producing huge diagnostic output. Cast tails to plain strings or extract structured fields directly before `ConvertTo-Json`.
- Source-snapshot generation can repeat the same large inherited-surface hashing across refresh, MCP/Skill, plugin/app, and preflight reports. Cache per-file SHA256 results by normalized source key plus `Length:LastWriteTimeUtc.Ticks`, preserve the final fingerprint from cached content hashes, and expose hit/miss counts so routine checks can prove reuse without weakening parity checks.
- After runtime sync, `test-anticodex-installed-overall.ps1 -Fast -TimeoutSec 45` can still exceed a 70-second outer timeout and write no `latest-installed-overall-fast.json`. Treat this as an oversized combined verifier for routine post-sync checks; prefer health plus narrow installed script/source checks unless the full fast report is explicitly needed.
- Refresh-feature verify-only has a separate directory-fingerprint hot path from source snapshots. Persist its file-hash cache under the Anti-Codex state cache and expose `hashCache.hitCount/missCount/entryCount`; a small temp-tree double run is enough to prove unchanged second runs are cache hits without scanning the real inherited tree.
- `translator/tests/sync-shared.plugin-host.test.mjs` can exceed a 30-second outer timeout when run as a whole. For narrow skill-sync regressions, use `--test-name-pattern` first, then reserve the full file for a larger verification budget.
- `test-anticodex-mcp-skills.ps1 -TimeoutSec 45` can exceed a 60-second outer timeout without writing `latest-mcp-skills.json` when it has to hash the inherited skill surface from cold. Cache its per-file hashes and expose `hashCache` evidence; use focused parser/source tests first, then rerun the full verifier with a realistic budget only when report evidence is required.
- Even after early mcp-skill hash-cache persistence, `test-anticodex-mcp-skills.ps1 -TimeoutSec 10` can exceed an 80-second outer timeout before writing a fresh report. The next slow path is after local fingerprinting, likely the real MCP smoke/list phase and source snapshot/report tail; add a total-budget boundary before relying on it as a routine short verifier.
- MCP/Skill verifier process cleanup can still outlive a successful report write. On 2026-06-23 it wrote `latest-mcp-skills.json` with `ok:true`, `capabilityState:"ready"`, `mcp.smoke.passed:true`, and all three MCP real smoke/list checks passing, while the outer command still timed out around 49 seconds. Treat the report as valid MCP evidence, but continue investigating the remaining PowerShell/child-process handle cleanup before using this verifier as a short interactive command.
- In PowerShell conditions, wrap function calls in parentheses before comparisons, including standalone `elseif` checks. `elseif (Get-RemainingVerifierBudgetMs -lt 5000)` calls the function with arguments and treats a nonzero return as truthy; use `elseif ((Get-RemainingVerifierBudgetMs) -lt 5000)`.
- Fast combined gates should not rerun heavyweight MCP/Skill verification when a fresh signed full report already proves `ok:true`, non-short-budget mode, real MCP smoke/list, skill fingerprints, and source snapshot evidence. Reuse that report explicitly and expose `coverageKind:"fresh-mcp-skills-report"` so the gate stays quick without hiding stale or degraded evidence.
- Apply the same fast-gate reuse rule to plugin capabilities only when the signed fresh report proves connector/app-tool parity is clean, no unauthorized or unattributed app tools are exposed, and source snapshot evidence exists. Otherwise rerun the verifier or fail; do not treat a generic `ok:true` as enough evidence.
- Plugin-capability fast reuse must also require GET/POST app-tool projection parity. A cached report is only acceptable when `appToolGetPostParity.checked:true` and both `missingFromGet` and `extraFromGet` are empty, otherwise Manager/API paths can drift while local cache parity still looks green.
- Sync summary fields should name the Anti-Codex local destination as `target` and the upstream Codex source as `sharedPath`. Reporting the source path as `target` makes successful local repairs look like they wrote back into the shared/native Codex home.
- Antigravity worker `ag-0fc71173-2f30-4fd6-a3b5-7871a7156dcd` touched files outside its two-file scope and stayed running after stop messages with empty logs. Do not cite that output as evidence; for tiny diagnostics/test edits, either give Antigravity one writable file or patch directly.
- `test-anticodex-mcp-skills.ps1 -TimeoutSec 5` must run in an explicit short-budget mode. Real MCP stdio smoke/list and inherited skill/source fingerprint refresh can each exceed the tiny budget; skip those heavy checks, reuse the previous full source snapshot when available, and emit a degraded report in seconds instead of letting the wrapper time out without a fresh report.
- DeepSeek worker model names must use the full CLI names such as `deepseek-v4-flash`; passing the short alias `v4-flash` fails before any read-only work starts.
- Cold relaunch is the only current optional passive gate gap and it is intentionally operator-triggered. It stops and restarts the isolated Anti-Codex host/translator, proves original Codex PIDs are unchanged, and writes `latest-cold-relaunch.json`; do not run it as a routine background check without explicit operator approval.
- High-impact verifier scripts need script-level confirmation, not only UI confirmation. Cold relaunch now requires `-ConfirmRelaunch`; direct accidental runs fail before stopping processes and do not overwrite `latest-cold-relaunch.json`.
- Optional cold-relaunch evidence must still fail closed when present but skipped. A no-confirm refusal report with `skipped:true` or `requiresConfirmation:true` is not restart evidence, even if a malformed artifact claims `ok:true`.
- Worker review jobs can surface useful audit text while still failing their own implementation success contract because no edits were produced. Treat that as review evidence only after direct local confirmation; do not cite empty Antigravity output as evidence.
- Local connector support evidence must include both endpoint safety and cache parity. Counts such as `exposedToolsPost` are not enough; summaries and diagnostics should also prove ChatGPT install URL scrubbing, metadata-only link endpoints, zero upstream requests, connector/app-tool parity, and GET/POST app-tool parity.
- Provider continuity gates should require nested route evidence as well as top-level booleans. For provider/protocol switches, the nested `continuationPreviousResponseId` in each switch block must exist and match the top-level chain, or a forged summary can look continuous while hiding the route that actually carried the request.
- DeepSeek review job `dsw_mqqymqym_qmqzoo` timed out with `caller_timeout_no_valid_changes`, changed no files, and produced no usable evidence. Retry review work with a materially smaller task or a separate worktree, and do not cite the failed job as audit support.
- Node `.mjs` verifiers must be launched with `node`, not `powershell.exe -File`. A mistaken PowerShell wrapper fails before product code runs and should be recorded as command construction failure, not release-gate evidence.
- Static MCP/Skill launcher contracts must reject the short-budget MCP skip strings, not require them. `-TimeoutSec 5` may skip heavy inherited-source fingerprint refresh by reusing the previous full snapshot, but it must still execute real `node_repl` smoke and worker `tools/list` checks.
- Anthropic upstream conversion must preserve explicit thinking configuration. If a request carries Anthropic-native `thinking` or Anti-Codex `thinking:"enabled"` plus `thinking_budget`, the Anthropic request body should keep that budget instead of silently dropping it during protocol switching.
- Provider protocol tests should cover Anthropic-assigned `tool_use.id` values, not only fixed synthetic OpenAI `tool_call_id` strings. The round-trip proof is that the converted OpenAI `tool_calls[0].id` returns as the later Anthropic `tool_result.tool_use_id`.
- Manager provider-continuity summaries should show both previous-response ID values and nested match booleans. Raw reports and diagnostics can prove `providerSwitchMatchesNested`, but operators using Manager need a visible `nestedMatches` line plus a `previousChain` line to debug route continuity without opening JSON.
- `test-anticodex-source-preflight.ps1` does not accept `-TimeoutSec`. Use an outer process timeout only; passing a non-existent parameter fails before product checks run and should be treated as command construction error.
- Release readiness must directly compare source runtime files against the installed Anti-Codex runtime, not only trust previous installed-overall or sync-readiness reports. A fresh green report can predate a later source patch; a zero-traffic hash gate catches that drift without restarting Anti-Codex or touching native Codex.
- After a full local feature refresh, the first MCP/Skill verifier run can still fail if its own 29k-file hash cache is cold, even though refresh-feature parity is already green. Rerun with a larger verifier budget after the cache warms; do not leave the degraded `latest-mcp-skills.json` as the active release-gate input.
- Antigravity worker job `ag-f69d2dd9-9ea2-4e29-84ad-904a7ea1d62b` timed out after 180 seconds with empty output. Treat empty Antigravity audit output as no evidence and retry with a much narrower single-file question if worker input is still needed.
- The refresh-feature file hash cache and MCP/Skill verifier file hash cache use compatible keys and metadata (`FullName` plus `Length:LastWriteTimeUtc.Ticks`). The MCP/Skill verifier can safely load `refresh-feature-file-hashes.json` as a fallback before recomputing hashes, so a full refresh can warm the first later MCP/Skill verifier run without weakening content parity.
- An isolated temp-state fallback probe can prove cache reuse even when real MCP stdio fails due missing temp MCP runtime: trust the cache fields (`fallbackHitCount`, `hitCount`, `missCount`) for fallback behavior, but do not treat that temp-state run as MCP readiness evidence.
- Installed runtime drift coverage should track report-producing verifier scripts and core translator modules, not only the launcher and a few high-level scripts. Otherwise a source patch to Manager injection, diagnostics, local connector verification, provider continuity, response history, or local projection can leave the installed Anti-Codex runtime stale while strict passive gates still report the old small runtime fingerprint as synced.
- Release-gate evidence must survive every support path, not only the raw `latest-*.json` report. When adding a gate such as `installedRuntimeSync`, surface its file counts, fingerprints, and mismatch samples through the summarizer, diagnostic bundle, and all Manager renderers, then verify the bundled summary directly.
- In PowerShell verifier code, wrap helper output in `@(...)` before indexing when a JSON field may be a single-item array. Otherwise pipeline unrolling can turn `["linked_tool"]` into a scalar and make `$value[0]` compare the first character instead of the first array item.
- Manager drift summaries must include extra-local residue, not only missing or changed shared items. For connector/app-tool parity, show `extraLocal` and GET/POST extra counts/samples so Anti-Codex-only cache records are visible without opening raw JSON.
- Large multi-file `apply_patch` hunks against generated or embedded Manager code are brittle because one escaped-string renderer can break the whole patch. Split these edits by file or by renderer after the first context miss, then rerun syntax checks before testing behavior.
- MCP/Skill app-tool registry parity should be enforced at the passive release gate, not only in the raw verifier and support summaries. If a stale or malformed report says `ok:true`, semantic checks must still reject missing registry evidence, content mismatch, fingerprint mismatch, or shared/local file-count mismatch.
- When Manager gains new evidence text, add it to `test-anticodex-ui-state.mjs` static Manager checks as well as launcher source contracts. The UI-state verifier is what keeps installed no-traffic checks from silently missing a removed Manager line.
- Same-Cascade resume needs a post-visible-event negative test in addition to pre-event retry coverage. Once a `text_delta`, replacement, tool-call, or done event has reached Codex, a later resume error must propagate instead of replaying the same native continuation.
- On Windows, never pass `translator\tests\*.mjs` as a literal search path to `rg`; use the directory plus `-g "*.mjs"`. The wildcard path form fails with OS error 123 and should be treated as command construction failure, not missing coverage.
- Standalone Manager button verifiers should treat HTTP 403 from `/anticodex/manager/action` as a possible stale action token after translator restart. Re-bootstrap the token once, then fail normally if the refreshed token also gets 403 so origin/token protections stay strict.
- Stale Manager action token handling must cover the actual injected and fallback Manager UIs, not only the standalone Manager button helper. Keep retries limited to one 403-triggered re-bootstrap, then fail normally so token and origin protections remain strict.
- Fallback Manager HTML patches are safer when anchored on ASCII identifiers such as endpoint paths and helper function names; escaped or localized text can make otherwise small patches brittle.
- When wrapping a JSON-producing Node verifier in `powershell.exe -Command`, avoid dense nested quotes for post-processing. A broken wrapper can fail with `The string is missing the terminator` before product code runs; rerun the verifier directly and record it as command construction failure only.
- Even a Manager client that bootstraps a token immediately before POST can race with a translator restart between those two requests. For `/anticodex/manager/action`, preserve HTTP status codes and retry exactly once on 403 with a fresh bootstrap; do not retry other failures.
- Scope Manager token retries to the action POST, not to the bootstrap request. A bootstrap 403 is an origin/trust failure and should surface directly; only a 403 from `/anticodex/manager/action` after a successful bootstrap is evidence of a possibly stale token race.
- Avoid clever regex searches through nested PowerShell quoting for routine audits. Use fixed-string `rg -F` or split searches, otherwise regex/quoting failures can waste time and produce no product evidence.
- Node `.mjs` verifiers in support paths should be launched with the Node executable directly. Static launcher contracts now reject PowerShell `-File`/`-Command` wrappers for release-gate and report-summarizer `.mjs` scripts so wrapper parsing failures do not masquerade as product evidence.
- Release-gate latest-report files should be treated as the source inventory for support visibility. Tests now derive that file set from release-gate definitions and require diagnostics bundles, Manager latest reports, and installed report-integrity checks to cover every gate file.
- Release-gate summary `effectiveOk` must be required by diagnostics, not optional. A summary with `ok:true` and `releaseReady:false` is effectively failing and support-bundle verification should fail if that field is missing.
- Missing-history recovery evidence is release-critical support context, not only a raw verifier detail. Surface HTTP 410, `response_history_missing`, `retryable:false`, previous-response match, recovery guidance, stable route state, and no-real-traffic coverage through the summarizer, diagnostics, and all Manager renderers.
- Do not embed PowerShell variable assignments and `& powershell.exe ...` inside a double-quoted `powershell.exe -Command` string from an already-running PowerShell shell. The outer shell expands `$out`/`$json` away and can leave a bare `&`; use escaped variables or a Node `spawnSync` wrapper for compact JSON post-processing.
- Diagnostic report evidence has two support hops: the Node summarizer emits fields, then `collect-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1` copies only allowlisted fields into `reports-summary.json`. When adding evidence, update both places or the verifier/Manager can see different support payloads.
- When bumping the injected Manager panel version, update the main injection script, open-injection version guard, sync-readiness version checks, UI-state expected version, and launcher source contracts together. A source-only bump leaves installed sync-readiness red until runtime sync is rerun.
- Static high-impact confirmation tests must track the actual Manager POST helper. After the Manager page moved to token-aware `managerPost`, anchoring the guard before old `getJson("/anticodex/manager/action")` produced a false self-test failure even though confirmation still ran before submission.
- Treat `anticodex-manager-open-inject.js` as part of the Manager runtime contract. It carries the panel version guard, so runtime sync required-file checks and installed runtime drift gates must track it alongside `anticodex-manager-inject.js` and the bridge.
- Installed runtime drift evidence should include sample filenames, not only counts. When source and installed runtime fingerprints diverge, Manager needs `missingSource`, `missingInstalled`, and `mismatch` samples so operators can identify the stale file without opening raw release-gate JSON.
- `rtk proxy` launches PATH executables, not PowerShell language commands. For bounded file reads through `Get-Content`, wrap it as `rtk proxy powershell.exe -NoProfile -Command "..."` or run native PowerShell directly.
- Inside an already-running PowerShell command string, `$($_.Line.Trim())` is expanded by the outer shell before the child `powershell.exe -Command` sees it. Use `rg`/fixed-string searches or escape `$` instead of nesting dense PowerShell expressions.
- `Select-Object -Index 320..380` can be parsed as a single string argument in nested command execution. Use `-Skip N -First M` for short source windows to avoid range-binding failures.
- On Windows PowerShell, `head` is not available by default. Use `Select-Object -First N` or `rg -m` for bounded command output.
- `--strict-optional-real-traffic` is not a passive release gate. If optional real-provider reports are intentionally `skipped:true`, that mode can return `releaseReady:false`; use the normal strict passive gate unless the operator explicitly approves refreshing optional real traffic.
- Do not search for stale verifier filenames such as `scripts/verify-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1`; the active diagnostics verifier is `scripts/test-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1`.
- `sync-anticodex-runtime.ps1 -NoRestart` may still copy runtime files and satisfy the source-vs-installed fingerprint gate while returning `ok:false` if the local Anti-Codex health endpoint is down. Treat the sync copy and the live health check as separate evidence.
- Account/proxy readiness reports contain both safe operational evidence and private account identity fields. Support summaries must use an explicit allowlist such as `diagnosisDetail`, `suggestedAction`, `localProxyBypassed`, `phaseSummary`, and `antigravityLogSignals`; never forward `account.fingerprint`.
- Old `latest-manager-real-buttons.json` artifacts can contain mojibake in nested UI text that makes PowerShell `ConvertFrom-Json` fail even when Node `JSON.parse` succeeds. Prefer the Node summarizer for support evidence and avoid parsing this raw report with PowerShell unless the writer is repaired.
- A fresh `latest-sync-readiness.json` can still be fast/offline evidence (`coverageKind:"installed-fast-no-traffic"`, `skipIconPaletteProbe:true`, Manager bridge process count `0`) even when installed runtime fingerprints are green. Release readiness should remain red because full sync-readiness semantics require full mode, installed no-traffic coverage, and Manager bridge readiness.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 does not accept `&&` as a command separator. Run sequential Git commands as separate shell calls instead of assuming newer PowerShell or POSIX shell syntax.
- Plugin/app capability evidence must carry the operational callable state, not just parity counts. Support summaries should show `capabilityState`, `degraded`, connector totals/enabled/access counts, POST/GET app-tool counts, `callableState`, `appToolsNote`, and `nativeCodexWriteback:false` so metadata-only connector projections cannot be mistaken for authorized callable tools.
- When using `powershell.exe -Command` through an already nested command string, even a simple trailing `"ok"` can lose quoting and run as a command. Prefer `Write-Output ([string]::new([char[]](111,107)))` or avoid decorative success output in parser checks.
- Local connector no-traffic support should show before/after upstream request counts as well as the delta. A delta of zero proves the endpoint path did not call out, while before/after values make accidental preexisting fake-provider requests visible during support triage.
- Prefer derived isolation booleans in summaries over raw temp paths. `sharedHomeUsed:false`, CODEX_HOME under the temp state root, shared home under the temp state root, and distinct local/shared homes prove isolation without exposing machine-specific paths in every Manager view.
- Provider-continuity Manager summaries should include actual route switches, not only previous-response IDs. Show provider switch from/to, protocol switch from/to plus provider/model, reverse switch from/to plus provider/model, reached booleans, structural tool evidence, and tool-history turn count so a correct ID chain cannot hide the route that carried it.
- Antigravity read-only audit job `ag-9a8bb3b6-3c08-47e9-83e4-e0f12c34ea84` timed out after 180 seconds without a usable result. Do not cite it as evidence; provider-continuity work in this batch is supported by direct local code review and the local fake third-party provider verifier instead.
- A fast/offline `latest-sync-readiness.json` can overwrite the latest full sync-readiness report even when a fresh full timestamped history report still exists. Release gates should use latest for routine files, but sync-readiness must be allowed to select a fresh signed full history report and surface `selectedFromHistory`/`selectedFile` so fast maintenance checks do not poison release evidence.
- `sync-anticodex-runtime.ps1 -NoRestart` through `rtk proxy` can exceed a 30-second wrapper timeout while still copying changed runtime files. Verify source-vs-installed hashes after the timeout before retrying or killing processes; do not terminate unrelated PowerShell/rtk processes without matching the exact command line.
- Whenever a release gate adds nested support evidence under `gates[]`, add the matching allowlisted summary field to both the Node summarizer and `test-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1`. Otherwise Manager may show raw release evidence while diagnostic verification silently drops the same support detail.
- Release-gate "missingRequired" can mean stale, missing, damaged, `ok:false`, or semantic failure. Always carry explicit `failureReasons` so operators do not waste time searching for a missing file when the actual blocker is an over-age signed report.
- A worker launched for read-only review may still report file changes if its workspace observes or repeats the host patch. Always inspect `git diff` after worker completion and treat the worker result as untrusted until the changed hunks are locally reviewed.
- Release-gate failure reasons need their own self-test guard, not only static launcher checks. Keep `gateFailureReasons` shared by self-test and runtime output, and assert every unsatisfied gate has both `failureReasons[]` and a primary `failureReason`.
- Antigravity read-only audit job `ag-a795c153-7302-4298-8384-0732a896350b` stayed running with empty stdout/stderr after the local task was already verified. Do not cite it as evidence; for small support-chain audits, prefer a direct local check plus a static verifier guard.
- In-app Manager must keep local connector allowlist and callable app-tool counts visibly separate. The live status card should show local allowlist metadata as preview only, gate callable wording on `accessibleConnectors > 0`, and never imply that a saved local allowlist authorizes connector calls.
- Provider-continuity support needs both threshold booleans and raw marker counts. `markerThresholds:true` proves the gate passed, but Manager and static contracts should also keep the `providerContinuity: turns=... / markers=15/16/18/20` line so operators can spot a weak or flattened history chain quickly.
- MCP/Skill short-budget support evidence must include both real MCP paths and skipped-heavy-work paths. Surface `mcpRealListCount`, `mcpRealListPassed`, `mcpRealListToolCount`, real list server names, `sourceSnapshot.reusedFromPreviousReport`, `refreshSkippedReason`, and `skills.fingerprintSkipped` through summaries, diagnostics, and Manager; otherwise a quick `ok:true` report can hide whether it really ran worker `tools/list`.
- App-tool registry parity is not clean when fingerprint or file-count evidence is absent. Require non-empty shared/local fingerprints plus present equal shared/local file counts before Manager or diagnostics call the registry clean.
- When running a child `powershell.exe -Command` from an already-running PowerShell shell, `$null` and other `$variables` in a double-quoted command are expanded by the outer shell. Use single-quoted child commands or escaped variables; otherwise a parser check can emit `= : The term '=' is not recognized` even though the script itself is valid.
- Old mojibake-heavy fallback Manager text in `translator/adaptive-server.mjs` is brittle patch context. Prefer ASCII anchors or the external/injected Manager renderers for small support-text changes unless the work explicitly includes repairing that encoded block.
- A direct diagnostics verifier run requires the local Anti-Codex gateway to be serving `http://127.0.0.1:8484`. If it is offline, `diagnostics request failed: ... unable to connect` is an environment/service-state result; keep using static diagnostics contracts and report summarizer checks unless the user approves starting or restarting the service.
- Plugin GET/POST app-tool parity needs an explicit `contentMatches:true` field. Empty `missingFromGet` and `extraFromGet` arrays are useful samples but should not be the sole clean signal in release gates or support summaries.
- After adding required report fields, old signed latest reports can become semantically stale even when fresh by timestamp. Do not patch report JSON by hand; either rerun the verifier or let passive gates fail with `semantic_failures` until real local evidence is regenerated.
- `test-anticodex-plugin-capabilities.ps1` can exceed short wrapper budgets when the local gateway is offline and the script also reaches source snapshot/parity checks. Do not leave timed-out verifier children running; identify by exact `-File scripts\test-anticodex-plugin-capabilities.ps1` command line before stopping.
- When stopping a timed-out Windows process, do not match only on a broad command fragment that also appears in the cleanup command itself. Include `-File <script>` or the exact PID captured from a previous process listing, otherwise the cleanup command can terminate itself.
- Short-budget plugin capability verification must fail closed. It may reuse a previous source snapshot as evidence of provenance, but it must not reuse previous connector/app-tool authorization or callable counts as if they were freshly verified.
- Release gates should reject plugin-capability reports with `shortBudgetMode:true`, degraded verification states, unchecked parity, or unchecked smoke even if a malformed report sets `ok:true` and carries empty drift arrays.
- Plugin/app parity summaries must treat `checked:false` as not clean even when drift arrays are empty. Short-budget degraded reports intentionally emit empty samples with unchecked parity, so support views must show `connectorChecked/appToolChecked/getPostChecked` and source-snapshot reuse instead of implying a fresh clean scan.
- MCP/Skill short-budget mode must skip local HTTP health/plugin/connector/app-tool endpoints when the gateway is offline. Keep real MCP stdio smoke/list and local cache/registry checks, but do not let `/health` or `/backend-api/...` retries stretch a 5-second verifier into a 28-second degraded report.
- Source snapshot mismatch is a stale-report problem, not necessarily a missing file or broken capability. Manager summaries should translate `*_source_snapshot_mismatch` into a plain refresh hint while still showing the exact semantic failure code for debugging.
- Plugin capability fake-endpoint tests must keep connector authorization false. It is useful to prove the HTTP and cache-parity paths with a loopback fake server, but the verifier should still report `metadata_only_no_accessible_connectors` and zero exposed app tools unless a real authorization path exists.
- In-process fake HTTP servers cannot answer while a Node test is blocked in `spawnSync`. Use async child process execution when the child is expected to call back into the test's loopback server.
- PowerShell may unwrap a single-element JSON array returned by `Invoke-RestMethod`, so `.Count` can become `$null` even when one object exists. Fake endpoint fixtures should use at least two records when they are testing array-count paths rather than scalar handling.
- MCP/Skill short-budget cleanup tests should match the temp fake MCP script path or the exact verifier script plus temp root, not broad `node` or `powershell` command fragments. This proves no child leak without risking unrelated user or system processes.
- Real-traffic release gates need user-facing guidance separate from ordinary missing reports. A stale or missing real-traffic report means explicit high-cost verification was not refreshed, not that the underlying feature is absent.
- Log DB guard evidence must preserve the no-traffic contract as well as trigger/protection state. Release gates should reject bad noTraffic/usedRealTraffic/coverageKind and invalid WAL sizes; diagnostics should not accept non-finite or negative WAL evidence.
- Source snapshot mismatch should direct operators to rerun the normal MCP/Skill and plugin capability verifiers, not to patch `latest-*.json` by hand. The mismatch is provenance drift between reports, not standalone evidence of a missing capability.
- Nested PowerShell launched from an already-running PowerShell shell can expand `$out`, `$err`, and `$p` before the child command runs, leaving `=Join-Path` or `=Start-Process` parser failures. Prefer direct focused commands or escape variables in the child `-Command` string.
- Plugin/app fake endpoint tests should include at least two app tools when asserting PowerShell-generated counts. A single returned tool can be unwrapped as a scalar and serialize count fields as null, hiding the actual fail-closed behavior being tested.
- Diagnostics should explicitly reject short-budget plugin capability reports and `degraded_short_budget` evidence. Short-budget plugin checks are useful interactive smoke reports, but they are not release-grade proof of connector/app-tool parity.
- An interrupted local feature refresh can leave the Anti-Codex connector cache one large JSON behind the shared Codex cache. In that state `latest-refresh-features.json` fails only connector parity while app-tools parity passes; rerun the local snapshot sync first, then `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1 -VerifyOnly` to prove connector/app-tool fingerprints match.
- Do not treat offline `/health` and connector endpoint failures as missing MCP/Skill or plugin functionality. When the local gateway is offline, MCP/Skill and plugin verifiers can still prove filesystem parity but cannot produce release-grade Manager/API endpoint evidence until Anti-Codex is running.
- Refresh-feature parity reports should include bounded missing/extra/changed file samples for connector and app-tool directories. Directory fingerprints alone prove drift but do not tell the operator whether the fix is rerun sync, remove a stale local file, or wait for a shared cache write to settle.
- Failed latest release reports need the same selected-report detail as history fallbacks. Carry `selectedKind`, `selectedFile`, `selectedMtime`, and bounded `reportFailureSamples` through passive gates, summaries, diagnostics, and Manager text so a stale or semantically bad report is actionable without hand-opening JSON.
- MCP/Skill short-budget source snapshot reuse must not trust only `latest-mcp-skills.json`. A degraded short-budget latest report can overwrite a previously good latest report, so reuse should scan recent MCP/Skill history and refresh-feature reports for a complete `sourceSnapshot`; hash cache files are not provenance evidence.
- Cross-report source snapshot mismatch is an attribution check, not a primary failure for every report with a different fingerprint. Only add the mismatch when both compared reports are otherwise satisfied; an offline or degraded plugin capability report should not drag a good MCP/Skill report into `semantic_failures`.
- Plugin-capability release evidence needs complete, fresh source provenance. A report with only `sourceSnapshot.fingerprint`, missing `fileCount` or `sourceMtimeMax`, or `reusedFromPreviousReport:true` may be useful for short-budget support context but must not satisfy release gates.
- Local gateway offline plugin failures should be translated once and carried through the support chain. Preserve the raw `/health` or endpoint failure samples, but also attach a stable `local_gateway_offline` guidance line so Manager, diagnostics, and summaries do not make operators infer the next step from a generic `ok:false`.
- PowerShell functions returning a one-item array can still unwrap at the assignment site. For plugin capability endpoint payloads, wrap `Get-ArrayItems ...` calls in `@(...)` at the caller, not only inside the helper, or `appToolCount`/`connectorCount` can serialize as `null`.
- Parser checks launched through nested PowerShell or `node -e` are easy to break by outer-shell `$` and quote expansion. Prefer a direct PowerShell `-Command` with backtick-escaped variables, and treat malformed wrapper errors as command construction failures until a clean parser check is rerun.
- `rtk proxy` runs executables, not PowerShell built-in cmdlets. For `Get-Content` or similar cmdlets, invoke `powershell.exe -NoProfile -Command ...` through `rtk proxy` instead of passing the cmdlet name directly.
- `apply_patch` resolves relative paths from the current thread workspace, not necessarily the repo subdirectory. In nested projects, use the full repo path or verify the working directory before patching project notes.
- Real-traffic release-gate failures need explicit approval guidance, not just `stale_report` or `missing_report`. A passive gate may explain that the next proof requires starting/verifying Anti-Codex and refreshing real-traffic reports, but it must not run those verifiers by itself.
- Keep git staging and commit as separate commands in this project. Chaining them with shell separators may succeed, but it weakens the review point between staged diff inspection and commit creation.
- Manager latest-report text has three maintained surfaces: external bridge, injected manager script, and adaptive-server embedded copy. When adding release-gate summary fields such as `nextActions`, update all three or the static launcher contract catches the drift.
- Release-gate numeric evidence checks should not rely only on `Number(value)`: `Number(null)` becomes `0` and can hide a missing report field. For required report fields such as log scan counts, require the original value to be a finite number before accepting it.
- Release-gate refresh actions must cover semantic failures as well as stale/missing reports. A report can be fresh and `ok:true` but fail the stronger field-level semantics; the passive gate should still show the correct verifier action instead of leaving `orderedActions` empty.
- Parser probes launched through `rtk proxy powershell.exe -Command` still expand `$p` and similar variables in the outer PowerShell context. For parser checks, use literal paths directly inside `ReadAllText(...)` or escape variables explicitly.
- `sync-anticodex-runtime.ps1 -NoRestart` still waits for `/health` after copying files; with the default `-TimeoutSec 45`, a 30-second outer wrapper can time out even though the copy path is healthy. For offline runtime sync, pass a short `-TimeoutSec` and then verify installed-runtime hashes with the passive release gate.
- A normal-budget `test-anticodex-plugin-capabilities.ps1 -TimeoutSec 8` can still exceed a 60-second outer wrapper when the gateway is offline because multiple endpoint probes each consume retry time before the cached parity/source work can finish. Do not use it as an offline refresh path; rely on `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1` evidence for metadata parity and run plugin capabilities only after the gateway is approved/running.
- `refreshFeatures` can prove inherited connector/app-tool metadata parity while `pluginCapabilities` remains red because live gateway endpoints are unavailable. Surface that as auxiliary offline evidence, but do not let it satisfy the gateway-backed plugin capability gate.
- Optional support fields in summarized release-gate failures should be omitted when absent, not serialized as `null`; existing tests and consumers compare stable JSON shapes for ordinary gates.
- Real-traffic release actions are also gateway-dependent. Mark them with both `requiresGatewayStart:true` and `requiresRealTraffic:true`; otherwise approval plans can falsely suggest a stale real-traffic verifier can run while the Anti-Codex service is offline.
- Release-gate support summaries should include stable approval counts and failure codes, not only raw localized error text. Keep raw samples for debugging, but show normalized codes such as `local_gateway_offline` and `gateway_endpoint_unreachable` so mojibake or OS-language differences do not hide the next action.
- If diagnostics collectors read a support field directly from raw `latest-release-gates.json`, the raw report must emit that field too. Do not rely only on `summarize-anticodex-report.mjs` deriving it later, or bundles can silently omit evidence such as installed runtime sync proof.
- In this launcher test file, `node --test --test-name-pattern` may still execute unrelated top-level tests and surface transient failures outside the intended focus. Treat the printed named subtests and a rerun of the affected test as the evidence, not the selector string alone.
- Offline support verifiers must validate guidance semantics, not just field presence. For `nextActions` and `approvalPlan`, require explicit approval blocking for gateway, real-traffic, and high-impact actions, and make sure summary counts cannot understate those risks.
- Missing approval-plan count fields are as risky as low counts. Reject absent `blockedCount`, `requiresGatewayStartCount`, `requiresRealTrafficCount`, and `highImpactCount` instead of treating missing fields as unknown-but-acceptable.
- In PowerShell, casting `$null` to `[int]` can silently become `0`. For report integrity fields, validate non-null non-negative integers before comparing counts.
- JavaScript has the same trap through `Number(null) === 0`. Manager/report summary code should use a helper that rejects null and requires a non-negative integer before displaying count fields.
- Plugin offline snapshot evidence is only useful support context when it is tied to a satisfied refresh-feature gate. Require both `refreshGateSatisfied:true` and a non-empty selected refresh report name before diagnostics accepts it as clean offline evidence.
- Manager support text should not hide approval metadata behind release failure details. Display top-level next actions and approval plans even when `releaseGateFailureReasons` is empty, and merge gate-level ordered actions so stale top-level lists do not drop blocked work.
- Keep diagnostics support-summary validation paths in lockstep. `-VerifyReportsSummary` and diagnostic-bundle verification both consume `reports-summary.json`; any new release-gate evidence rule, especially approval-plan counts or plugin offline snapshot provenance, must be enforced in both paths or factored into a shared helper.
- Do not run runtime sync and passive release-gate checks in parallel. The gate can snapshot installed-runtime hashes while sync is still copying files and report a transient `installedRuntimeSync` mismatch; run sync first, then the passive gate.
- Approval metadata should not be trusted when the action command itself proves a higher-risk operation. Diagnostics support checks should reject known gateway, real-traffic, and high-impact verifier commands whose `requiresGatewayStart`, `requiresRealTraffic`, `highImpact`, or explicit approval block flags have been understated.
- A no-traffic gate needs explicit no-traffic attestation even when every subcheck is green. Source-preflight reports must carry `noTraffic:true`, `usedRealTraffic:false`, `behaviorCovered:true`, and a dedicated `coverageKind`, otherwise a forged report can pass semantic checks while using real traffic.
- When extracting compact release-gate fields, avoid nested one-liners that mix outer PowerShell quotes with child PowerShell or `node -e` quoting. Use escaped PowerShell variables or a checked script path; malformed wrappers can strip string literals and produce parser or `require(...)` errors unrelated to the project.
- In this repository, a `node --test --test-name-pattern` run against `translator/tests/anticodex-launcher.test.mjs` can still take around 35-40 seconds and effectively exercise the full file. Avoid a 30-second wrapper for that file when adding diagnostics fixtures, and record the actual pass/skip counts instead of assuming the selector made it tiny.
- Broad WMI/CIM process command-line queries can exceed short budgets on this machine. Prefer narrow `Get-Process` checks first, and only query exact command lines when a specific child PID or process pattern is already known.
- Release-gate next actions must cover every required failed gate, not only the gates that currently fail in the local environment. Self-tests should assert required-failed-gate action coverage, because otherwise a future stale no-traffic or short gate can appear red without an operator-safe refresh command.
- In PowerShell-facing report actions, `%APPDATA%` is not a safe path placeholder. Use `$env:APPDATA` inside a PowerShell command wrapper or avoid environment expansion in the reported command.
- Whenever Manager support text truncates a list for readability, add an explicit hidden count. `nextActions` already used `(+N more)`; `failureReasons`, `reportFailures`, `guidance`, and `semanticFailures` need the same pattern so later gate failures do not disappear from support views.
- If `rtk proxy powershell.exe` returns a wrapper-level `Access is denied` for a read-only compact query, rerun the same bounded query directly in PowerShell and record that `rtk` interfered. Do not treat that wrapper failure as product evidence.
- Release-required real-traffic reports need field-level semantics, not only `ok:true`, freshness, and `usedRealTraffic:true`. Post-verification must prove completed state, ready wait state, no failures, and release-ready installed overall evidence; Codex Responses must prove completion plus final marker and Chinese answer evidence.
- Approval-plan support text should show blocked count, required short/release counts, and phase count, not only total actions and risk classes. Otherwise support views can hide how much of the remaining work is actually approval-blocked or release-required.
- Installed-runtime support text should show `satisfied` and both source/installed max mtimes, not just file counts and fingerprint status. File counts can match while runtime drift still exists; the timestamps make stale sync versus real mismatch visible without opening raw JSON.
- Release-gate failure summaries should carry report age, freshness, history selection, and required flags in the compact Manager line. A bare `gate:stale_report` forces operators to open raw JSON to tell stale latest reports from selected full-history evidence or optional blockers.
- Tool-loop release gates must not trust `ok:true` alone. Require report-name and real-traffic attestations plus the loop-specific evidence: call IDs, non-pseudo native IDs, preserved markers/results, real MCP smoke output, and custom input preservation.
- Web, compaction, and cancellation real-traffic gates need behavior-specific proof. Web reports need fetched evidence and no protocol leakage; compaction reports need seed continuity through compact summary and resume turns; cancellation reports need created/cancelled SSE events and no completed/failed state.
- High-impact real task gates need artifact/executor evidence, not just a successful final sentence. `realMiniTool` should prove the generated CLI file exists, ran, and matched its marker; `realWork` should additionally prove the real MCP smoke path observed its marker.
- Semantic release-gate failures should offer a report-only diagnostic action before any gateway or real-traffic action. Deduplicate that action globally so it does not crowd out later required actions such as final post-verification.
- `reportOnlyCount` is not enough by itself. Diagnostics should also verify the matching `nextActions[].reportOnly` marker, semantic-failure `releaseGateReportOnlyCheck` presence, and phase-level `reportOnly` summaries so a dropped display flag cannot look clean.
- Release support validation must cover both top-level `nextActions` and nested gate `orderedActions`. Manager merges both for display, so nested actions need the same gateway/real-traffic/high-impact/approval metadata checks even though they should not affect `approvalPlan` counts.
- Approval phase summaries should have a cap larger than the release-gate phase inventory. A 12-item cap hid final/optional phases when all phases were present; keep the cap bounded but high enough to preserve the current full phase list.
- Missing-history verification is no-traffic but still gateway-dependent. It calls the local `/health`, `/v1/responses`, and model-route endpoints, so release-gate actions and diagnostics command-risk checks must mark `test-anticodex-missing-history.mjs` with `requiresGatewayStart:true` and explicit approval blocking when the gateway is offline.
- A raw `fetch failed` from `test-anticodex-missing-history.mjs` means the local gateway was unreachable before the 410 contract could be exercised. Preserve endpoint context, stable `local_gateway_offline` / `gateway_endpoint_unreachable` codes, and operator guidance in the report instead of leaving support views with an unclassified transport error.
- Diagnostics should fail when required support summaries are absent, not only when present summaries are malformed. `latest-third-party-provider.json` and `latest-missing-history.json` must have explicit missing-summary failures so a sparse bundle cannot silently hide provider continuity or missing-history evidence.
- Sync-readiness is read-only, but a `source_codex_changed` / source-active hash / guardrail failure means the actual fix is host sync. Release-gate support should still offer the read-only recheck, but it must also surface `syncHost` as `highImpact:true` and approval-blocked with host-sync guidance because `sync-anticodex-launcher.ps1` builds and may replace the Anti-Codex host after candidate validation.
- Stale gateway-only release gates need support guidance even when there is no endpoint error sample. Use the ordered action metadata (`requiresGatewayStart:true` and no real traffic) rather than group names alone, so Manager and no-traffic stale reports tell operators to restore the local gateway instead of looking like unexplained feature failures.
- Optional high-impact release actions also need guidance. Even when they are not release-blocking, stale soak and missing cold-relaunch reports should say why they remain approval-only; otherwise support views show executable high-impact actions without the reason for not running them automatically.
- Support bundle validation should reject action-only release failures. If `releaseGateFailureReasons[].orderedActions` is present but `guidance` is blank, the bundle preserved the button/command but lost the operator reason; fail it offline before Manager or diagnostics presents an unexplained action.
- Optional real-provider gates can be actionless by design, but they still need guidance. A stale real third-party provider report should say that any rerun needs explicit approval and an environment-variable API key; a stale provider-to-Antigravity return report should say it performs a real Antigravity return turn.
- `optionalRealTrafficFailures` is a support surface, not only a boolean list. Diagnostics should require each listed optional failure to have matching failure details and guidance, otherwise the bundle can say optional real traffic is not ready without preserving the approval/key/real-turn warning.
- `releaseGateFailures` must stay joined to `releaseGateFailureReasons`. A bare failed gate id is not enough support evidence; diagnostics should fail summaries that drop the reason object because that object carries guidance, samples, semantic failures, and ordered actions.
- `missingRequired` must also stay joined to `releaseGateFailureReasons`. A next action proves there is something to run, but the reason object explains why the release blocker exists and what approval/guidance applies.
- `missingShort` and `missingOptional` must stay joined to `releaseGateFailureReasons` too. They may not require default next actions, but support summaries need the reason object so operators keep guidance, freshness, and selected-report context for short and optional gates.
- `damagedReports` needs the same reason-detail join as failed and missing gates. A damaged list entry without `releaseGateFailureReasons` tells operators something is broken but drops whether the problem is parse, signature, hash, stale selection, or another report-specific failure.
- Host-sync release failures should carry stable sample codes. Normalize `source_codex_changed` / installed-Codex-changed samples to `host_sync_required` so diagnostics and Manager do not depend on localized raw text to explain a high-impact sync action.
- Approval plans should match `nextActions` exactly, not only avoid undercounting risk. Reject over-reported gateway/real-traffic/high-impact/blocked totals too, and validate phase summaries only when a real `phases` property is present so old compact summaries do not become false failures.
- Clean plugin offline parity is weak without source provenance. Diagnostics should require the refresh-feature-backed offline snapshot to include fingerprint, file count, and max mtime so support views can distinguish a current parity proof from a hand-trimmed or stale summary.
- Passive release gates must stay offline by construction. Static contracts should reject networking and process entry points such as Node `http/https/net/child_process` imports and PowerShell HTTP cmdlets, while still allowing inert local URL strings in evidence checks.
- `releaseGateGroups` is a support index, not a second source of truth. Any group-level missing required/optional id must also exist in the top-level missing list and matching failure details; required group misses also need a next action.
- Top-level `nextActions` can be capped for readability, but truncation must be explicit. Emit available, hidden, and truncated fields alongside the visible action list, and have diagnostics validate the numbers so support bundles cannot silently hide later actions.
- Manager support text should prefer report-provided next-action hidden/available counts over recomputing from the compact list. Once `nextActions` has already been capped upstream, recomputing from visible actions can understate how many release actions are hidden.
- Sync-readiness summaries must distinguish installed Codex source evidence from the active Anti-Codex host evidence. Prefer `updateStatus.source.packageVersion` for the source version, preserve active/source host and CLI hashes, and show the hash-match booleans so host-sync drift is obvious without opening raw JSON.
- Any support path that accepts `syncReadinessEvidence` should reject partial drift evidence. Version numbers without `updateStatusReason` and source/active host+CLI hash pairs can still hide whether the repair is a high-impact host sync or a stale summary.
- Release group summaries must surface required missing gates as well as optional ones. `releaseGateGroups` is an index over the actionable top-level evidence, so Manager text and diagnostics JSON should preserve `missingRequired` instead of only showing `missingOptional`.
- Installed-runtime support evidence is only clean when all drift lists are present and empty. `satisfied:true` plus matching fingerprints is not enough if `missingSourceFiles`, `missingInstalledRuntimeFiles`, or `mismatchedRuntimeFiles` has been stripped or still contains entries.
- Read-only worker instructions must explicitly forbid restoring or cleaning the shared main worktree. A worker that tries to "restore" files can erase the main agent's uncommitted in-progress edits even when the last committed checkpoint remains safe.
- Hidden next-action counts must be preserved at both producer and display layers. Updating Manager text to read `nextActionsHiddenCount` is not enough if an embedded summarizer path drops `nextActionsAvailableCount`, `nextActionsHiddenCount`, or `nextActionsTruncated`.
- Approval-plan counts must be based on the full ordered action set, not the visible capped `nextActions` list. Once `nextActions` is truncated, diagnostics can only validate hidden-aware counts against `nextActionsAvailableCount` and visible-action lower bounds.
- Approval phase details are support evidence, not decoration. Showing only the phase count hides whether the remaining queue is report-only, gateway-start, real-traffic, high-impact, or approval-blocked.
- Per-gate `failureReasons` and `semanticFailures` need producer-level available/hidden counts plus display-level `(+N more)` text. If a summarizer keeps the full array, its hidden count should stay zero; only paths that actually slice the array should report hidden items, or Manager text can double-count hidden semantic failures.
- Any hidden-count display field added to Manager should also be validated by diagnostics bundle checks. Otherwise the live Manager can look honest while exported support evidence silently drops or misstates the available/hidden counts.
- If diagnostics validates a support-summary field, the original producer should emit it too when feasible. Relying only on `summarize-anticodex-report.mjs` to derive fields later creates a gap between raw latest reports and exported summaries.
- Keep producer and summarizer support shapes compatible during migrations. For release gates, emitting both legacy `groups` and explicit `releaseGateGroups` lets older consumers keep working while newer diagnostics can validate the support array directly.
- History-selected report evidence should be emitted at the producer level. Summarizer fallback from per-gate fields is useful for old reports, but raw release-gate reports should directly carry `releaseGateSelectedReports` when they depend on history fallback.
- Support-field migrations must update both summarizers: the standalone `summarize-anticodex-report.mjs` and adaptive-server's embedded Manager summarizer. Updating only one leaves installed Manager text behind source report JSON.
- The launcher contract test file can exceed the interactive 30-second feedback budget even with a narrow `--test-name-pattern`, because full-file setup still runs. For one-field embedded summarizer migrations, use syntax plus a targeted static contract check first, and reserve the full launcher file for checkpoint/release verification.
- Top-level release-gate `nextActions` should carry the same `reason` and `guidance` as their source gate. Otherwise Manager can show the command order while hiding why an action is needed or why it is approval-blocked.
- Avoid PowerShell `>` redirection when a Node verifier output will be parsed again by Node; Windows PowerShell can write UTF-16 text that makes `JSON.parse` fail. Let Node write the UTF-8 file or parse stdout directly.
- Diagnostics should not require a specific gate, such as sync-readiness, to have selected-history evidence unless that gate is actually marked `selectedFromHistory:true`. Selected-report details are proof of history fallback, not a mandatory field for every fresh latest report.
- Report summaries must not truncate `releaseGateFailureReasons` below the number of release-gate ids that diagnostics cross-checks against `missingRequired`, `missingShort`, `missingOptional`, and group missing lists. Keep per-gate samples/reason lists compact, but preserve enough gate-level entries for support validation.
- High-risk or approval-blocked top-level release actions need both `guidance` and `reason`. Guidance tells the operator what to do; reason links the action back to stale/missing/semantic failure state so summaries can be audited without expanding raw gate details.
- Gate-level `orderedActions` need the same `guidance` and `reason` as top-level `nextActions`. When top-level actions are capped or reconstructed from failure details, support views still need the operator instruction and machine-auditable failure cause.
- When syncing runtime and immediately checking installed-runtime fingerprints, run the passive fingerprint check after the sync command returns. Running them in parallel can produce a transient mismatch even though the copy completes moments later.
- Manager latest-report action text has three live renderers: external bridge, injected Manager script, and adaptive-server embedded fallback. When one adds action support fields such as `actionReasons` or `actionGuidance`, update all three and keep the static launcher contract pointed at the shared markers.
- Windows PowerShell 5.1 does not accept `&&` as a statement separator. For grouped syntax checks in this repo, run separate commands in parallel or use a PowerShell-native wrapper instead of Unix-style chaining.
- Report-only release-gate actions are still operator-facing support actions. Even though they do not need gateway, real traffic, high impact approval, or explicit blocking, diagnostics should reject them if they lose `reason` or `guidance`.
- A short-readiness miss without a matching next action is a broken support bundle, even when the failure reason details are present. Operators need the action object to distinguish read-only checks from gateway-start or real-traffic work.
- Keep short-gate action coverage checked at both layers: producer self-test should prove failed short gates emit actions, and diagnostics should reject exported summaries that drop those actions.
- Missing-gate failure detail is incomplete without `guidance`. Diagnostics should reject missing required, short, and optional gate details that keep the failure reason but lose the operator instruction.
- Do not use `node --check` on `.ps1` scripts. Use the PowerShell parser for PowerShell files; Node reports `ERR_UNKNOWN_FILE_EXTENSION`, which is only a command-selection error.
- Selected-history support details need freshness evidence. If `releaseGateFailureReasons` marks a gate as `selectedFromHistory:true`, diagnostics should require the matching `releaseGateSelectedReports` entry to preserve `selectedMtime`, not just the selected file name.
- Approval-plan required counts are support evidence. Diagnostics should require `requiredForShortCount` and `requiredForReleaseCount` and bound them against action evidence, just like gateway, real-traffic, high-impact, report-only, and blocked counts.
- Missing-gate failure details also need required markers. A missing required or short gate without `requiredForRelease:true` / `requiredForShort:true`, or a missing optional gate without an explicit non-release-required marker, can make support views understate which work is release-blocking.
- Release-gate group summaries need count integrity. Diagnostics should reject missing or inconsistent `total`, `satisfied`, `required`, `requiredSatisfied`, `optional`, and `optionalSatisfied` fields so Manager group summaries cannot display untrusted totals.
- Do not pass PowerShell parser one-liners with `$tokens`, `$errors`, `[ref]$tokens`, or `> $null` through `rtk proxy` unless the dollars are escaped for both shells. The wrapper can strip variables and create a parser error unrelated to the project; run the parser directly or use a checked script.
- Semantic-failure support evidence has two surfaces. If `releaseGateFailures[].semanticFailures` is present, diagnostics should require a matching `releaseGateFailureReasons[].failureReasons` entry with `semantic_failures` and require the report-only next action even if the reason marker was stripped.
- Optional real-traffic readiness must be self-consistent. A support bundle should fail offline if `optionalRealTrafficReady:true` still lists optional failures, or if strict optional real-traffic is enabled while `releaseReady:true` hides that optional evidence is not ready.
- `effectiveOk` must be tied to release readiness and report integrity. Diagnostics should reject `effectiveOk:true` when `releaseReady` is not true or when `damagedReports` is non-empty.
- Release-gate ready booleans must be tied to their missing lists. Diagnostics should reject `shortReady:true` with non-empty `missingShort`, and `releaseReady:true` with non-empty `missingRequired`.
- `test-anticodex-plugin-capabilities.ps1 -TimeoutSec 20` previously did not bound the entire offline verifier path; one refresh attempt exceeded 49 seconds without leaving a verifier process behind. The script now caps per-request waits and writes a degraded timeout-budget report before local parity/source-snapshot work when the remaining budget is too low.
- Verifier request timeouts must be total-budget aware when they retry. `test-anticodex-tool-bridge-mode.ps1` now caps each diagnostics request to the remaining verifier budget and keeps sleeps inside that same budget.
- `test-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1` had the same retry-budget risk on `/anticodex/diagnostics`: two attempts could each consume the full `TimeoutSec`. It now uses a verifier-wide timer to cap request timeouts and sleeps, with a silent-endpoint fixture proving it exits with JSON.
- `test-anticodex-ui-idle-stability.ps1` must own the timeout for child UI-state checks. Passing `-TimeoutSec` to the child is not enough; the parent now starts the child with redirected temp files, waits on a bounded timer, kills only that child on timeout, and caps idle sleeps to the remaining duration.
- Diagnostics bundle verification must also own child-process timeout. `Invoke-JsonScript` now starts collector children with redirected temp files, waits only for the remaining verifier budget, and kills only that child on timeout. A fixture needs enough budget for the pre-bundle diagnostics request before it can prove child timeout behavior.
- Do not put PowerShell variables such as `$attempt` inside double-quoted `rg` regex commands. PowerShell expands them before `rg` sees the pattern and can create invalid regex text; split the search into simpler literal commands or quote the pattern so variables are preserved.
- When using `rtk proxy powershell.exe -Command` from an already-PowerShell thread, any `$p`, `$_`, or `$env:*` in the command string can be eaten by the outer shell before the child PowerShell runs. Prefer direct literal paths or Node probes for compact JSON/path checks.
- `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1` has no `-TimeoutSec` parameter. A full refresh can exceed a 60-second wrapper while still copying useful local snapshots; use `-VerifyOnly` with a larger wrapper for parity evidence, or a narrow skills-only sync path when only skills drifted.
- PowerShell does not support bash heredoc syntax such as `node - <<'NODE'`. Use `node -e` with a compact one-liner, or create a checked helper script when the probe is too large.
- Do not pass `%APPDATA%` as a literal argument to Node scripts from PowerShell. Build report paths from `process.env.APPDATA` or pass `$env:APPDATA` only through a correctly escaped PowerShell wrapper.
- `summarize-anticodex-report.mjs` takes the report JSON path as its first positional argument; it does not accept `--report`. Passing `--report` makes Node try to open a file literally named `--report`.
- Avoid JavaScript template literals inside `node -e` commands launched from PowerShell; backticks are PowerShell escapes and can corrupt the inline script before Node sees it. Use string concatenation or a checked helper script.
- Direct `summarize-anticodex-report.mjs` output does not add the diagnostic collector's `file` field. When building a temporary `reports-summary.json` outside `collect-anticodex-diagnostics.ps1`, add `{ file: latestReportName, ...summary }` before running diagnostics summary validation.
- Manager real-button checks must pass explicit verifier budgets through the Manager action definition. `mcpSkills` and `pluginCapabilities` default to short script-level budgets when no `-TimeoutSec` is supplied, which can overwrite a previously good latest report with a degraded timeout-budget report.
- MCP/Skill release-gate skill counts must account for duplicate skill names across shared `.codex\skills` and `.agents\skills`. A non-conflicting duplicate is expected to produce one local copy, so gate semantics should compare the de-duplicated expected count with the local skill count and separately fail only conflicting duplicate names.
- PowerShell array parameters after `-File` can be passed as one comma-containing string and fail `ValidateSet` before script code can split it. For scripts that accept `[string[]]`, use a script block with an explicit array or pass separate values in a form PowerShell binds as an array.
- Manager bridge token retries must use the freshly returned token variable all the way into the action request header. A stale reference such as `bootstrap.token` inside `postManagerActionWithToken(token)` throws `bootstrap is not defined` only at real-click time and makes otherwise valid Manager actions fail.
- Manager bridge fixes that change persistent page globals need a native panel version bump. Otherwise an already-open Anti-Codex page can keep the old `__ANTICODEX_RUN_MANAGER_ACTION__` implementation even after the runtime files are synced.
- Manager UI action timeouts must be at least as large as the real local verifier runtime. MCP/Skill and plugin capability checks can take about 60-70 seconds; a 28-second UI timeout marks the real click as "still running" and makes the Manager button verifier fail even though the backend action eventually succeeds.
- Manager real-button verification should keep long actions isolated by process. A single `--actions pluginCapabilities,mcpSkills` helper run can leave the second action with stale or incomplete Manager state even when both actions pass as separate real clicks.
- Run plugin/app-tool capability before MCP/Skill in the standalone Manager real-button gate. Shared connector metadata can drift in the background, so the plugin parity check should run as soon as possible after a local feature refresh; MCP/Skill is less sensitive to connector-cache churn.
- Do not treat connector metadata drift as fake authorization or model-interface failure. If plugin capability reports `missingShared` or `changedShared` while unauthorized app tools remain zero, refresh Anti-Codex local feature snapshots and rerun the verifier.
- Diagnostics payload size can push local `/anticodex/diagnostics` above three seconds even when the gateway is healthy. Keep diagnostics request waits bounded by the verifier-wide budget, but allow a higher per-request cap than tiny health probes before declaring a timeout.
- When proving Anti-Codex can actually work, desktop UI chat must be a release-grade real-traffic gate, not an implied result of `/v1/responses` or `/v1/chat/completions` passing. A stuck session lock can leave the UI showing "processed/thinking" while backend probes still answer.
- Do not run multiple real-traffic tool verifiers in parallel when they read the latest model-route audit. Parallel native/MCP checks can make one verifier read another request's latest route and fail with a route mismatch even though the tool call executed correctly. Run tool verifiers sequentially for release evidence.
- A stale reconnect string inside old conversation history is not the same as an active reconnect state. UI state checks should scope reconnect detection to active shell/status surfaces, while preserving historical transcript text only as context.
- For the current usability bar, accept only evidence that exercises the desktop and execution path: `ui-chat-smoke` for real UI reply, `real-mini-tool` for file-write plus local tool execution, `real-work` for file-write plus real MCP call, and account/proxy readiness for model-route health.
- A `source_codex_changed` sync-readiness failure is a release blocker, not proof that the current chat path is unusable. It means the installed Codex host changed and Anti-Codex needs explicit-approval host sync before 100% release acceptance.
- Manager bridge helper processes can outlive a previous base URL and pollute sync-readiness support evidence. If sync-readiness reports stale Manager bridge processes, stop only those helper processes and rerun the read-only check before diagnosing host drift.
- Do not claim 100% until host sync, installed no-traffic overall, fresh post-verification, and the promised one-or-two real small-project usability pass all pass after the same current host/runtime state. A green single chat or tool check proves immediate usability, not release completion.
- Full `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1` can partially succeed before failing to rename the active local `skills` directory with EPERM. If connector/app-tool parity was the actual drift, rerun plugin capability and then `refresh-anticodex-local-features.ps1 -VerifyOnly` to write a clean read-only evidence report; investigate the skills lock separately instead of treating connector parity as still broken.
- Manager bridge base URL checks must distinguish stale remote addresses from valid local machine addresses. A bridge launched with a same-machine LAN address such as `http://192.168.x.x:8484` can be the live Anti-Codex gateway and should not fail sync-readiness as stale; compare against loopback plus local IPv4 addresses, not only `127.0.0.1`.
- A real small-project verifier must force the first tool call. Leaving `tool_choice` implicit can make the model spend the whole request budget drafting code and leave no files or evidence.
- If a verifier receives a tool call but fails before sending `function_call_output`, the Antigravity session lock can remain active even with zero active upstream requests. Failure paths for real tool/project verifiers should query diagnostics and use the Manager `resetAntigravitySessionLock` action only when `activeAntigravityRequests.active == 0`.
- PowerShell `Start-Process -PassThru` exit code can read as unset until the process object is refreshed. After `WaitForExit`, call `Refresh()` before treating `ExitCode` as authoritative; otherwise a passing `node --test` run can be reported as non-zero.
- Do not run installed overall fast/full UI checks in parallel. They share Manager/browser lock state; parallel runs can create a false `anticodex-manager-button.lock` wait failure. Run overall gates serially, and increase `-SubcheckTimeoutSec` for full UI-state when the dynamic Manager probe is slow.
- Installed overall no-traffic should not duplicate the host-sync release blocker as a runtime-usability failure. If `sync-readiness` is otherwise well-formed and only reports `source_codex_changed`, classify it as `host_sync_required_not_runtime_unusable`, keep it release-blocking, and let the separate sync-readiness gate require explicit host-sync approval.
- After changing source verifier scripts, rerun `sync-anticodex-runtime.ps1 -NoRestart` before reading release gates. Otherwise `installedRuntimeSync` can correctly fail on a source/runtime fingerprint mismatch even though the live gateway is healthy.
- For user-facing usability, keep a simple desktop UI web-search task as a P0 gate. A backend responses/chat probe is not enough: the gate must send a normal Chinese prompt through the Anti-Codex desktop UI, require a timely visible answer, require source URLs, reject missing-tool/blocked detours, and confirm the Antigravity session lock is clear afterward.
- "Reconnecting" in the Anti-Codex desktop can be a local gateway/session-lock symptom, not public network loss. If diagnostics show account ready, model catalog ready, circuit closed, but `antigravitySessionLock.active` or `activeAntigravityRequests.active` remains set, debug the local SSE/tool-continuation path first.
- Antigravity streaming responses must abort when the desktop client disconnects. Without a `res.close` abort hook, a stopped or reconnecting UI can leave a live upstream request, busy marker, or queued session behind and make the next simple chat appear to hang.
- Idle tool-continuation cleanup must be allowed even when a follow-up request is queued. Treating `queue.queued > 0` as "not idle" can make the waiting follow-up prevent stale-lock cleanup and deadlock the UI.
- Simple release-date/news questions such as "什么时候发布" should route through translator-owned `web_search` before exposing broad shell tools. Otherwise the model may inspect local transcripts or runtime files instead of answering the user's web/current-info question.
- Real task verifiers should use model-friendly tool schemas. A nested `files[]` array made the model emit non-JSON parameter content and caused a false "no artifact" failure; using explicit string fields such as `report_md` and `index_html` proved the actual research-to-artifact path.
- UI state verification must not require the dynamic Manager overlay probe when static Manager HTML plus live page context already prove the current injected version, action endpoint/token, and result area. The dynamic probe can stall the CDP evaluation path and make a healthy UI look like it is stuck thinking.
- Reconnect detection must separate active status surfaces from stale transcript text. Old chat history can contain "正在重新连接" from a prior failure; treating that as a current reconnect creates false release blockers after the UI has recovered.
- Nested verifier wrappers need more child-process headroom than the child wrapper's own timeout margin. `ui-idle-stability` must not kill `ui-state` at exactly the same outer deadline that `ui-state.ps1` uses to collect JSON and clean up.
- Desktop "thinking/reconnecting" after a tool call can mean the upstream task ignored abort and kept the shared Antigravity queue slot active. SSE must fail locally on stream idle timeout, and the request queue must release an aborted active slot even if the underlying upstream promise never settles.
- One-off user artifacts are not source edits. For generated reports, HTML pages, and user-specified output directories, Anti-Codex should prefer ordinary shell/PowerShell file writes plus read-back verification instead of retrying large `apply_patch` bodies until the desktop UI hangs.
